Representations of loop groups as factorization module categories
Lin Chen, Yuchen Fu, Dennis Gaitsgory and David Yang

Abstract




Introduction

In this paper we show that the (2-)category of categorical representations of the loop group embeds
fully faithfully into the (2-)category of factorization module categories with respect to the affine Grassmannian.

0.1.
Why might one expect this kind of thing to be true?

0.1.1.

Let
X
be a smooth curve and
x_{0}\in X
a point on it. Let
G
be a reductive group.

A construction going back to A. Beilinson (and probably first fleshed out in
[
Ga0
]
) says that there exists a
family parameterized by
X
, whose fiber at
x\neq x_{0}
is the product
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and
whose fiber at
x_{0}
is
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, where:

•

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x}
is the affine Grassmannian of
G
associated with the formal disc
\mathcal{D}_{x}
around
x
;

•

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is the loop group of
G
associated with the formal punctured disc
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
around
x_{0}
.

One can generalize this construction slightly, and construct a similar family parameterized by
X^{n}
for any

n
.

0.1.2.

In modern language, this construction says that we can regard
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
as a factorization space,
and
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
as a factorization module space at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
(see Sect.
1.2
,
where the relevant definitions are recalled).

Another insight of Beilinson’s, articulated in the early 2000’s, says that this factorization module space is
universal in the following (imprecise) sense:
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
should be isomorphic to the factorization
homology of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
over
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
:

(0.1)

\underset{\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}}{\int}\,{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},

whatever this means.

0.1.3.

The above principle can be made precise in the topological setting:

The affine Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
is homotopy-equivalent to
\Omega(G)
, the loop space of
G
.
Hence, applying Lurie’s non-abelian Poincaré duality, we obtain that the
factorization homology of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
over the circle is homotopy-equivalent to

\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{cont}}(S^{1},G),

which can be regarded as a topological counterpart of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

0.1.4.

The goal of this paper is to give an articulation of this principle in algebraic geometry. We do so by
finding an appropriate linearized statement.

There are (at least) two ways to linearize the above principle:
0
-categorical and
1
-categorical.

The
0
-categorical way is straightforward: it says that the factorization homology
of
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
on
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
(which can be made precise sense of)
maps isomorphically to
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
.

This is a true statement, and it will serve as an ingredient in the proof
of our main theorem, see Sect.
0.2.6
.

0.1.5.

The 1-categorical linearization is richer:

We can consider the category of D-modules on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
as a factorization category, and
we can take its factorization homology over
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
. This is a monoidal category
that maps to
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
(the latter is viewed as a monoidal category under
convolution), and we can ask whether this functor is an equivalence.

The answer is “no” and that is for a simple reason: factorization homology of a category
in the de Rham setting is too loose a construction; we rarely expect it to be equivalent
to something sensible. However, we do expect that it has the “right category” as a quotient.

And indeed, this happens to be this case. Our main result, Theorem
2.1.6
, is
equivalent to saying that the functor

\underset{\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}}{\int}\,\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})

is a quotient.

0.1.6.

Finally, we would like to draw a (loose) analogy between the above statement and
the contractibility result of
[
Ga2
]
:

The latter says that for a complete curve
X
, the pullback functor

\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(X))\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}})

is fully faithful.

Note also that in the global setting, the corresponding statement in topology is that
the factorization homology of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq\Omega^{2}(BG)
over
X
is homotopy equivalent to

\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,BG),

which is in turn homotopy-equivalent to
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(X)
.

0.2.
What is actually done in this paper?

0.2.1.

In the main body of the paper we do not talk about factorization homology of categories over the punctured disc.
Rather, we formulate our main result as follows:

We can view
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
as a factorization module category at
x_{0}

with respect to the factorization category
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
; as such it carries a commuting
action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
“on the right”. This structure allows us to construct a functor

(0.2)

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},

where:

•

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is the 2-category of categorical representations of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
(see Sect.
B.3
,
where the definition is recalled);

•

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
is the 2-category of factorization module categories at
x_{0}
with respect to

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
(see Sect.
1.4
).

Our main result, Theorem
2.1.6
, says that the functor (
0.2
) is fully faithful.

0.2.2.

Concretely, Theorem
2.1.6
says that for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the functor

(0.3)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}),

induced by (
0.2
), is an equivalence.

It is easy to see that when proving this statement, one can assume that the source category, i.e.,
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
, is
a copy of
\operatorname{Vect}
, equipped with the trivial action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
. I.e., we have to show that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the functor

(0.4)

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}),

induced by (
0.2
), is an equivalence.

0.2.3.

The first step in the proof of Theorem
2.1.6
consists of rewriting the right-hand side of (
0.4
) in
terms of
factorization modules
over a
factorization algebra
.

Namely, we show that for any
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
, we have a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}},

where:

•

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
is the dualizing sheaf on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
, viewed as a factorization algebra in the factorization category

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
(see Sect.
1.5.1
);

•

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}
denotes the category of factorization modules at
x_{0}
in
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}

with respect to
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
(see Sect.
1.5.3
).

Thus, we obtain that Theorem
2.1.6
is equivalent to the following statement, which appears as Theorem
3.1.7
in the main
body of the paper:

The functor

(0.5)

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}})\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\left({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\right)_{x_{0}},

induced by (
0.2
), is an equivalence
.

0.2.4.

We now briefly indicate the main steps involved in the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
.

The first step, which is the geometric core of the argument says that when proving (
0.5
),
one can replace
{\mathbf{C}}
by its maximal subcategory
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}})
, on which the action
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is
almost trivial
(see Sect.
4.6
for what this means).

This step is carried out in Sect.
8
and it involves playing with the geometry of the fusion
construction.

0.2.5.

Once we assume that the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is almost trivial, there is no more “de Rham complexity” in the
game, and the idea is to try to mimic the topological argument.

The second step in the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
consists of replacing “almost trivial” by “trivial”. This is done
in Sect.
4.7
, by a categorical Koszul duality type argument.

This reduces the proof that (
0.5
) is an equivalence to the case when
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
. I.e.,
we have to show that the functor

(0.6)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\left(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\right)_{x_{0}}

is an equivalence.

0.2.6.

The third step in the proof of Theorem
0.6
consists of establishing the equivalence (
0.6
).
We show that both sides admit monadic forgetful functors to
\operatorname{Vect}
, and we show that the corresponding monads
are isomorphic. This is done in Sect.
6
.

That said, one could view/prove the assertion that (
0.6
) is an isomorphism differently:

The left-hand side in (
0.6
) identifies with the category of modules over
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
, where the structure
of associative algebra on it is induced by the group structure on
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

The right-hand side in (
0.6
) identifies with the category of factorization modules with respect to
the factorization algebra
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
. Hence, it can be further identified with

\Bigl(\underset{\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}}{\int}\,\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\Bigr){\operatorname{-mod}}.

Hence, the assertion that (
0.6
) is an isomorphism is equivalent to the assertion that the map

\Bigl(\underset{\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}}{\int}\,\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\Bigr){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}){\operatorname{-mod}}

is an isomorphism, which is the linearization statement from Sect.
0.1.4
.

0.3.
Extensions, applications and relation to prior work

0.3.1.

Recall that our main result, Theorem
2.1.6
, has the following form: it says that a certain functor from the 2-category of modules
over a given monoidal category to the 2-category of factorization modules over a factorization category is fully faithful.

As far as we know, this is the
second-of-its-kind
result of this form. The first such result was established in
[
Bogd
]
. There,
the main theorem says that a certain naturally defined functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{G}^{\operatorname{restr}}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}})){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

is fully faithful, where:

•

\operatorname{LS}_{G}^{\operatorname{restr}}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}})
is the stack of local systems with restricted variation on
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
with respect to
G

(defined as in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.4]
).

This result paves a way to questions of spectral decomposition in the
restricted
local geometric Langlands theory,
see
[
Ga5
, Sect. 2.6]
.

0.3.2.

That said, one expects a stronger result to be true. Namely, we expect that (a similarly defined functor)

(0.7)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{G}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}})){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

is fully faithful, where:

•

\operatorname{LS}_{G}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}})
is the stack of de Rham local systems on
\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}
with respect to
G
.

A result of this form would be of crucial importance for the full (i.e., unrestricted) local geometric Langlands theory.

0.3.3.

Note, however, that when
G=T
is a torus, the functor (
0.7
) identifies with the functor
(
0.2
) for the dual torus.

So, thanks to our Theorem
3.1.7
, the fully-faithfulness of
(
0.7
) is known for tori.

0.3.4.

For a non-commutative
G
, one can formulate the following conjecture:

Recall that to a category
{\mathbf{C}}
acted on by the loop group, one can associate its Whittaker model,

\operatorname{Whit}({\mathbf{C}})
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 1.3.3]
. Moreover, this construction works in the factorization setting.

Thus, on the one hand, we can consider

\operatorname{Whit}(G):=\operatorname{Whit}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}))

as a factorization category.

On the other hand, we can consider the monoidal category

\operatorname{bi-Whit}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}):={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Whit}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})).

A construction similar to (
0.2
) gives rise to a functor

(0.8)

\operatorname{bi-Whit}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{Whit}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

We conjecture that the functor (
0.8
) is fully faithful. (Note that when
G
is a torus, the Whittaker operation
is the identity functor, and the functor (
0.8
) is just the functor (
0.2
)).

0.3.5.

Now, the geometric Casselman-Shalika equivalence says that we have an equivalence of factorization categories

\operatorname{Whit}(G)\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\check{G}}),

where
{\check{G}}
is the Langlands dual group of
G
.

And one of the conjectures in local geometric Langlands says that

\operatorname{bi-Whit}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{LS}_{G}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{x_{0}}))

as monoidal categories.

Under this equivalence, the functor (
0.2
) is supposed to correspond to the functor
(
0.8
). This is the basis for believing that (
0.8
) is fully faithful.

0.3.6.

Up until now, we have discussed the idea that (
0.1
) is an equivalence, when we linearize
our algebro-geometric objects by applying the functor
\operatorname{D}(-)
.

One may wonder, however, whether one could expect a similar behavior when we linearize by means of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(-)
instead.

The answer is that an analog of Theorem
2.1.6
will fail in this case. We explain a counterexample in Sect.
10.6
.

That said, this failure (at least, in our example) happens for subtle homological algebra reasons
(it takes place, so to say, at the cohomological
-\infty
). It is not impossible that one could
modify the definitions around the objects involved and make an analog of Theorem
2.1.6
hold.

0.3.7.

We now explain one concrete application of our Theorem
2.1.6
, rather in its incarnation as Theorem
3.1.7
,
to usual representation theory.

Let
\kappa
be a non-negative integral Kac-Moody level. To it we can associate a chiral algebra

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}
.

For example, when
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected,
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}

is the “maximal integrable quotient” of the vacuum chiral algebra
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}
.
When
G
is a torus,
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}
is the lattice chiral algebra.

It is known that
at the level of abelian categories
, the category
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

is equivalent to the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
of
integrable
Kac-Moody modules at level
\kappa
, i.e.,
representations of the central extension

1\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\widehat{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\kappa,x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to 1,

corresponding to
\kappa
, on which the central
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts by the standard character.

In Theorem
10.1.8
, we show that this equivalence continues to hold at the derived level.

It is known that the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
is semi-simple (even at the derived level). So the content of
Theorem
10.1.8
is that there are no higher Exts between irreducible objects of
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
.

0.4.
Structure of the paper

We now explain the structure of the paper section-by-section.

0.4.1.

In Sect.
1
we supply some background in factorization, mostly borrowed from
[
GLC2
, Sects. B and C]
.

0.4.2.

In Sect.
2
we construct the functor (
0.2
), state our main result (Theorem
2.1.6
) and
reduce it to the case when the source category is
\operatorname{Vect}
.

0.4.3.

In Sect.
3
we state Theorem
3.1.7
, which says that (
0.5
) is an equivalence.
We prove that Theorem
3.1.7
is logically equivalent to Theorem
2.1.6
.

The rest of the paper (up until Sect.
10
) is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
.

0.4.4.

In Sect.
4
we discuss the notion of
almost trivial
action of a group (in particular, a loop group)
on a category.

We state Theorem
4.7.3
that says that for the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
we can
assume that the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\mathbf{C}}
is trivial.

We show that Theorem
4.7.3
allows us to reduce Theorem
3.1.7
to the case
when
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
.

Theorem
4.7.3
will be proved in Sects.
7
-
9
.

0.4.5.

In Sect.
5
we prove several technical statements formulated in Sect.
4
.

0.4.6.

In Sect.
6
we prove Theorem
3.1.7
for
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
by a direct calculation, which
amounts to an algebro-geometric incarnation of a particular case of Lurie’s non-abelian
Poincaré duality.

0.4.7.

In Sect.
7
we prove Theorem
4.7.3
for a torus using local geometric class field theory.

0.4.8.

In Sect.
8
we supply a key geometric argument that tackles Theorem
4.7.3

in the non-abelian case.

0.4.9.

In Sect.
9
we finish the proof of Theorem
4.7.3
.

0.4.10.

In Sect.
10
we discuss the application of our Theorem
3.1.7
to integrable Kac-Moody representation.
In addition, we
disprove
a coherent version of Theorem
2.1.6
.

0.4.11.

In Sect.
A
we (re)collect some material pertaining to the theory of D-modules on algebro-geometric
objects of infinite type.

0.4.12.

In Sect.
B
we (re)collect some material pertaining to categorical representations of groups and
in particular, loop groups.

0.4.13.

In Sect.
C
we supply proofs of statements pertaining to factorization categories and modules.

0.5.
Conventions and notation

0.5.1.

Throughout the paper, we will be working over a ground field
k
, assumed algebraically closed and of characteristic
0
.

0.5.2.

We will be working with
k
-linear
higher algebra
. The basic object of study for us is the
\infty
-category
of
k
-linear DG categories, denoted
\operatorname{DGCat}
(see
[
GR1
, Sect. 1]
, where the relevant definitions are discussed
in detail).

The
\infty
-category
\operatorname{DGCat}
carries a symmetric monoidal structure, the Lurie tensor product. Its unit is
the category
\operatorname{Vect}
of chain complexes of
k
-vector spaces.

In particular, all objects of
\operatorname{DGCat}
are automatically enriched over
\operatorname{Vect}
; for
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
and

{\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
, we will denote by
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2})
the corresponding object of
\operatorname{Vect}
.

The category
\operatorname{DGCat}
has an internal Hom, denoted

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}),\quad{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in\operatorname{DGCat}.

0.5.3.

As we will be interested in D-modules
1
1

1

Except Sect.
10.6
.
, we can stay in the world of
classical

(as opposed to
derived
) algebraic geometry. By a prestack we will mean a (presentable) functor

(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\infty\operatorname{-Grpds},

where
\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}
is the category of classical affine schemes over
k
.

All specific classes of algebro-geometric objects (e.g., schemes, ind-schemes, algebraic stacks, etc.) are full
subcategories in the category
\operatorname{PreStk}
of prestacks.

0.5.4.

The theory of D-modules on prestacks locally of finite type is built in
[
GR2
, Chapter 4]
. An extension
to relevant algebro-geometric objects of infinite type is discussed in Sect.
A
.

0.5.5.

The material in this paper that has to do with factorization relies to a large extent on
[
GLC2
]
. We make
a brief review in Sect.
1
, and refer the reader to
loc. cit.
for details.

0.5.6.

The main result of this paper, Theorem
2.1.6
, talks about comparing 2-categories.

In this paper, by a (
k
-linear) 2-category, we will mean an
\infty
-category enriched over
\operatorname{DGCat}
.
In particular, given a 2-category
\mathfrak{C}
, and
\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2}\in\mathfrak{C}
, we will denote by

\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{2})

the corresponding object of
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

0.5.7.

The main source of 2-categories will be of the form

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

where
{\mathbf{A}}
is a monoidal DG category, i.e., an associative algebra object in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

Objects of
{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
are
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories, and for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
,

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})

is the naturally defined category of
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear functors.

0.5.8.

All other conventions and notations follow ones adopted in
[
AGKRRV
]
and
[
GLC2
]
.

0.6.
Acknowledgements

The third author would like to thank Sasha Beilinson for generously sharing his ideas, in particular about the factorization
perspective on local representation theory and (
0.1
).

We would like to thank Gurbir Dhillon
for helping us with an observation that proved crucial for Theorem
10.1.8
(see Remark
10.4.3
).

We would also like to thank Sam Raskin for some very helpful discussions on topics related to this paper:
much of the mathematics that this paper relies on was developed in collaboration with him.

1.
Background and preliminaries

For the readers convenience, in this section, we will (re)collect the definitions of the main
players appearing in the paper. The discussions will mostly repeat
[
GLC2
, Sect. B and C]
.

1.1.
The arc and loop groups

The material here follows
[
GLC2
, Sects. B.3 and B.4]
.

1.1.1.

The Ran space of
X
, denoted
{\operatorname{Ran}}
is a prestack that attaches to a test affine scheme
S
the
set of finite non-empty subsets of
\operatorname{Hom}(S,X)
.

We let
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
be a variant of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, where we consider finite subsets with a distinguished element
corresponding to

S\to\operatorname{pt}\overset{x_{0}}{\to}X.

1.1.2.

For an
S
-point
\underline{x}
of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, let
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}
be the
complete
formal disc around
\underline{x}
,
i.e. the completion of
S\times X
at the union of graphs of the maps comprising
\underline{x}
.
This is ind-affine ind-scheme. We let
\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}
be the colimit of
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}
taken in the category of
affine schemes.

For example, for
\underline{x}=\{x_{0}\}
and
S=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
, if
t
is a local coordinate at
x_{0}
, then

\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}=\operatorname{Spf}(R[\![t]\!]):=\underset{n}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,\operatorname{Spec}(R[t]/t^{n})\text{ and }\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}=\operatorname{Spec}(R[\![t]\!]).

The above union of graphs is naturally a closed subscheme of
\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}
. We denote by

\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}
the open complement. This is also an affine scheme.

In the above example,

\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}=\operatorname{Spec}(R(\!(t)\!)).

1.1.3.

We let
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}
be the group-scheme over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, whose points are pairs
(\underline{x},g)
, where

\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}})
and
g
is a point of
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}},G)
. Note that the latter is the same as

\operatorname{Hom}(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}},G)
, since
G
is affine.

We let
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\operatorname{Ran}}
be the group-scheme over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, whose points are pairs
(\underline{x},g)
, where

\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}})
and
g
is a point of
\operatorname{Hom}(\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}},G)
.

We will denote by
(-)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
and
(-)_{x_{0}}
the base change of the above objects along

{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
\operatorname{pt}\overset{\{x_{0}\}}{\to}{\operatorname{Ran}}
, respectively.

Explicitly, the group of
S
-points of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
is

\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spf}(R[\![t]\!]),G)\simeq\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spec}(R[\![t]\!]),G)=G\left(R[\![t]\!]\right).

The group pf
S
-points of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is

\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spec}(R(\!(t)\!)),G)=G\left(R(\!(t)\!)\right).

1.1.4.

The object of study of this paper is categories equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
. We refer the reader
to Sect.
B.3
, where this notion is reviewed.

1.2.
Factorization spaces

The material here follows
[
GLC2
, Sects. B.1 and B.2]
.

1.2.1.

A factorization space
{\mathcal{T}}
is a prestack
{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
equipped with the datum of isomorphisms:

(1.1)

{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}},\operatorname{union}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\simeq({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}})\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}},

where:

•

({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\subset({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})
is the disjoint locus, i.e., the open subfunctor
consisting of pairs
(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2})\in({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})
such that
\operatorname{Graph}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\cap\operatorname{Graph}_{\underline{x}_{2}}=\emptyset
;

•

\operatorname{union}
is the union map
{\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

The isomorphisms (
1.1
) must be equipped with a homotopy-coherent data of commutativity and associativity.

Remark 1.2.2
.

We distinguish notationally
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
: the latter is a just prestack over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, and the former
taken into account the factorization structure.

1.2.3.

Note that for
(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2})\in({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}
and
\underline{x}=\underline{x}_{1}\cup\underline{x}_{2}
, we have

\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\sqcup\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}_{2}}\text{ and }\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}\simeq\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\sqcup\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}_{2}}.

These isomorphisms endow
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
and
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
with a factorization structure. We denote the
resulting factorization spaces by
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
and
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
, respectively.

1.2.4.

A key geometric player is the factorization space

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}:={\mathfrak{L}}(G)/{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G).

Explicitly, for a point
\underline{x}
of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, the fiber
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\underline{x}}
is the set of pairs
({\mathcal{P}}_{G},\alpha)
, where:

•

{\mathcal{P}}_{G}
is a
G
-bundle on
\mathcal{D}_{\underline{x}}
;

•

\alpha
is a trivialization of
{\mathcal{P}}_{G}|_{\overset{\circ}{\mathcal{D}}_{\underline{x}}}
.

Or equivalently, it is the set of pairs
({\mathcal{P}}_{G}^{\operatorname{glob}},\beta)

•

{\mathcal{P}}_{G}^{\operatorname{glob}}
is a
G
-bundle on
X
;

•

\beta
is a trivialization of
{\mathcal{P}}_{G}^{\operatorname{glob}}|_{X\setminus\underline{x}}
.

1.2.5.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be a factorization space. A factorization module space
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}
at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathcal{T}}
is a prestack

({\mathcal{T}}_{m})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, equipped with the datum of isomorphisms:

(1.2)

({\mathcal{T}}_{m})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{union}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\simeq({\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\times({\mathcal{T}}_{m})_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}})\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}{\times}({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}},

equipped with a homotopy-coherent datum of associativity against (
1.1
).

We will often think of
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}
as a prestack
({\mathcal{T}}_{m})_{x_{0}}
, equipped with an additional datum of extension to a prestack
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, all of whose fibers are specified by (
1.2
).

1.2.6.

For a factorization space
{\mathcal{T}}
, the pullback

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}:={\mathcal{T}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}

has a natural factorization structure against
{\mathcal{T}}
.

We denote the resulting factorization module space by
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
. We refer to it as the

vacuum
factorization module space at
x_{0}
.

1.2.7.

For a factorization space, one can talk about it having a
unital
,
counital
and
corr-unital

structure. See Sect.
C.1
for their definitions.

For example,
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
is unital,
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
is counital, and
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
is corr-unital.

If
{\mathcal{T}}
is factorization space that is
unital
(resp.,
counital
,
corr-unital
) and
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}

is a factorization module space at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathcal{T}}
, one can talk about
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}
being

unital
(resp.,
counital
,
corr-unital
) against the corresponding structure on
{\mathcal{T}}
.

1.3.
Factorization categories

The material here follows
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11]
. The reader is referred to
loc. cit.
for more details.

1.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack (assumed locally of finite type). A crystal of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
on
{\mathcal{Y}}
is an assignment:

•

(S\overset{y}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}})\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}_{S,y}\in\operatorname{D}(S){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, where
S
is an affine scheme of finite type,
and
\operatorname{D}(S)
is viewed as a symmetric monoidal category via the
\overset{!}{\otimes}
operation;

•

(S_{1}\overset{f}{\to}S_{2})\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}_{S_{1},y_{1}}\simeq\operatorname{D}(S_{1})\underset{\operatorname{D}(S_{2})}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}_{S_{2},y_{2}}
,
where
y_{1}=y_{2}\circ f
, and the symmetric monoidal functor
\operatorname{D}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{D}(S_{1})
is
f^{!}
.

•

A homotopy-coherent system of compatibilities for higher order compositions.

1.3.2.

The most basic example of a crystal of categories is
\underline{\operatorname{D}}({\mathcal{Y}})
, whose value on every
S\overset{y}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}

is
\operatorname{D}(S)
.

1.3.3.

We let
\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}})
denote the 2-category of crystals of categories over
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

We have a naturally defined functor

(1.3)

\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},\quad\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto\underset{S\overset{y}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}_{S,y}.

Recall (see
[
Ga3
, Definition 1.3.7]
) that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}
is said to be
1-affine
if the functor (
1.3
)
is an equivalence.

For example
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is 1-affine (see
[
GLC2
, Lemma B.8.15]
).

1.3.4.

For a map
g:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, there is a tautologically defined pullback functor

g^{*}:\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}).

When there is no ambiguity for
g
, for
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, we will sometimes write

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}|_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}:=g^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

We have a naturally defined functor

g^{!}:\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},g^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})).

For
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
,
i=1,2
, we define

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

naturally: for
y_{i}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
,

(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})_{S,(y_{1},y_{2})}:=(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1})_{S,y_{1}}\underset{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\otimes}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})_{S,y_{2}}.

We have a naturally defined functor,

\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1})\otimes\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\to\mathbf{\Gamma}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}),

to be denoted

{\mathbf{c}}_{1},{\mathbf{c}}_{2}\mapsto{\mathbf{c}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{2}.

1.3.5.

A factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
is a crystal of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
, equipped with an equivalence

(1.4)

\operatorname{union}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}

equipped additionally with a homotopy-coherent datum of commutativity and associativity.

Remark 1.3.6
.

Even though
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{{\operatorname{dR}}}
is 1-affine, we distinguish notationally between
{\mathbf{A}}
,
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
and

{\mathbf{A}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}:=\mathbf{\Gamma}({\operatorname{Ran}},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}).

That said, the data of (
1.4
), can be equivalently spelled out in terms of
{\mathbf{A}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

1.3.7.

The most basic example of a factorization category, denoted
\operatorname{Vect}
, is when the corresponding crystal
of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}
is
\underline{\operatorname{D}}({\operatorname{Ran}})
itself.

1.3.8.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be a factorization space. Assume that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
is locally of finite type. Then the crystal of categories

(\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}}))\mapsto\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}})

has a natural factorization structure.

We denote the resulting factorization category by
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}})
.

1.3.9.

A prime example of this is when
{\mathcal{T}}={\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
. This way, we obtain a factorization category
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
,
which is the second main player in this paper.

Remark 1.3.10
.

Let us weaken the hypothesis that
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
is locally of finite type. Instead, let us require that for every
\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}})

(with
S
an affine scheme of finite type), the prestack
{\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}}
is an
ind-placid ind-scheme
, see Sect.
A.4.7

for what this means.

In this case, the assignments

(\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}}))\mapsto\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}})\text{ and }(\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}}))\mapsto\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}})

are crystals of categories equipped with natural factorization structures.

We denote the resulting factorization categories by

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{T}})\text{ and }\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{T}}),

respectively.

1.3.11.

Given a factorization category, one can talk about a
unital
structure on it. We refer the reader to
[
GLC2
, Sect. C.11]
.
Some of this material will be reviewed in Sect.
C
of this paper.

1.4.
Factorization
module
categories

The material here follows
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.12]
. The reader is referred to
loc. cit.
for more details.

1.4.1.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a factorization category. A factorization module category
{\mathbf{C}}
at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
is a crystal
of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
equipped with an equivalence

(1.5)

\operatorname{union}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}}},

equipped with a homotopy-coherent data of associativity against (
1.4
).

The totality of factorization module categories at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
naturally forms a 2-category, to be denoted

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

1.4.2.

We have a tautological forgetful functor

(1.6)

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathbf{A}}:{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{DGCat},\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}_{x_{0}}.

Note, however, that the functor (
1.6
) is
not
conservative. Rather, it induces conservative functors between
\operatorname{Funct}
categories. As a formal corollary, we obtain the following:

Lemma 1.4.3
.

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
be a 1-morphism in
{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
that admits an adjoint (on either side).
Then if

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathbf{A}}(F):{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathbf{A}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\to{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathbf{A}}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})

is an equivalence, then so is
F
.

1.4.4.

The category
{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
has colimits that commute with the forgeftul functors

(1.7)

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{D}(S){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

for any
\underline{x}:S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
.

Assume now that
{\mathbf{A}}
is such that for any
\underline{x}:S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
, the category
{\mathbf{A}}_{S,\underline{x}}
is dualizable.
Then
{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
also contains all limits that commute with the forgetful functors (
1.7
).

1.4.5.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be as in Sect.
1.3.8
, and let
{\mathcal{T}}_{m}
be a factorization module space at
x_{0}
with respect to

{\mathcal{T}}
, also assumed locally of finite type.

Then the assignment

(\underline{x}\in\operatorname{Hom}(S,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}))\mapsto\operatorname{D}(({\mathcal{T}}_{m})_{\underline{x}})

is a crystal of categories that carries a natural factorization structure against
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}})
.

We denote the resulting object of
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
by

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}}_{m})\in\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

1.4.6.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a factorization category. The pullback of
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
along
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
has a natural factorization
structure against
{\mathbf{A}}
.

We denote the resulting object by

{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\in{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

and refer to it as the
vacuum factorization module category
at
x_{0}
.

Note that in the example of Sect.
1.3.8
,
we have

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}),

where
{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
is as in Sect.
1.2.6
.

1.4.7.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
be a homomorphism of factorization categories. For
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

one attaches an object

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in{\mathbf{A}}_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

which is the universal object such that there is a morphism
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
compatible with
\Phi
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.12.11]
.

This construction will be reviewed in Sect.
C
of the present paper.

1.4.8.

Given a unital factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
, one can talk about
unital factorization module categories
at
x_{0}
with respect to

{\mathbf{A}}
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. C.14]
. We denote the resulting 2-category by

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

When we want to ignore the unital structure on
{\mathbf{A}}
, we will denote the resulting 2-category of
non-unital
factorization
module categories by

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact-n.u.}}_{x_{0}}.

There exists an obvious forgetful functor

(1.8)

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact-n.u.}}_{x_{0}}.

Note, however, that (
1.8
) is
not
fully faithful. Yet, it is 1-fully faithful, i.e., it is fully
faithful on the
\operatorname{Funct}
-categories.

1.5.
Factorization algebras and modules

1.5.1.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a factorization category. A factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
in
{\mathbf{A}}
is an object
{\mathcal{A}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
, equipped
with an identification between

\operatorname{union}^{!}({\mathcal{A}})\in\mathbf{\Gamma}\left(({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}},\operatorname{union}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\right)\text{ and }{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbf{\Gamma}\left(({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}},{\mathbf{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{A}}\right)

with respect to the equivalence (
1.4
), and further equipped with a homotopy-coherent
system of compatibilities.

Taking
{\mathbf{A}}=\operatorname{Vect}
, we recover the usual notion of factorization algebra.

1.5.2.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
be a homomorphism of factorization categories. Then
\Phi

sends factorization algebras in
{\mathbf{A}}_{1}
to factorization algebras in
{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
.

1.5.3.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a factorization module category at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
. Given a factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
in

{\mathbf{A}}
, we define a factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module at
x_{0}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
to be an object
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathbf{C}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
, equipped
with an identification

\operatorname{union}^{!}({\mathcal{M}})\in\mathbf{\Gamma}\left(({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}},\operatorname{union}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\right)\text{ and }{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathbf{\Gamma}\left(({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})_{\operatorname{disj}},{\mathbf{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{A}}\right)

with respect to the equivalence (
1.5
), and further equipped with a homotopy-coherent
system of compatibilities.

1.5.4.

We let

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}

denote category of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules at
x_{0}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

We have a naturally defined
conservative
forgetful functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{x_{0}}.

1.5.5.

In the particular case when
{\mathbf{C}}={\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
, we will use a short-hand notation

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}:={\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})_{x_{0}}.

In the particular case when
{\mathbf{A}}=\operatorname{Vect}
, and thus
{\mathcal{A}}
is a usual factorization algebra we thus recover the usual category

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules at
x_{0}
.

1.5.6.

Consider the pullback of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
with respect to
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
. It has a natural factorization module structure
against
{\mathcal{A}}
. We denote the resulting object by

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

We refer to it as the
vacuum
factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module at
x_{0}
.

1.5.7.

Let
\phi:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
be a homomorphism between factorization algebras in
{\mathbf{A}}
. Then to
\phi
one attaches a restriction
functor

\operatorname{Res}_{\phi}:{\mathcal{A}}_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}},

see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.9.25]
.

This construction will be reviewed also in Sect.
C
of the present paper.

1.5.8.

Let now
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
be a homomorphism between factorization categories. Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
be a factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}_{1}
,
and set
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}:=\Phi({\mathcal{A}}_{1})
, which we view as a factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
(see Sect.
1.5.2
above).

Let
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
be an object of
{\mathbf{A}}_{2}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
and set

{\mathbf{C}}_{1}:=\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{C}}_{2}),

see Sect.
1.4.7
.

In this case, we have a canonical equivalence

(1.9)

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathcal{A}}_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})_{x_{0}},

see
[
GLC2
, Lemma B.12.14]
, to be reviewed in Sect.
C
.

1.5.9.

Assume that
{\mathbf{A}}
is unital. In this case, one can talk about a factorization algebra being unital,
see
[
GLC2
, Sect. 7]
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a unital factorization module category at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
, and let
{\mathcal{A}}

be a unital factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}
. In this case, the category of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules
at
x_{0}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
contains a full subcategory of
unital
factorization modules, see
[
GLC2
, C.11.19]
,
to be reviewed in Sect.
C
of the present paper.

In this case, we denote this subcategory by
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}
, and the category of
not necessarily unital
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules by
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact-n.u.}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}
.

2.
Statement of the result

In this section we state the main result of this paper, Theorem
2.1.6
, and then reformulate it
in terms of the computation of a category of factorization modules with respect to
a certain factorization algebra.

2.1.
From loop group modules to factorization modules over
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}

In this subsection we will construct a functor

(2.1)

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}

with the basic property that it preserves the forgetful functors from both sides to
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

2.1.1.

Recall that
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
is a unital factorization space and
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
is a unital factorization category, see Sect.
1.3.9
and Sect.
C.1.16
.

The functor (
2.1
) will be constructed by exhibiting a suitable bimodule
category, to be denoted
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
, which is an object of

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
, and as such carries an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

Moreover, the underlying DG category of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
(i.e., its
fiber over
x_{0}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
) will
identify with
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
itself, with the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action given by right translations.

2.1.2.

The category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
will be defined as the category of D-modules
on a certain prestack, to be denoted
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
, which is a factorization module space

at
x_{0}
with respect to the factorization algebra space
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
(see Sect.
1.2.5
), and as such equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

Moreover, the fiber of

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
at
x_{0}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
will identify with
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
itself, with the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action given by right translations.

2.1.3.

The space
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
is constructed as follows:

For an affine test scheme
S
and a given
S
-point
\underline{x}
of
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, the fiber

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}}

of
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
over it consists of the data of

({\mathcal{P}}_{G},\alpha,\epsilon),

where:

•

{\mathcal{P}}_{G}
is a
G
-bundle on
S\times X
;

•

\alpha
is a trivialization of
{\mathcal{P}}_{G}
over
S\times X-\operatorname{Graph}_{\underline{x}}
;

•

\epsilon
is a trivialization of
{\mathcal{P}}_{G}
over the formal completion of
S\times X
along
S\times x_{0}
.

The (unital) factorization module space structure on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
is contructed using Beauville-Laszlo theorem.

The action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
is given by the standard regluing procedure. It is easy to see this action preserves the factorization module space structure in above.

2.1.4.

As in Sect.
A.5
, one can show
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}}
is ind-placid, and there is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{D}^{!}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})\simeq\operatorname{D}_{*}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})
. We will simply write
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})
for these categories.

Now the assignment

\underline{x}\mapsto\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})

is a crystal of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
that carries a natural (unital) factorization structure against
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
. See Sect.
1.4.5
. We define

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}

to be this object
2
2

2

Using Proposition
C.10.20
, one can show

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\simeq\mathbf{Res}_{p^{!}}\big(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))}\big),

where
p^{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
is the unital factorization functor given by
!
-pullbacks and
\mathbf{Res}_{p^{!}}
is the restriction functor along it (see Sect.
1.4.7
).

.

By construction,
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
acts on
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
. We define the functor in (
2.1
) by

(2.2)

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\underset{\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}.

By Corollary
B.4.12
and Sect.
1.4.4
, the functor (
2.2
) commutes with limits and colimits.

2.1.5.

We can now state the main result of this paper:

Theorem 2.1.6
.

The functor (
2.1
) is fully faithful.

2.1.7.

Before we proceed any further, let us remark that the functor (
2.1
) is
not

an equivalence. Indeed, it is easy to exhibit an object in
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
that
which is not in the essential image of (
2.1
).

Namely, restriction to the unit section defines a factorization functor

\iota^{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}.

Hence, we obtain a restriction functor

\mathbf{Res}_{\iota^{!}}:\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

(see Sect.
1.4.7
).

Nonzero objects in the essential image of this functor do not lie in the essential image of the functor
(
2.1
) unless
G
is trivial
3
3

3

Sketch of proof: suppose
\mathbf{Res}_{\iota^{!}}({\mathbf{C}})
is contained in the essential image of (
2.1
). By Theorem
3.1.7
below, we have

{\mathbf{C}}_{x_{0}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathbf{Res}_{\iota^{!}}({\mathbf{C}}))_{x_{0}}\simeq\iota^{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\simeq k{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}_{x_{0}},

which is impossible unless
G
is trivial or
{\mathbf{C}}\simeq 0
.
.

Remark 2.1.8
.

For the trival group, it is easy to see the corresponding functor

(2.3)

\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

is fully faithful. However, it is
not
an equivalence. To see this, consider the Ran space
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}
for the punctured curve
X-x_{0}
. Write
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}:={\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}\sqcup\{\emptyset\}
, and consider the map

j:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},\;\underline{y}\mapsto\underline{y}\sqcup\{x_{0}\}.

One can show
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}
, viewed as a prestack over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, has a natural factorization structure against the factorization space
{\operatorname{Ran}}
(see Sect.
1.2.5
). By Sect.
1.4.5
, we obtain a factorization module category with respect to
\operatorname{Vect}
, such that
\mathbf{\Gamma}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},-)
sends it to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ})
. Moreover, using the
unital
Ran spaces (see Sect.
C.1.3
), we can upgrade it to a
unital
factorization module category with respect to
\operatorname{Vect}
. In other words, we obtain a bizarre object, denote by

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{disj}}\in\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

which is not isomorphic to the vaccum factorization module category
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
, but has the same fiber at
x_{0}
(i.e.
\operatorname{Vect}
). It is clear this obect is not in the essential image of (
2.3
).

Remark 2.1.9
.

At the moment we do not know how to characterize (even conjecturally) the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

equal to the essential image of (
2.1
).

2.2.
Functoriality

In this subsection we establish a functoriality property of the construction in Sect.
2.1
with respect to homomorphisms
of reductive groups.

2.2.1.

Let
\phi:G^{\prime}\to G
be a homomorphism between connected reductive groups. By a slight abuse of notation we will
denote by the same symbol
\phi
the resulting homomorphism

{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}.

Let

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}

between (unital) factorization spaces.

Direct image with respect to
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}
has a natural structure of (unital) factorization functor

({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi})_{*}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}})\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}).

2.2.2.

Note that we also have a morphism between (unital) factorization module spaces

(2.4)

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G^{\prime},{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}},

compatible with
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}
and the right actions of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}
and
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, respectively.

In particular, (
2.4
) induces a morphism

(2.5)

\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}}{\underline{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G^{\prime},{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}},

compatible with the actions of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, where
\underset{H}{\underline{(-)}}
means “divide by the diagonal action of
H
.”

Moreover, the diagram

\begin{CD}\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}}{\underline{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G^{\prime},{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}@>{}>{}>{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime},{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}@>{}>{}>{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\end{CD}

in Cartesian.

In particular, we obtain that the morphism (
2.5
) is ind-proper.

2.2.3.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{Res}_{\phi}
the restriction functor

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

corresponding to the factorization functor
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi})_{*}
, see Sect.
1.4.7
.

From Sect.
2.2.2
, for any
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-module category
{\mathbf{C}}
and the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}
-module category
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
given by restriction along
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, we obtain a naturally defined functor

(2.6)

({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}})}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},

compatible with
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}
.

In particular, we obtain a 1-morphism

(2.7)

({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}})}\to\mathbf{Res}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}).

in
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
.

We claim:

Lemma 2.2.4
.

The 1-morphism (
2.7
)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
C.10.20
.

∎

2.3.
The first reduction step

From now on, until Sect.
10
, we will be occupied with the proof of Theorem
2.1.6
. In this subsection we perform
the first reduction case: we show that we can assume that the source object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is
\operatorname{Vect}
,
equipped with the trivial action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

2.3.1.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a category acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
. Let

(2.8)

{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

denote its image under the functor (
2.2
).

The assertion of Theorem
2.1.6
is that for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
as above, the functor

(2.9)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})

is an equivalence.

In this subsection we will perform the first reduction step in the proof of Theorem
2.1.6
. Namely,
we will show that we can assume that
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}=\operatorname{Vect}
, equipped with the trivial action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

2.3.2.

First, since the functor (
2.2
) is compatible with
colimits
, both sides
in (
2.9
) send colimits in
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
to limits in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

Hence, it is enough to show that (
2.9
) is an isomorphism on objects of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

that generate this category under colimits.

2.3.3.

Note that a collection of generating objects for
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
provided by

(2.10)

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{0},

where
{\mathbf{C}}_{0}
is a plain DG category (i.e., the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on (
2.10
) comes from the action
by left translations on the first factor).

This reduces the assertion that (
2.9
) is an equivalence to the particular case when
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
is of the form
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{0}
.

2.3.4.

It is easy to see that for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}:={\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{0}
, where the action comes from the first factor, we have

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbf{C}}_{0},\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}))

and

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbf{C}}_{0},\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}{}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})).

Hence, if (
2.9
) is an equivalence for
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}
, then it is an equivalence for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
.

2.3.5.

Thus we obtain a further reduction of the assertion that (
2.9
) is an equivalence to the case when
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}=\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})
.

2.3.6.

Note that both sides in (
2.1
) have a natural symmetric monoidal structure:

In the left-hand side, if
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
are DG categories equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, then the tensor product

{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
acquires a
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action via the diagonal action map
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.
Furthermore, an object in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is dualizable if and only if the underlying DG category is dualizable.

In the right-hand side, if
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
are factorization module categories at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
,
then
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
(i.e., the tensor product of the corresponding crystals of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
) is
naturally a factorization module category at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
. We produce the
sought-for factorization module category at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
by applying the restriction functor
(see Sect.
1.4.7
) along the (unital) factorization functor

(\Delta_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{*}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\times{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\simeq\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\otimes\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}).

The following assertion follows from Lemma
2.2.4
:

Lemma 2.3.7
.

The functor (
2.1
) has a canonical symmetric monoidal structure.

Thus, we obtain that if
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is dualizable, then the functor (
2.9
) is an equivalence
if and only if it is an equivalence for
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
replaced by
\operatorname{Vect}
and
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
replaced by
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
.

2.3.8.

Since
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
is dualizable as a DG category (and, hence, as an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
), we obtain that in order
to prove that (
2.9
) is an equivalence, it is enough to do so in the case when
{\mathbf{C}}^{1}=\operatorname{Vect}
.

In other words, it suffices to show that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the functor

(2.11)

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},{\mathbf{C}})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})

is an equivalence.

3.
A reformulation: factorization modules for the dualizing sheaf

In the previous section we reduced the assertion of Theorem
2.1.6
to its particular case, when the source
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-module
is
\operatorname{Vect}
.

From now on we will focus on this particular case and we will formulate a statement, Theorem
3.1.7
, which talks about calculating
the category of factorization modules for a particular factorization algebra. We will show that Theorem
3.1.7
is equivalent to
the above particular case of Theorem
2.1.6
.

3.1.
Factorization modules for the dualizing sheaf

In this subsection we state Theorem
3.1.7
. In a sense, this theorem on its own is no less interesting than Theorem
2.1.6
: it gives a
recipe of how to calculate
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-invariants using factorization algebras.

3.1.1.

Denote by
\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
the projection

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}.

It has a natural structure of map between factorization spaces, which we denote by

\pi:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\to\operatorname{pt}.

We consider

(3.1)

\pi^{!}:\operatorname{Vect}\simeq\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{pt})\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})

as a
lax-unital
factorization functor between unital factorization categories (see Sect.
C.5.1
).

The image of the unit factorization algebra
k\in\operatorname{Vect}
under
\pi^{!}
is a
unital
factorization
algebra, denoted
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
in
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
. The corresponding object

(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{\operatorname{Ran}}\in(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}))_{\operatorname{Ran}}:=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}})

is
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}}
, equipped with its natural factorization structure.

3.1.2.

Denote by
\pi^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
the projection
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
.
We can regard it as a map between factorization module spaces for
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
and
\operatorname{pt}
, respectively, compatible with
\pi
.
Denote by

(\pi^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}})^{!}:\operatorname{Vect}\simeq\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{pt})\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}

the resulting (lax-unital) functor between the factorization module categories with respect to
\operatorname{Vect}\simeq\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{pt})
and
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
, respectively,
compatible with
\pi^{!}
(see Sect.
C.7.1
).

According to
C.7.14
, the functor
(\pi^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}})^{!}
induces a functor

(3.2)

\operatorname{Vect}\simeq k{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect})_{x_{0}}\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},

where

(3.3)

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},

is the category of (unital) factorization modules at
x_{0}
with respect to

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}))

in the (unital) factorization module category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
at
x_{0}
with respect to

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
, see Sect.
1.5.4
and Sect.
1.5.9
.

3.1.3.

The functor (
3.2
) sends the generator
{\mathsf{e}}\in\operatorname{Vect}
to an object

(3.4)

(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}\in\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

in (
3.3
). Explicitly, this object is given by the dualizing sheaf

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}),

equipped with its natural factorization structure with respect to
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
.

3.1.4.

Note that the object (
3.4
) is naturally
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-equivariant,
with respect to the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on (
3.3
) induced from its action on
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}

by
right translations
(see Sect.
2.1.3
).

Since

\left(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\right)^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},

the object (
3.4
) corresponds to an object

(3.5)

k^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}\in\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.

3.1.5.

By functoriality, the object (
3.5
) gives rise to a functor

(3.6)

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},{\mathbf{C}})\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

for
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
.

3.1.6.

Over the course Sects.
4
-
9
we will prove:

Theorem 3.1.7
.

The functor (
3.6
) is an equivalence.

A particular case of Theorem
3.1.7
is:

Corollary 3.1.8
.

The functor (
3.2
) is an equivalence.

3.1.9.

In Sect.
3.2
below we will show that the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
is logically equivalent to the statement that
the functor (
2.11
) is an equivalence. As we have concluded in Sect.
2.3.8
, the
latter is equivalent to the statement of Theorem
2.1.6
.

3.2.
The implication Theorem
3.1.7

\Rightarrow
Theorem
2.1.6

In this subsection we will show that Theorem
3.1.7
implies (the particular case of
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}=\operatorname{Vect}
of)
Theorem
2.1.6
.

The basic tool here is the adjunction of Proposition
C.10.8
.

3.2.1.

Note that since the map
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
is proper, the functor
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}^{!}
admits a
left adjoint, given by
(\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}})_{!}
, compatible with the factorization structure.

We will denote by
\pi_{!}
the resulting (unital) factorization functor
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{pt})=\operatorname{Vect}
.
The functors
(\pi_{!},\pi^{!})
form an adjoint pair as factorization functors, where
\pi^{!}
is
lax-unital
.

Denote by
\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}
the resulting restriction operation on factorization categories, see Sect.
1.4.7
.

3.2.2.

Recall that for a factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
, we denote by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
the tautological
(i.e., vacuum) factorization module at
x_{0}
with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
, see Sect.
1.4.6
.

In particular, for
{\mathbf{A}}=\operatorname{Vect}
, we can consider the (unital) factorization module category
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
at
x_{0}
,
which under the embedding

\operatorname{DGCat}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

corresponds to
\operatorname{Vect}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
.

We claim:

Proposition 3.2.3
.

We have a canonical identification

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\simeq\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}).

The proof will be given in Sect.
3.3
.

3.2.4.

Recall now that for the
lax-unital
factorization functor
\pi^{!}
there is a
unital
restriction operation

\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

see Sect.
C.10.6
.

Moreover, we have the following basic facts:

For

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
, there is a canonical identification:

(3.7)

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}),\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}},\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})),

see Proposition
C.10.8
.

For
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
, we have

(3.8)

\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\simeq\pi^{!}(\operatorname{unit}_{\operatorname{Vect}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}},

see Proposition
C.10.10
.

3.2.5.

Combining Proposition
3.2.3
with (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), we obtain an equivalence

(3.9)

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}(\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}),\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})\simeq\\
