\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}},\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}))\simeq\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}\simeq\pi^{!}(\operatorname{unit}_{\operatorname{Vect}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}=\\
=\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}})_{x_{0}}.

3.2.6.

Let now
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
. Unwinding the constructions, we obtain:

Lemma 3.2.7
.

The functor

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:ff from Vect}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})\simeq\\
\overset{\text{\eqref{e:comparison}}}{\simeq}\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

identifies canonically with the functor (
3.6
).

3.2.8.

From Lemma
3.6
, we obtain that the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
is equivalent to the statement that
the functor (
2.11
) is an equivalence, which in turn is equivalent to the statement of Theorem
2.1.6
.

3.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.2.3

3.3.1.

By construction, we can identify
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
with

(3.10)

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}},

where we take invariants with respect to the (right) action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
, viewed as a factorization module category
at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
.

We will now rewrite (
3.10
) slightly differently.

3.3.2.

Consider the vacuum factorization module space over
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
at
x_{0}
; denote it

({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
, see Sect.
1.2.6
. The resulting factorization module
category identifies with
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
.

The
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
gives rise to an action of the
Hecke groupoid
\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}
at
x_{0}
on
({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
.

Tautologically, we can rewrite (
3.10
) as

(3.11)

(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}}.

Since
\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}
is proper, we can rewrite (
3.11
)
also as

(3.12)

(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})_{\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}}.

3.3.3.

The functor of !-pushforward along

\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}:({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}

gives rise to a functor

(\pi_{x_{0}})_{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}

as factorization module categories with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}})
and
\operatorname{Vect}
,
compatible with the factorization functor
\pi_{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}})\to\operatorname{Vect}
.

Moreover, the functor
(\pi_{x_{0}})_{!}
canonically factors via a functor

(3.13)

(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})_{\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}.

i.e., a functor

(3.14)

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}},

as factorization module categories with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}})
and
\operatorname{Vect}
,
compatible with the factorization functor
\pi_{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}})\to\operatorname{Vect}
.

By the definition of the restriction operation
\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}
, the functor (
3.14
)
gives rise to a (unital) functor

(3.15)

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})

as factorization module categories over
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
.

The functor (
3.15
) is the sought-for functor in Proposition
3.2.3
.

3.3.4.

We will now show that (
3.15
) is an equivalence. In order to do so, we will apply
Proposition
C.10.20
.

Condition (i) in this lemma is satisfied because the morphism
\pi_{\operatorname{Ran}}
is proper. Condition (iii)
is satisfied, since at the level of fibers at
x_{0}
, the functor (
3.15
) induces an identity
endofunctor of
\operatorname{Vect}
.

Hence, it remains to show that the functor (
3.15
) admits a right adjoint, viewed
as a functor between sheaves of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
.

3.3.5.

Pullback along
\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
is a functor

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{D}(({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}),

which naturally factors via a functor

(3.16)

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to(\operatorname{D}(({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}))^{\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}}.

Interpreting
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
as (
3.11
), the functor
(
3.16
) provides a right adjoint to (
3.15
).

∎[Proposition
3.2.3
]

Remark 3.3.6
.

Unwinding the construction, one can describe the functor right adjoint (which is also the inverse) of (
3.15
)
as follows:

By (
3.7
), a datum of a functor

(3.17)

\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})\to\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}

is equivalent to that of a functor

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\pi^{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}),

while the latter is equivalent to that of a functor

(3.18)

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}

of factorization module categories over
\operatorname{Vect}
and
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
, respectively, compatible with

\pi^{!}
.

The functor (
3.18
) is given by the natural factorization of

\pi_{x_{0}}^{!}:\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{D}(({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})

as

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\to(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}.

3.4.
Example: the case of
\operatorname{Vect}

In this section we run a plausibility check for Theorem
3.1.7
when
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
and the group
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected.
We calculate explicitly both sides and show that they are abstractly isomorphic.

3.4.1.

Let us apply Theorem
3.1.7
to
{\mathbf{C}}:=\operatorname{Vect}
, viewed as an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, equipped with the trivial
action. We obtain:

Corollary 3.4.2
.

The functor

(3.19)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

of (
3.6
)
is an equivalence.

3.4.3.

In Sect.
4.7.7
we will show that the assertion of Corollary
3.4.2
is equivalent to a key
calculation involved in the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
.

In the rest of this subsection, we will explain that the existence of
an
equivalence

(3.20)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

is a priori known, at least when
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected.

3.4.4.

Recall that according to Proposition
3.2.3
, we have

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\simeq\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}).

Combining with (
1.9
), we obtain

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\simeq\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})_{x_{0}}=:\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

where in the right-hand side,
\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})
is viewed as a plain unital factorization algebra, and
{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
refers to the plain
category of unital factorization modules at
x_{0}
.

3.4.5.

Assume now that
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected. Let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}
be the Lie algebra
4
4

4

The Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}

is actually abelian. Indeed,
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BG)
, which is isomorphic to a polynomial algebra.
that controls the
rational homotopy type of the classifying stack
BG
of
G
, which is characterized by a canonical isomorphism between cocommutative coalgebras

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG})\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(BG),

where the first
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(-)
denoted the homological Chevalley complex of a Lie algebra.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}:={\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes k_{X}
be the corresponding constant Lie
⋆
-algebra and
U^{\operatorname{ch}}({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X})
be its chiral universal enveloping algebra. We have the following result:

Lemma 3.4.6
.

Let

\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{\operatorname{ch}}:=\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})|_{X}[-1]

be the unital chiral algebra corresponding to the unital factorization algebra
\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})
(see
[
GLC2
, Sect. D.1]
). We have a canonical isomorphism

\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{\operatorname{ch}}\simeq U^{\operatorname{ch}}({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}).

Sketch.

By
[
Ga1
, Theorem 15.3.3]
, the
augmented cocommutative factorization algebra
5
5

5

A unital cocommutative factorization algebra is a unital factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
whose structural isomorphism

\operatorname{union}^{!}({\mathcal{A}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}({\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{A}})|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}

is extended to a not necessarily invertible map
\operatorname{union}^{!}({\mathcal{A}})\to{\mathcal{A}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{A}}
(and is equipped with a homotopy-coherent data of commutativity and associativity). Being augmented means it is equipped with a homomorphism
{\mathcal{A}}\to\operatorname{unit}_{\operatorname{Vect}}
compatible with the cocommutative factorization structures. Informally speaking, this means the cocommutative coalgebra structure on the cochain complex
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
is naturally compatible with its factorization structure induced from the factorization space
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
.

{\mathcal{A}}:=\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})
and the
augmented commutative factorization algebra
6
6

6

A unital commutative factorization algebra is a unital factorization algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
whose structural isomorphism is extended to a not necessarily invertible map
{\mathcal{B}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{B}}\to\operatorname{union}^{!}({\mathcal{B}})
(and is equipped with a homotopy-coherent data). By
[
BD1
, Sect. 3.4.20-3.4.22]
, knowing a unital commutative factorization algebra is equivalent to knowing a unital commutative algebra in the symmetric monoidal category
\operatorname{D}(X)
. Via this correspondence,
{\mathcal{B}}
is given by
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BG)\otimes\omega_{X}
.

{\mathcal{B}}:=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BG)
are “Verdier dual” to each other after removing the augmented units. Here the notion of Verdier duality is developed in
[
Ga1
]
and is subtler than the usual one because the D-modules
{\mathcal{A}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{\operatorname{Ran}}
are not compact. Nevertheless,
[
Ho
, Theorem 1.5.9]
shows that after passing to the
chiral Koszul duals
(see
[
FG
]
for what this means), this Verdier duality becomes the usual one. In other words,

{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}\simeq\mathbb{D}^{\operatorname{Ver}}_{X}({\mathcal{B}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}),

where
{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}
is the Lie
⋆
-algebra that is Koszul dual to
{\mathcal{A}}
, while
{\mathcal{B}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}
is the Lie
⋆
-coalgebra that is Koszul dual to
{\mathcal{B}}
. Since
{\mathcal{B}}|_{X}
is constant with
!
-fibers equal to
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BG)
, we see
{\mathcal{B}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}
is constant with
!
-fibers equal to the Koszul dual of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BG)
, which is just the Lie coalgebra
({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG})^{*}
. It follows that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}
is constant with
*
-fibers equal to the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}
. In other words,

{\mathcal{A}}^{\vee,\operatorname{KD}}\simeq{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}.

By
[
FG
, Proposition 6.1.2]
, this implies

{\mathcal{A}}\simeq U^{\operatorname{ch}}({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}).

∎

3.4.7.

As a corollary, we have

\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\simeq\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{\operatorname{ch}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{Lie}^{\star}}_{x_{0}}.

Since
{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG,X}
is a constant Lie
⋆
-algebra, the above category is equivalent to the category of modules over the Lie algebra

(3.21)

{\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}),

or which is the same, to the category of modules over

(3.22)

U({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})).

3.4.8.

The category
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}
is equivalent to the category of modules over

(3.23)

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}),

viewed as an associative algebra via the product operation on
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}
be the group-object in the category of Lie algebras that controls the homotopy of
G
.
The assumption that
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected implies that
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is connected
and simply-connected. According to
[
GL
, Theorem 1.4.4]
, the group-object in the category of Lie algebras
that controls the rational homotopy of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
is canonically isomorphic to

{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}).

Hence,

(3.24)

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{a}}_{G}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})).

The structure of an associative algebra on
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{a}}_{G}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}))
is induced
by the group structure on
{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})
as a Lie algebra, where the latter results from the
group structure on
G
.

3.4.9.

Note that we have

{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}=\Omega({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}),

as group-objects in the category of Lie algebras,
where
\Omega(-)
is the loop functor on the category of Lie algebras.

Hence, we also have

{\mathfrak{a}}_{G}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})\simeq\Omega({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})).

Finally, according to
[
GR2
, Chapter 5, Theorem 6.1.2]
, we have

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(\Omega(-))\simeq U(-),

and hence the associative algebras (
3.22
) and (
3.23
) are canonically
isomorphic.

3.4.10.

Summarizing, we obtain

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(\Omega({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}))){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\\
\simeq U({\mathfrak{a}}_{BG}\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}})){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\pi_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.

4.
Proof of Theorem
3.1.7
: reduction to the case of a trivial action

In this subsection we will reduce the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
to the case when
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
. The main
tool will be the notion of
almost trivial
action of a group on a category.

4.1.
Almost constant sheaves

In this subsection we discuss the notion of
almost constant
sheaf on a scheme of finite type.
This notion will be relevant for defining the notion of
almost trivial
action in the subsequent subsections.

4.1.1.

Let
Y
be a scheme of finite type. Let

(4.1)

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}(Y)

be the full subcategory generated by the constant sheaf
\underline{k}_{Y}
.

We have a canonical identification

(4.2)

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y){\operatorname{-mod}},

given by the action of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y)
on
\underline{k}_{Y}
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

We warn the reader that the assignment
Y\mapsto\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
(unlike its close relative

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
, see Sect.
4.1.4
) is
not
a sheaf even
for the Zariski topology. See, however, Sect.
5.2.3
for a descent-type statement.

4.1.3.

The embedding (
4.1
) admits a right adjoint. In terms of the identification (
4.2
),
this right adjoint is given by

{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}(Y)\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y,{\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y){\operatorname{-mod}}.

Thus, we can view
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
as a quotient of
\operatorname{D}(Y)
by the full subcategory

\{{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}(Y)\,|\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y,{\mathcal{F}})=0\}.

4.1.4.

The category
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
carries a natural t-structure,
in which
\underline{k}_{Y}
is in the heart.

Let

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

be the left-completion of
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
with respect to this t-structure.
It is easy to see that the embedding (
4.1
) extends to a fully faithful embedding

(4.3)

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}(Y).

The essential image of (
4.3
) is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(Y)
consisting
of objects, whose cohomologies with respect to either perverse or the
usual
t-structure
7
7

7

One can mimic the definition
of the usual t-structure on constructible sheaves and define its counterpart on
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(Y)
.

admit a filtration with
subquotients isomorphic to
\underline{k}_{Y}
.

Remark 4.1.5
.

The (fully faithful) embedding

(4.4)

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

is not always an equivalence. E.g., it fails to be an equivalence for
Y={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
(and, which is more, relevant for us,
for
Y
being a semi-simple group).

4.1.6.

Assume that
Y
is connected and simply-connected. Choose a base point
y\in Y
, and let

L_{Y}
be the Lie algebra that controls the homotopy type of
Y
(since
Y
is simply-connected,

L_{Y}
sits in cohomological degrees
\leq-1
). In particular, we have

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y)\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(L_{Y})\text{ and }\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(Y)\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(L_{Y}).

The functor of *-fiber at
y
defines an equivalence

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\simeq L_{Y}{\operatorname{-mod}}.

In particular, we obtain that (
4.4
) is an equivalence if and only if
the universal enveloping algebra of
L_{Y}
is eventually coconnetive (equivalently, if
L_{Y}

sits only in
odd
degrees).

Note also that if
U(L_{Y})
is
not
eventually coconnetive, the embedding (
4.3
)
does not preserve compactness; in particular, its right adjoint is discontinuous.

4.1.7.

Let

(4.5)

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}(Y)

be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(Y)
obtained from ones in (
4.4
)
by
\overset{*}{\otimes}
-tensoring with
\omega_{Y}
.

We still have an equivalence

(4.6)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}},

given by sending

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y)\in\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y){\operatorname{-mod}}\,\mapsto\omega_{Y}\in\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}.

4.1.8.

Note that Verdier duality identifies

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq(\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{\vee}.

Under this identification, the functor (
4.5
) is the dual of the
right adjoint of (
4.1
).

4.1.9.

Let now
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an ind-scheme of ind-finite type:

{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,Y_{i},

where
Y_{i}
’s are schemes of finite type, and the transition maps
Y_{i_{1}}\to Y_{i_{2}}
are closed embeddings.

Recall that the category
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})
is defined as

\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i}),

where the limit is taken with respect to the !-pullbacks.

Let

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})

be the full subcategory equal to

\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i})

4.1.10.

Recall that

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}).

Hence,
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})
acts on
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}
, and we obtain a functor

(4.7)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}.

However, the functor (
4.7
) is in general not even fully faithful
(e.g., it fails to be such for
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}
).

4.1.11.

Note that we can also write

(4.8)

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i}),

with transition functors being given by *-pushforward.

We let
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
be the
quotient
category of
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})
,
defined in terms of (
4.8
) as

\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}.

We do not know whether the category
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is dualizable in general.
However, we have:

(4.9)

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}},\operatorname{Vect})\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}.

4.1.12.

In terms of the equivalence (
4.2
), we have:

(4.10)

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}},

where the transition functors are given by restriction along the maps

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i_{2}})\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i_{1}}).

We have a naturally defined functor

(4.11)

\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}},

given by restriction.

In terms of (
4.9
), the dual of the functor (
4.11
)
is the functor (
4.7
).

4.2.
Almost trivial actions: the case of algebraic groups

In this subsection we develop the notion of
almost trivial
action for groups of finite type.

4.2.1.

Let
H
be an algebraic group of finite type.

Consider the embedding

(4.12)

\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}(H).

By (
4.2
), we can identify

(4.13)

\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H){\operatorname{-mod}}.

4.2.2.

The subcategory (
4.12
) is preserved by the monoidal operation, and
hence inherits a monoidal structure. The
right
adjoint to (
4.12
)
is (strictly) compatible with monoidal structures.

In terms of the identification (
4.13
), the monoidal structure
on
\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
corresponds to the Hopf algebra structure on
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H)
,
induced by the group-structure on
H
.

The latter description implies that the monoidal category
\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}

is
semi-rigid
(see
[
AGKRRV
, Appendix C]
for what this means).

4.2.3.

The monoidal functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H,-):\operatorname{D}(H)\to\operatorname{Vect}

induces a monoidal functor

(4.14)

\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\to\operatorname{Vect},

which admits a left adjoint. The functor (
4.14
) is conservative.

In terms of the identification (
4.13
), the functor (
4.14
)
corresponds to the forgetful functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

4.2.4.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a category acted on by
H
. Set

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}):=\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\underset{\operatorname{D}(H)}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}.

The embedding (
4.12
) and its right adjoint give rise to a pair of adjoint functors

(4.15)

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}},

with the left adjoint being fully faithful.

4.2.5.

We shall say that action of
{\mathbf{C}}
is
almost trivial
if the functors (
4.15
)
are mutually inverse equivalences.

4.2.6.
Example

An example to keep in mind of an action that is
not
almost trivial is

{\mathbf{C}}:=\operatorname{D}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}.

In fact, for

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{D}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}/\operatorname{D}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}},

we have
({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{G}=0
.

4.2.7.

Let
(H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-trivial}}\subset H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
be the full subcategory that consists of
H
-module
categories equipped with an almost trivial action.

The embedding

(4.16)

(H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-trivial}}\hookrightarrow H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

admits
a right adjoint, given by

(4.17)

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}).

The counit of this adjunction is the left adjoint in (
4.15
). Since (
4.15
)
admits a right adjoint, we can identify (
4.17
) also with the
left
adjoint
of (
4.16
).

4.2.8.

Consider the category

\operatorname{inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}):={\mathbf{C}}^{H}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{D}(H)}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}.

Consider the corresponding pair of
adjoint functors

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{H}:{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}}:\operatorname{Av}^{H}_{*}.

It is clear that the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{H}
has essential image contains in
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})
.
Hrnce, the functor
\operatorname{Av}^{H}_{*}
factors as

{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}}^{H},

where the first arrow is the right adjoint in (
4.15
), and the second arrow is the right adjoint to

(4.18)

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{H}:{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\to\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}}).

One can view the adjunction

{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})

as obtained by tensoring
-\underset{\operatorname{D}(H)}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}

from the adjunction

(4.19)

\operatorname{Vect}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}.

From the above it follows that the essential image of
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{H}
generates
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})
under colimits.
Moreover, we have:

Lemma 4.2.9
.

The kernel of the right adjoint in (
4.15
) equals
\operatorname{ker}(\operatorname{Av}^{H}_{*})
.

4.2.10.

Note that the left adjoint in (
4.19
) itself admits a left adjoint
8
8

8

This follows, e.g., from the fact that

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H)
is finite-dimensional.
. Since the monoidal category

\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is semi-rigid, this left adjoint is automatically
\operatorname{D}(H)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
-linear.

This implies that the forgetful functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\to\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})
also admits a left adjoint,
functorially in
{\mathbf{C}}
. We denote this left adjoint by
\operatorname{Av}^{H}_{!}
.

4.2.11.

Consider the functor

\operatorname{inv}_{H}:H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{DGCat}.

It naturally enhances to a functor

\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{H}:H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{H}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

where we identify

\operatorname{Vect}^{H}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect})

as a monoidal category.

The functor
\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{H}
admits a left adjoint, given by

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{H}}{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}.

It is clear, however, that the adjunction
((\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{H})^{L},\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{H})
factors
as

\operatorname{Vect}^{H}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\rightleftarrows(H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}\rightleftarrows H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

We claim:

Proposition 4.2.12
.

The adjoint functors

(4.20)

\operatorname{Vect}^{H}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\rightleftarrows(H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}

are mutually inverse equivalences.

The proposition will be proved in Sect.
5.1
.

Corollary 4.2.13
.

For
{\mathbf{C}}\in H{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the counit of the adjunction

\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{H}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\to{\mathbf{C}}

is fully faithful with essential image
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{H}({\mathbf{C}})
.

4.3.
Almost constant sheaves on the affine Grassmannian

In this subsection we give a Koszul-dual description of the category of almost constant sheaves on the affine Grassmannian
and related geometries.

4.3.1.
Notational change

From now and until Sect.
4.7
we will adopt the following
notational change
9
9

9

We do it since the geometry of the curve will not be involved, unlike other
places in this paper.

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\mathfrak{L}}(G),\,\,{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G),\,\,{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}.

4.3.2.

We return to the setting of Sect.
4.1.12
. We take

{\mathcal{Y}}
to be the
neutral connected component
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K
, where

K=K_{i}
for
i\geq 0
.

4.3.3.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}:={\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K
. Consider the restriction map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0}).

From (
3.24
)
10
10

10

Indeed, the cochain algebras involved are the same as in the
semi-simple simply-connected case.
, we obtain that the restriction map

(4.21)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0}){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}

preserves compactness.

We let
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}
,
generated by objects in the essential image of (
4.21
).
The embedding

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}

admits a continuous right adjoint.

4.3.4.
Example

Let
i=0
, so
{\mathcal{Y}}
is the neutral component of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{x_{0}}}
. We have

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(V),

where
V
is a finite-dimensional, cohomologically graded vector space,
concentrated in positive even degrees.

In this case

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0},

where the category in the right-hand side is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}
,
generated by the augmentation module.

4.3.5.

Recall the functor (
4.11
). We claim:

Proposition 4.3.6
.

The functor (
4.11
), i.e.,

\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}},

is an equivalence onto
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\subset\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}
.

The proposition will be proved in Sect.
5.3
.

Corollary 4.3.7
.

The category
\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is dualizable.

Corollary 4.3.8
.

The category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is dualizable.

Corollary 4.3.9
.

For a category
{\mathbf{C}}
, the functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}},{\mathbf{C}})

is an equivalence.

Corollary 4.3.10
.

For a category
{\mathbf{C}}
, the functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Follows from the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\operatorname{alm-const}},{\mathbf{C}})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K),{\mathbf{C}}),\end{CD}

in which the right vertical arrow is given by Verdier duality on
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)
, and the bottom horizontal arrow
is fully faithful.

∎

Remark 4.3.11
.

As another consequence of Proposition
4.3.6
, we obtain that

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)

is the full subcategory generated under colimits by the dualizing sheaf.

4.4.
Almost constant sheaves on the loop group

In this subsection we define what we mean by the category of almost constant sheaves on
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
. As usual,
some extra care is needed here, since
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
is of infinite type.

4.4.1.

Let us return to the setting of Sect.
4.1.7
. Let
f:Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a map of schemes
of finite type that is a universal homological equivalence.

In this case the functor

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}(Y)\to\operatorname{D}(Y^{\prime})

gives rise to an
equivalence

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{D}(Y^{\prime})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}.

A similar observation applies to a map between indschemes
f:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
.

4.4.2.

Let
K^{\prime}\subset K
be subgroups as in Sect.
4.3.2
, but we assume that
K
is pro-unipotent.
We obtain that the pullback functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K^{\prime})

induces an equivalence

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K^{\prime})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}.

Denote by

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

the full subcategory equal to the essential image of

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)\overset{!\operatorname{-pullback}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

for some/any
K
as above.

Define

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

similarly, by swapping the roles of left and right.

4.4.3.

We claim:

Proposition 4.4.4
.

The subcategories

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\supset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}

coincide.

Proof.

Let
Y
be a
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
-invariant subscheme of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
. Let
K
be as above.
Note we can find
K^{\prime}
sufficiently small so that the projection

Y\to Y/K

factors
K^{\prime}\backslash Y\to Y/K
; moreover, the latter map is smooth with contractible fibers, and hence is a universal homological
equivalence.

This implies that the subcategory

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}\subset\operatorname{D}(Y)

defined to be the essential image of

(4.22)

\operatorname{D}(Y/K_{1})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}(Y/K_{1})\overset{!\operatorname{-pullback}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{D}(Y)

for some/any
K_{1}
, coincides with the subcategory

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}\subset\operatorname{D}(Y)

defined to be the essential image of

(4.23)

\operatorname{D}(K_{2}\backslash Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}(K_{2}\backslash Y)\overset{!\operatorname{-pullback}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{D}(Y)

for some/any
K_{2}
.

This implies the statement of the proposition, since

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

equals

\underset{Y}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}\subset\underset{Y}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y)

and

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

equals

\underset{Y}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}\subset\underset{Y}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y).

∎

4.4.5.

Denote the subcategory

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{right}}=\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const},\operatorname{left}}

of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
by
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}
.

4.4.6.

From Proposition
4.4.4
and Corollary
4.3.9
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.4.7
.

The coproduct functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

sends
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
to the full subcategory

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)).

4.4.8.

It follows from Corollary
4.3.8
that the category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}
is dualizable.
Denote its dual by
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
; we can view it as a quotient category of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
.

From Corollary
4.4.7
we obtain that the
monoidal
structure on
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
gives
rise to a monoidal structure on
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
.

Remark 4.4.9
.

It follows from Remark
4.3.11
that

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))

is the full subcategory, generated under colimits by the dualizing sheaf.

4.5.
Almost invariants for the loop group

4.5.1.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a category equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
. We let

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
be the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}
equal to

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}}^{K_{1}}).

The contents of Sect.
4.2
apply equally well to this situation.

4.5.2.

Let now
{\mathbf{C}}
be equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
. We let

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
to be the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}

consisting of objects that are sent by the co-action functor

{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes{\mathbf{C}}

to the full subcategory

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\subset\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes{\mathbf{C}}.

We can identify

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})=\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\underset{\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}},{\mathbf{C}}).

4.5.3.

It is easy to see that
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
, viewed as a full subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}
,
is preserved by the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
, i.e., it can itself be viewed as a category acted on by

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
.

4.5.4.
Example

Let
G=T
be a torus. Then it is easy to see that the inclusion

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}({\mathbf{C}})\subset\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)}({\mathbf{C}})

is an equality.

4.5.5.

Unwinding the definitions, we obtain:

Lemma 4.5.6
.

(i) The full subcategories

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\operatorname{-right}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))),\,\,\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\operatorname{-left}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))),\,\,\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\times{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)))

and

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}

of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
, coincide.

(ii) For
K=K_{i}
,
i\geq 1
, the full subcategories

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K))\text{ and }\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}

of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K)
, coincide.

4.5.7.

Consider

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

It follows from the definitions that we have an inclusion of subcategories

(4.24)

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})).

We claim:

Proposition 4.5.8
.

The inclusion (
4.24
) is an equality.

The proof will be given in Sect.
5.4
.

4.5.9.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
. Recall that
{\mathbf{C}}
is said to be
spherically generated
if the
(a priori fully faithful) functor

(4.25)

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\underset{\operatorname{Sph}_{G}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}

is an equivalence, where

\operatorname{Sph}_{G}:=\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}.

We claim:

Proposition 4.5.10
.

Suppose that
{\mathbf{C}}
is spherically generated. Then the embedding

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}

admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the universal case, i.e.,
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
. I.e., we need to show that the
embedding

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}))\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})

admits a left adjoint.

By Proposition
4.5.8
, this is equivalent to showing that the embedding

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})

admits a left adjoint.

Dually, we need to show that the projection functor

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}

admits a continuous right adjoint, i.e., that it preserves compactness.

We can work at one connected component of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
at a time, and it is enough
to consider the neutral connected component; denote it by
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Thus, by Proposition
4.3.6
,
we have to show that the functor

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{F}}),\quad\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}

preserves compactness.

Note, however, that since
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})
is isomorphic to a polynomial algebra on generators in
even
degrees,
an object of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}
is compact if and if the underlying vector space is finite-dimensional.

The required assertion follows now from the fact that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is ind-proper, and so the functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect}

preserves compactness.

∎

Corollary 4.5.11
.

The embedding

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})

admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

Note that
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
is contained in the spherically generated subcategory of

{\mathbf{C}}
, i.e., the essential image of (
4.25
). Hence, we can assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is
spherically generated.

Now the assertion follows from Proposition
4.5.10
.

∎

Remark 4.5.12
.

It follows from the proof of Proposition
4.5.10
that the formation of the left adjoint in
Proposition
4.5.10
(resp., Corollary
4.5.11
) is functorial in
{\mathbf{C}}
, i.e.,
the corresponding Beck-Chevalley natural transformation is an isomorophism.

4.6.
Almost trivial actions of the loop group

In this subsection we define what it means for a
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action on a group to be almost trivial,
and we give a Koszul-dual description of the totality of such categories.

4.6.1.

We shall say that an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
on
{\mathbf{C}}
is
almost trivial
if the embedding

(4.26)

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}

is an equivalence.

4.6.2.

Note that we can also characterize almost trivial actions as follows: an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
on
{\mathbf{C}}
is almost trivial if and only if
the monoidal action of
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
on
{\mathbf{C}}
factors through the quotient

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}}.

4.6.3.

For future use, we notice:

Lemma 4.6.4
.

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
be a 1-morphism in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, which is

conservative
as a functor on the underlying categories. Then if
the action on
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
is almost trivial, then so it is on
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
.

Proof.

Since the action on
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
is almost trivial, both arrows in

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}_{2}

are equivalences. We need to show that the same is true for

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}_{1}.

We have a commutative diagram
11
11

11

The horizontal arrows are the adjoint functors from (
4.15
), both of which
are functorial in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}_{1}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\\
@V{F}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{F}V\\
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}_{2}.\end{CD}

Hence, the fact that the counit of the adjunction

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}}_{2}

is an isomorphism and the conservativity of
F
imply that
the unit of the adjunction

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}}_{1}

is an isomorphism.

The proof for

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{i})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{i})

is similar using left adjoints and Corollary
4.5.11
.

∎

4.6.5.

Let

(4.27)

({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

be the full subcategory, consisting of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
-module categories, on which the action is almost trivial.

Note that we can identify

({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

viewed as a full subcategory of

{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

The assignment

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})

is a right adjoint to the embedding (
4.27
).

4.6.6.

Consider the (symmetric) monoidal category

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
.

The functor

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}:{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{DGCat}

naturally upgrades to a functor

\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}:{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

which admits a left adjoint, given by

(4.28)

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}}{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}.

It is clear that the above adjoint pair factors as

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\rightleftarrows({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}\rightleftarrows{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

4.6.7.

We will prove:

Theorem 4.6.8
.

The adjoint functors

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\rightleftarrows({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}

are mutually inverse equivalences.

The theorem will be proved in Sect.
5.5
.

4.6.9.

From Theorem
4.6.8
we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.10
.

The naturally defined functor

\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}}{\otimes}\operatorname{Vect}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}

is an equivalence.

4.6.11.

For future use, we record the following consequence of Theorem
4.6.8
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
. Note that since
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
is proper, the forgetful functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}:{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}

admits a
left
adjoint, to be denoted

\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{!},

see Sect.
B.4.1
.

We claim:

Proposition 4.6.12
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be spherically generated. Then the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
is almost trivial if the functor
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{!}
is conservative.

Proof.

Since both
{\mathbf{C}}
and
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
are spherically generated, the inclusion

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{C}}

is an equivalence if and only if the functor

((\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}))^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)},

which is a priori also a fully faithful inclusion, is an equivalence.

By Theorem
4.6.8
, we can identify the above functor with

(4.29)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}.

Thus, the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
is almost trivial if and only if the left adjoint to (
4.29
) is conservative.

The precomposition of (
4.29
) with

(4.30)

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\to\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}

is the forgetful functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}
.

Thus, if the left adjoint to

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}
, i.e.,
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{!}
, is conservative, then so is the left adjoint to (
4.29
).

∎

Remark 4.6.13
.

In fact, one can show that the assertion of Proposition
4.6.12
is “if and only if”, but we will not need this.

4.6.14.

Note that we an identify

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)){\operatorname{-mod}},

as monoidal categories.

Hence, being equivalent to the category of modules over a Hopf algebra,
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
is a Frobenius
algebra in
\operatorname{DGCat}
. In particular, we have a canonical equivalence

(\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}

as
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
-module categories.

4.7.
The reduction step

After all the preparations, in this subsection we will finally finally formulate a reduction step in the proof
of Theorem
3.1.7
: the claim is that it is sufficient to prove it for
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
.

4.7.1.

It is clear that the embedding (
4.26
)
induces an equivalence

(\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}))^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}.

4.7.2.

We will prove:

Theorem 4.7.3
.

The embedding (
4.26
) induces an equivalence

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}))^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.

Theorem
4.7.3
will be proved in Sects.
7
-
9
. In the remainder of this section and
Sect.
6
, we will show how Theorem
4.7.3
implies Theorem
3.1.7
.

4.7.4.

Note that by Theorem
4.7.3
and Sect.
4.7.1
we obtain that it suffices to prove
Theorem
3.1.7
for
{\mathbf{C}}\in({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}
.

By Theorem
4.6.8
, we can assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is of the form

\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

4.7.5.

It follows from Theorem
4.6.8
that the operation

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}

preserves limits.

Moreover, by Sect.
4.6.14
, any object of
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
can be written as a totalization of a cosimplicial object with terms
of the form

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\in\operatorname{DGCat}.

4.7.6.

This reduces the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
to the case when the action

{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}\otimes\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\simeq\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\in\operatorname{DGCat},

equipped with the trivial action.

In Sect.
6
, we will prove the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
in this case by
an explicit calculation.

4.7.7.

To simplify the exposition we will consider the case when
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}_{0}=\operatorname{Vect}
.
The case of a general category is completely analogous.

Note that the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
for
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
coincides with that of
Corollary
3.4.2
.

5.
Proofs of Propositions
4.2.12
,
4.3.6
,
4.5.8
and Theorem
4.6.8

The goal of this subsection is to supply proofs of Proposition
4.2.12
,
4.3.6
,
4.5.8
and Theorem
4.6.8
.
These all are Koszul duality-type statements, and we essentially need to take care of convergence issues.

For the duration of this section we keep the notational change

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\mathfrak{L}}(G),\,\,{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G),\,\,{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}.

5.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.12

5.1.1.

It is easy to see that the statement of Proposition
4.2.12
for
H
follows from the corresponding statement
for its neutral connected component. Hence, for the duration of this subsection, we will assume that
H

is connected.

5.1.2.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a monoidal category equipped with a monoidal
\phi
functor to
\operatorname{Vect}
. Denote

{\mathbf{B}}:=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect})^{\operatorname{rev}},

where
{\mathbf{A}}
acts on
\operatorname{Vect}
via
\phi
.

The functor

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbf{A}}=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},{\mathbf{C}})

upgrades to a functor

\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\mathbf{A}}:{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to{\mathbf{B}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

and the latter admits a left adjoint given by

\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}\underset{{\mathbf{B}}}{\otimes}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}},

where the augmentation on
{\mathbf{B}}
is given by the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

We shall say that the pair

({\mathbf{A}},\phi)
satisfies Koszul duality if the adjoint functors
((\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\mathbf{A}})^{L},\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\mathbf{A}})

are mutually inverse equivalences.

5.1.3.

Let
A
be a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra, and let
{\mathbf{A}}:=A{\operatorname{-mod}}
. Let
\phi
be the tautological forgetful
functor
A{\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}
.

It is easy to see that in this case

{\mathbf{B}}\simeq B{\operatorname{-mod}},

where
B
is the linear dual of
A
.

5.1.4.
Example

Let
W
be a compact object of
\operatorname{Vect}
, concentrated in odd degrees. Set

{\mathbf{A}}:=\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}},

which we regard as a monoidal category with respect to
convolution
.
Let
\phi
be the tautological forgetful functor
\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}
.

According to Sect.
5.1.3
,

{\mathbf{B}}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(W^{*}){\operatorname{-mod}}
, viewed as a monoidal category
also with respect to
convolution
.

Then it is easy to see that this pair
({\mathbf{A}},\phi)
satisfies Koszul duality.

5.1.5.

The statement of Proposition
4.2.12
is equivalent to the fact that the monoidal category
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H){\operatorname{-mod}}
equipped
with the tautological forgetful functor to
\operatorname{Vect}
satisfies Koszul duality.

By construction, this example fits the pattern of Sect.
5.1.3
. Hence, it suffices to show that it fits
in fact the pattern of Sect.
5.1.4
.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}
be the Lie algebra that controls the rational homotopy type of
BH
. Note that
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(H)\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\Omega({\mathfrak{a}}))
. Recall also that
{\mathfrak{a}}
is abelian and is concentrated in odd degrees. Hence,

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\Omega({\mathfrak{a}}))\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{{\mathfrak{a}}}^{*}),

as Hopf algebras, where
\underline{{\mathfrak{a}}}
is the vector space underlying
{\mathfrak{a}}
.

∎[Proposition
4.2.12
]

Remark 5.1.6
.

Note that by Sect.
5.1.3
, we obtain that

\operatorname{Vect}^{H}\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(H){\operatorname{-mod}},

as is supposed to be the case.

5.2.
A descent result for almost constant sheaves

5.2.1.

Let
H
be a connected algebraic group, and let

f:\widetilde{Y}\to Y

be an
H
-torsor and
Y
is a scheme of finite type.

We consider the category
\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})
as equipped with an action of
H
.
Its full subcategory
\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is stable under this action; moreover
the
H
-action on
\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is almost trivial.

5.2.2.

The functor of *-pullback identifies

\operatorname{D}(Y)\overset{\sim}{\to}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y}))^{H}.

Since
f^{*}
sends

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\to\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}

and

(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}=\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\underset{\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})}{\times}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y}))^{H},

we obtain a commutative diagram

(5.1)

\begin{CD}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{H}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{\sim}A\\
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{D}(Y),\end{CD}

where the horizontal arrows are fully faithful.

We claim:

Lemma 5.2.3
.

The above functor

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\to(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The functor in question is fully faithful and preserves compactness (since the other three arrows in
(
5.1
) have this property).

Hence, it is enough to show that it sends compact generators of
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
to
generators of
(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}
.

This functor sends

\underline{k}_{Y}\mapsto\underline{k}_{\widetilde{Y}}.

Hence, it remains to show that the latter is a generator of
(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}
. (It is here that
the assumption that
H
is connected will be used.)

Indeed, for a category
{\mathbf{C}}
with an action of
H
and
{\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{H}
, the object

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Vect}

naturally upgrades to an object of
\operatorname{Vect}^{H}
, while

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{H}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{inv}_{H}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})).

Now, if
{\mathbf{c}}
is a generator of
{\mathbf{C}}
, we have

{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}\neq 0\,\Rightarrow{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime})\neq 0.

We now use the fact that for a connected
H
, the functor

\operatorname{inv}_{H}:\operatorname{Vect}^{H}\to\operatorname{Vect}

is conservative.
12
12

12

Indeed, in the notations of Sect.
5.1.5
, we have
\operatorname{Vect}^{H}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{{\mathfrak{a}}}^{*}){\operatorname{-mod}}
,
and since
\underline{{\mathfrak{a}}}^{*}
is concentrated in
odd
degrees, this category is generated by the augmentation module.

∎

Corollary 5.2.4
.

The functor
f^{!}
induces an equivalence

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\to(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}})^{H}.

5.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.3.6

5.3.1.
Reduction to the case of the affine Grassmannian

We change the notations slightly and denote by
{\mathcal{Y}}
the neutral connected component of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
, and by

\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
its preimage in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K
.

Write

{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}=\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,Y_{i},

and set

\widetilde{Y}_{i}:=Y_{i}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}},

so that

\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,\widetilde{Y}_{i}.

We view both sides of

\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{Y}_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}){\operatorname{-mod}},

as acted on (almost trivially) by
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K
. Since operation
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}(-)
is conservative
on the subcategory of almost trivial modules, it suffices to show that the functor

(5.2)

\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{Y}_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}\overset{\sim}{\to}(\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{Y}_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}\to(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}){\operatorname{-mod}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}

is an equivalence onto

(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}\subset(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}){\operatorname{-mod}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}.

By Lemma
5.2.3
, we can identify the terms

(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\widetilde{Y}_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}\simeq(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y}_{i})^{\operatorname{alm-const}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)/K}

with

\operatorname{D}(Y_{i})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}}.

Similarly, it is easy to see (e.g., using (
3.24
)) that the right-hand side in (
5.2
)
identifies with
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}
, which contains
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}
as a full subcategory.

Thus, we obtain that it suffices to show that the resulting map

\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}.

is an equivalence.

5.3.2.

Note that for a
coconnective
algebra
A
and
n\geq 0
, the truncation
A^{\leq n}
has a natural structure
of algebra, equipped with a map from
A
.

Consider the commutative diagram

(5.3)

\begin{CD}\underset{i,n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}))^{\leq n}{\operatorname{-mod}}@>{}>{}>\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}){\operatorname{-mod}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}))^{\leq n}{\operatorname{-mod}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}.\end{CD}

We need to show that the right vertical arrow is an equivalence. We will achieve this by showing that
the other three arrows in (
5.3
) are equivalences.

5.3.3.

The equivalence is immediate for the top horizontal arrow: indeed for a fixed
i
, the family

n\rightsquigarrow(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}))^{\leq n}

stabilizes to
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i})
, since
Y_{i}
is finite-dimensional.

5.3.4.

We claim that for a fixed
n
, the family

i\rightsquigarrow(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y_{i}))^{\leq n}

also stabilizes to
(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}))^{\leq n}
. Indeed, this follows from the cellular decomposition of the affine Grassmannian.

Hence, the left vertical arrow in (
5.3
) is an equivalence.

5.3.5.

Thus, it remains to show that the functor

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}))^{\leq n}{\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}

is an equivalence.

We identify
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})
with
\operatorname{Sym}(V)
, see Sect.
4.3.4
. So we need to show that the functor

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{Sym}(V)/\operatorname{Sym}^{>n}(V)){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}

is an equivalence.

5.3.6.

It is easy to reduce the assertion to the case when
V
is one dimensional. In this case, we can use
the grading-shearing trick (see
[
AG
, Sect. A.2]
),
and assume that
V
is a finite-dimensional vector space in cohomological degree
0
.

Hence, the assertion becomes that

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{Sym}(V)/\operatorname{Sym}^{>n}(V)){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}

is an equivalence. I.e., we have to show that

(5.4)

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S_{n})\to{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})_{0}

is an equivalence, where
S_{n}=\operatorname{Spec}(k[t]/t^{n})
.

Note, however, that the composition

(5.5)

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{IndCoh}(S_{n})\to\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}(S_{n})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:QCoh trunc}}}{\longrightarrow}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})_{0}

is an equivalence (e.g., by
[
GR0
, Proposition 7.4.5]
). This implies that (
5.4
) is an equivalence, since the first arrow
in (
5.5
) is a Verdier quotient.

∎[Proposition
4.3.6
]

5.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.5.8

5.4.1.

Denote
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}:={\mathfrak{L}}(G)/K_{1}
, so that

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}/G.

Unwinding the definitions and using Lemma
4.5.6
, we obtain that the category
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}))

identifies with

(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}})^{G}.

Thus, we need to show that the inclusion

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}})^{G}

is an equality.

5.4.2.

Write

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}=\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,Y_{i},

and set

\widetilde{Y}_{i}:=Y_{i}\underset{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\times}\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G},

so that

\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,\widetilde{Y}_{i}.

We have:

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{i})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}

and

\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y}_{i})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}},

and hence

(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}})^{G}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{Y}_{i})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}})^{G}.

Now the required isomorphism follows from Corollary
5.2.4
.

∎[Proposition
4.5.8
]

5.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.6.8

5.5.1.

We consider the adjunction

(5.6)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\rightleftarrows({\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}})_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}.

Since the functor
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
(and hence
\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
)
commutes with tensor products, the left adjoint in (
5.6
) is fully faithful.

Hence, the two functors are mutually inverse equivalences if and only if
\operatorname{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}

is conservative.

5.5.2.

We first consider the case when
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected.

We view

{\mathbf{A}}:=\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
as a monoidal
category under convolution and a natural monoidal functor
\phi
to
\operatorname{Vect}
.
We claim that this pair
({\mathbf{A}},\phi)
satisfies Koszul duality (see Sect.
5.1.2
).

By Proposition
4.3.6
, we can identify

{\mathbf{A}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0},

equipped with the tautological forgetful functor to
\operatorname{Vect}
.

By (
3.24
) and Sect.
3.4.9
, we can identify
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))
as a Hopf algebra with

\operatorname{Sym}(V)\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(W),

where
V
is a cohomologically graded vector space concentrated in positive even degrees,
and
W
is a cohomologically graded vector space concentrated in positive odd degrees.

Under this identification
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}
corresponds to

\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}.

5.5.3.

It is enough to show that both monoidal categories

{\mathbf{A}}_{1}:=\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\text{ and }{\mathbf{A}}_{2}:=\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}},

equipped with the forgetful functors to
\operatorname{Vect}
, satisfy Koszul duality.

The case of
{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
is immediate, see Sect.
5.1.4
.

5.5.4.

In the case of
{\mathbf{A}}_{1}
, Using the grading-shearing trick (see
[
AG
, Sect. A.2]
), we can assume that
V
is a
finite-dimensional vector space in cohomological degree
0
. In this case, we identify

\operatorname{Sym}(V){\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(V^{*}),

where
V^{*}
is regarded as an (additive) algebraic group. In this case, the Koszul duality
statement is well-known.

5.5.5.

Next we consider the case when
G=T
is a torus. We will show
directly that the counit of the adjunction in (
5.6
) is an equivalence. Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\operatorname{alm-triv}}
.
The counit is the functor

(5.7)

\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\to{\mathbf{C}}.

Consider the short exact sequence

1\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(T)\to\Lambda\to 1.

By Proposition
4.2.12
, in order to show that (
5.7
)
is an equivalence, it suffices to show that

(5.8)

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\overset{\sim}{\to}(\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)}

is an equivalence.

We regard both sides of (
5.8
) as acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)/{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)\simeq\Lambda
.
In order to show that (
5.8
) is an equivalences, it is sufficient that it becomes
so after taking
\Lambda
-invariants:

(5.9)

(\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)})^{\Lambda}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\overset{\sim}{\to}\left(\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\right)^{\Lambda}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)})^{\Lambda}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}.

However, the latter composition is the identity functor

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\simeq(\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)})^{\Lambda}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)}.

5.5.6.

We now consider the case when the derived group
G^{\prime}
of
G
is simply connected. We have a short
exact sequence

1\to G^{\prime}\to G\to T_{0}\to 1,

where
T_{0}
is a torus.

In this case, the fact that the counit of the adjunction in (
5.6
) is an equivalence follows from the validity of
Theorem
4.6.8
for
G^{\prime}
and
T_{0}
by the argument in Sect.
5.5.5
above.

5.5.7.

Finally, let
G
be arbitrary. We wish to show that the functor
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(-)
is conservative.

Choose a short exact sequence

1\to T_{0}\to\widetilde{G}\to G\to 1,

where
T_{0}
is a torus and
\widetilde{G}
is such that its derived group is simply-connected. By what we proved
above, the the operation
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(\widetilde{G})_{x_{0}}}(-)
is conservative. Hence, it suffices to show that for
a functor
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
if

(5.10)

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\to\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}_{2})

is an equivalence, then so is

(5.11)

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(\widetilde{G})}({\mathbf{C}}_{1})\to\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(\widetilde{G})}({\mathbf{C}}_{2}).

For a category
{\mathbf{C}}
with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
, we have

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(\widetilde{G})}({\mathbf{C}})\simeq\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T_{0})}).

We have a monadic adjunction

\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T_{0})}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Vect}.

From here we obtain a monadic adjunction

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(\widetilde{G})}({\mathbf{C}})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}}).

Moreover, for a functor
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
, the functor (
5.10
) intertwines
the two monads. Hence, if (
5.10
) is an equivalence, so is
(
5.11
).

∎[Theorem
4.6.8
]

6.
Proof of Theorem
3.1.7
: the case of a trivial action

In this section we will prove Theorem
3.1.7
for
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
. The proof will amount
