to calculating a certain monad, and this calculation will turn out to be equivalent to the

contractibility
13
13

13

A.k.a.,
non-abelian Poincaré duality
.
statement
from
[
Ga1
]
.

This calculation is the crux of the proof, and expresses the intuitive idea (alluded to in the
Introduction) that

\underset{\text{punctured disc}}{\int}\,{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(G).

6.1.
Setting up the monad

In this subsection we will reduce the assertion of Theorem
3.1.7
for
{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{Vect}
to a calculation that says that
some particular map (in
\operatorname{Vect}
) is an isomorphism.

6.1.1.

We consider the functor

(6.1)

\Phi:\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}(Gr_{G})})_{x_{0}}

of (
3.19
) (which is a particular case of (
3.6
)).

It makes the diagram

(6.2)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{Id}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A{}A@A{}A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{\Phi}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\end{CD}

commute, where the vertical arrows are the tautological forgetful functors.

6.1.2.

Note that the left vertical arrow in (
6.2
) is conservative and admits a left
adjoint, to be denoted
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}
.

By the Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem, we can identify
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}
with the category of modules in
\operatorname{Vect}

over the resulting monad.

The right vertical arrow in (
6.2
) is also conservative. We will show (shortly)
that it also admits a left adjoint.

Thus, in order to prove that
\Phi
is an equivalence, it suffices to show that
\Phi

induces an isomorphism between the two monads.

6.1.3.

Denote
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}:={\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}/K
, where
K=K_{i}
for some/any
i\geq 1
. We consider it as an ind-scheme, equipped with an action of

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
. Consider the corresponding factorization module category at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
:

(6.3)

\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}.

Denote:

\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}:={\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}/K_{i}.

We regard
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
as a factorization module
space at
x_{0}
with respect to the factorization space
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
. The factorization module
category (
6.3
) is given by considering D-modules on
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
,
viewed as a crystal of categories over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
equipped with a natural factorization
structure against
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
.

6.1.4.

Let
\widetilde{\pi}_{x_{0}}
denote the projection
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}\to\operatorname{pt}
, and let
\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
denote the projection

\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}/{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}},

viewed as a map between factorization module spaces at
x_{0}
over
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
.

Pullback with respect to
\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
can be viewed as a functor

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\to\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}

as factorization module categories at
x_{0}
with respect to
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
.

6.1.5.

Consider the following diagram

(6.4)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{\operatorname{Id}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\\
&&@A{}A{\iota_{1}^{!}}A\\
@A{}A{\operatorname{Id}}A\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})\\
&&@A{}A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}@>{\widetilde{\Phi}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A{}A@A{}A{(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{\Phi}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},\end{CD}

where:

•

\iota_{1}
denotes the embedding of the unit point into
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}
;

•

\widetilde{\Phi}
denotes the functor that sends the generator
k\in\operatorname{Vect}
to
\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}}
,
equipped with its natural factorization structure against
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
.

It is easy to see that the outer diagram in (
6.4
) identifies with (
6.2
).

6.1.6.

We will show that:

(1)

The functor
\iota_{1}^{!}\circ{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}
admits a left adjoint;

(2)

The partially defined functor
(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}
, left adjoint to the lower-right vertical functor
in (
6.4
), is defined on the essential images of
\widetilde{\Phi}
and
(\iota_{1}^{!}\circ{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}})^{L}
;

(3)

The Beck-Chevalley natural transformation

(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}\to\Phi\circ\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}

(arising from the lower portion of (
6.4
)) becomes an isomorphism after applying the functor

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\overset{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

(4)

The Beck-Chevalley natural transformation

(\iota_{1}^{!}\circ{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}})^{L}\to\widetilde{\Phi}

(arising from the upper portion of (
6.4
)) becomes an isomorphism after applying the functor

(6.5)

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\overset{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

It is clear that the above properties (1)-(4) imply the required property of the monad from Sect.
6.1.2
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the verification of properties (1)-(4).

Remark 6.1.7
.

We will give a purely geometric proof of Properties (2) and (3). However, if we allow ourselves to use
Theorem
4.7.3
(which will be proved independently), the proof of both properties
can be significantly simplified.

Indeed, by Theorem
4.7.3
, we can replace

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\rightsquigarrow\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}))^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.

Then, the left adjoint in point (2) is induced by the functor

(6.6)

\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}))\to\operatorname{Vect}

as objects of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, where (
6.6
) is the left adjoint to

\operatorname{Vect}\to\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}},\quad k\mapsto\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}},

which exists, e.g., since the objects of
\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}
are ind-holonomic
14
14

14

Alternatively
for any
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the functor
{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}\to\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})
admits a left adjoint, which is the composition

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}({\mathbf{C}})\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}_{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{!}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
.
.

Property (3) follows from the fact that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformation arising from the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{k\mapsto\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}}}>{}>\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A{}A@A{}A{\widetilde{\pi}_{x_{0}}^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}>{}>\operatorname{Vect}\end{CD}

is an isomorphism. This follows by identifying the above diagram with

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\end{CD}

with

{\mathbf{C}}_{1}:=\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\omega,\operatorname{alm-const}}
,
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}=\operatorname{Vect}
and the functor being
\widetilde{\pi}_{x_{0}}^{!}
, and the above fact that

\widetilde{\pi}_{x_{0}}^{!}
admits a left adjoint in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
.

6.2.
Left adjoint for factorization modules

In this subsection we establish Property (1) in Sect.
6.1.6
.

6.2.1.

We will apply Proposition
C.12.11
to

{\mathbf{A}}:=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}),\,\,{\mathbf{C}}=\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},\,\,{\mathcal{A}}:=\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}.

Lemma 6.2.2
.

In the above setting, any object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})
is adapted to
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
-induction (see Sect.
C.12.8
).

6.2.3.

To prove the lemma, we need some notations.

Unwinding the definitions, the functor (
C.51
) is given by

\jmath_{I}^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{I})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I}),

where

•

{\mathcal{Z}}_{I}
is the categorical prestack (see Sect.
C.1.1
)

\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\big)\times\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}},

where

–

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is the space encoding the unital factorization structure of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
(see Sect.
C.1.7
);

–

\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is the space encoding the unital factorization
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
-module structure of
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}

–

(-)_{\operatorname{disj}}
means we apply base-change along the open subspace

\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\subseteq\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},

see Sect.
C.1.13
.

•

{\mathcal{Y}}_{I}
is the categorical prestack

\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\times\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}},

where
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is the space encoding the unital factorization structure of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
, but for the punctured curve
X_{\circ}:=X\setminus x_{0}
. See Sect.
C.12.4
.

•

The morphism

\jmath_{I}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{I}\to{\mathcal{Z}}_{I}

is the base-change of the morphism (
C.50
). The functor

\jmath_{I}^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{I})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I})

is the
!
-pullback functors for lax D-modules (see Sect.
C.3.4
and Sect.
C.3.7
).

6.2.4.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})
and write

\omega\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}})

for the dualizing D-module. To prove Lemma
6.2.2
, we need to check:

•

For any marked finite set
I
, the partially defined left adjoint
\jmath_{I,!}
of
\jmath_{I}^{!}
is defined on the object

(6.7)

(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\omega)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{I}).

•

The canonical morphism

\jmath_{I,!}((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\omega)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{F}})\to\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\omega)\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\jmath_{!}(\omega\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{F}})\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}

is invertible.

6.2.5.

To verify the claims in Sect.
6.2.4
, we need some preparations.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be any categorical prestack. We say a lax D-module
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is ind-holonomic if its
!
-pullback along any affine point
S\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is contained in
\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(S)
. Note that (
6.7
) is an ind-holonomic object.

By definition,
!
-pullback functors preserve ind-holonomic lax D-modules.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a categorical prestack. We say a collection of (finite type) indschemes
(f_{\alpha}:Y_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{Y}})_{\alpha\in A}
over
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
adapted to
!
-direct images
if

•

The functors

f_{\alpha}^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{D}(Y_{\alpha})

are jointly conservative.

•

The left adjoint of
f_{\alpha}^{!}
exists, i.e., we have the
!
-direct image functor

f_{\alpha,!}:\operatorname{D}(Y_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{D}^{{\operatorname{lax}}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

•

The functor
f_{\alpha,!}
preserves ind-holonomic lax D-modules, i.e., we have a functor

(6.8)

f_{\alpha,!}:\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(Y_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{D}^{{\operatorname{lax}}}_{\operatorname{hol}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

It is clear these conditions imply that
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}_{\operatorname{hol}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is generated by the images of (
6.8
).

6.2.6.

Let
\jmath:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
be a morphism between categorical prestacks, and
(f_{\alpha}:Y_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{Y}})_{\alpha\in A}
and
(g_{\beta}:Z_{\beta}\to{\mathcal{Z}})_{\beta\in B}
be collections of indschemes that are adapted to
!
-direct images. We say these two collections are
compatible with
\jmath
if for any
\alpha
, there exists
\beta
such that the composition
Y_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
factors through
Z_{\beta}
.

We claim the above condition implies the partially defined left adjoint
\jmath_{!}
of

\jmath^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Z}})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}})

is defined on
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}_{\operatorname{hol}}({\mathcal{Y}})
. Namely, we only need to show
(\jmath\circ f_{\alpha})_{!}
is defined on
\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(Y_{\alpha})
for any
\alpha\in A
. Note that
\jmath\circ f_{\alpha}
factors as
\jmath\circ f_{\alpha}
as

Y_{\alpha}\xrightarrow{\jmath_{\alpha\beta}}Z_{\beta}\xrightarrow{g_{\beta}}{\mathcal{Z}}

for some
\beta
. Now the claim follows from the following two facts:

•

The partially defined left adjoint
\jmath_{\alpha\beta,!}
of

\jmath_{\alpha\beta}^{!}:\operatorname{D}(Z_{\beta})\to\operatorname{D}(Y_{\alpha})

is defined on
\operatorname{D}_{\operatorname{hol}}(Y_{\alpha})
, because
Y_{\alpha}
and
\beta
are (ind-finite type) indschemes;

•

The functor
g_{\beta,!}:\operatorname{D}(Z_{\beta})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(Z)
left adjoint to
g_{\beta}^{!}
exists by assumption.

6.2.7.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
be a morphism between categorical prestacks and
{\mathcal{W}}
be another categorical prestack. Let
(f_{\alpha}:Y_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{Y}})_{\alpha\in A}
,
(g_{\beta}:Z_{\beta}\to{\mathcal{Z}})_{\beta\in B}
and
(h_{\gamma}:W_{\gamma}\to{\mathcal{W}})_{\gamma\in C}
be collections of indschemes that are adapted to
!
-direct images. We say these collections are
compatble with
f
and
(f,\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{W}})
if

•

(f_{\alpha},h_{\gamma})_{(\alpha,\gamma)\in A\times C}
is a collection of schemes over
{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{W}}
that is adapted to
!
-direct images;

•

(g_{\beta},h_{\gamma})_{(\beta,\gamma)\in B\times C}
is a collection of schemes over
{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{W}}
that is adapted to
!
-direct images;

•

(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in A}
and
(g_{\beta})_{\beta\in B}
are compatible with
f
.

As in Sect.
6.2.6
, one can show these conditions imply for
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\operatorname{hol}}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{Y}})
and
{\mathcal{N}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\operatorname{hol}}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{W}})
, we have

(\jmath,\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{W}})_{!}({\mathcal{M}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{N}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\jmath_{!}({\mathcal{M}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{N}}.

6.2.8.

Finally, let us apply the above paradigm to the claims in Sect.
6.2.4
. We only need to find collections of indschemes over

{\mathcal{W}}:={\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}},\,{\mathcal{Y}}:={\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times\widetilde{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}},\,{\mathcal{Z}}:={\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

satisfying the conditions in Sect.
6.2.7
. Note that here we can ignore the functor
(-)|_{\operatorname{disj}}
because it commutes with arbitrary
!
-direct images.

Note that
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}
are defined over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}
). Now the desired collections of indschemes over them can be given by applying base-change to the schemes

\displaystyle X^{I}\times x_{0}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},\;|I|<\infty

\displaystyle(X-x)^{I}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}},\;|I|<\infty

respectively. Namely, the conditions in Sect.
6.2.7
can be verified by combining the following two arguments:

•

The maps
X^{I}\times x_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
into the
non-unital
marked Ran space are pseudo-proper, hence there is a
!
-direct image functor along

{\mathcal{T}}|_{X^{I}\times x_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}

for
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{Y}}
or
{\mathcal{Z}}
. Moreover, these functors preserve ind-holonomic objects and commute with external tensor products.

•

{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{Y}}
or
{\mathcal{Z}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{T}}={\mathcal{W}}
) is a
coCartesian space
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}
), see Sect.
C.1.5
. Also, for any morphism
\underline{x}\subseteq\underline{x}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
(resp. in
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}
), the structural morphism

{\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}}

is ind-proper. Then we can mimic the construction in
[
Ga1
, Sect. 4.3]
to obtain a
!
-direct image functor along

{\mathcal{T}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{T}},

which preserves ind-holonomic objects and commute with external tensor products.

∎[Lemma
6.2.2
]

6.2.9.

As a consequence of Lemma
6.2.2
, we can apply Proposition
C.12.11
to
V:=\delta_{1}
, where
1\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}

is a unit point. This gives an object

{\operatorname{ind}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}(\delta_{1})\simeq\jmath_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G},\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\delta_{1})\in\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}).

Note that the underlying lax D-module of this object is

\jmath_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\delta_{1})\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}),

where we recall that
\jmath
is the map

\jmath:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\times\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

6.3.
Verification of Properties (2) and (3)

6.3.1.

Using the method in Sect.
6.2.6
- Sect.
6.2.8
, one can show the partially defined functor
(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}
, left adjoint to

(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}),

is defined on ind-holonomic objects, and it sends objects in

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},

whose underlying object of
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}})
is ind-holonomic to objects in

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},

thereby providing a left adjoint to the lower-right vertical functor
in (
6.4
).

This establishes Property (2) in Sect.
6.1.6
.

6.3.2.

We now proceed to establishing Property (3). We need to establish that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformation
corresponding to the diagram

(6.9)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Vect}@>{\widetilde{\Phi}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A{}A@A{}A{(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}@>{\Phi}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\end{CD}

is an isomorphism.

Note that we can view (
6.9
) as a commutative diagram involving the functors (
3.6
):

(6.10)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})@>{\widetilde{\Phi}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}})@>{\Phi}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},\end{CD}

where
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K}
and
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}
are considered as objects of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
with respect to the
left

action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and
(-)^{K}
and
(-)^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}
are taken with respect to the
right
action.

The vertical arrows in (
6.10
) are induced by
the 1-morphism

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K}

in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, given by
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to K}
with respect to the
right
action.

6.3.3.

Let
\Phi^{+}
be the functor

\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},

which is a particular case of (
3.6
) for
{\mathbf{C}}:=(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})
.

We expand diagram (
6.10
) as

(6.11)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})@>{\widetilde{\Phi}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to K}}A{}A@A{}A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to K}}A\\
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})@>{\Phi^{+}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A{}A@A{}A{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}}A\\
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}})@>{\Phi}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.\end{CD}

It is enough to show that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformations

(6.12)

({\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{L}\circ\Phi^{+}_{{\operatorname{oblv}}}\to\Phi\circ({\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{L}

and

(6.13)

({\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to K})^{L}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}\to\Phi^{+}\circ({\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to K})^{L}

are both isomorphisms.

6.3.4.

The assertion for (
6.12
) follows from the fact that the 1-morphism

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}:(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}

admits a left adjoint already in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, see Sect.
4.6.11
.

6.3.5.

To prove the assertion for (
6.13
), we expand the upper portion of (
6.11
):

(6.14)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})@>{\widetilde{\Phi}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{alm-}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})@>{\Phi^{\operatorname{alm-}+}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{alm-}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})@>{\Phi^{+}}>{}>\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}},\end{CD}

where the symbol
\operatorname{alm-}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
refers to
almost invariants
with respect to
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

It suffices to show that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformations corresponding to both subdiagrams are isomorphisms.

For the lower square this follows from the fact that
the 1-morphism

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{alm-}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}

admits a left adjoint already in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, see Sect.
4.2.10
.

6.3.6.

For the upper square in (
6.14
) we note:

•

The left vertical arrow is an equivalence;

•

The right vertical arrow is fully faithful. Indeed, the 1-morphism

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{alm-}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{K}

in
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is fully faithful and admits a
right
adjoint.

Now, the fact that the Beck-Chevalley natural transformation is an isomorphism follows from the next
general assertion:

Lemma 6.3.7
.

Let

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}_{1}@>{\Phi}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\\
@A{\iota_{1}}A{}A@A{\iota_{2}}A{}A\\
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}@>{\Phi^{\prime}}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{2}\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram, in which
\iota_{1}
is an equivalence and
\iota_{2}
is fully faithful.
Then the Beck-Chevalley natural transformation

(\iota_{2})^{L}\circ\Phi\to\Phi^{\prime}\circ(\iota_{1})^{L}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the natural transformation

(\iota_{2})^{L}\circ\Phi\circ\iota_{1}\to\Phi^{\prime}\circ(\iota_{1})^{L}\circ\iota_{1}

is an isomorphism.

We have a commutative diagram of functors

\begin{CD}(\iota_{2})^{L}\circ\Phi\circ\iota_{1}@>{}>{}>\Phi^{\prime}\circ(\iota_{1})^{L}\circ\iota_{1}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
(\iota_{2})^{L}\circ\iota_{2}\circ\Phi^{\prime}@>{}>{}>\Phi^{\prime}.\end{CD}

We have to show that the top horizontal arrow is an isomorphism.
However, the conditions of the lemma imply that all three other arrows
are isomorphisms.

∎

6.4.
Verification of Property (4)

In this subsection we perform the key calculation involved in the proof of Theorem
3.1.7
.

6.4.1.

We rewrite the functor (
6.5
) as

(6.15)

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\overset{{\operatorname{oblv}}}{\to}\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}})\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})\overset{\iota_{x_{0}}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where

\iota_{x_{0}}:\operatorname{pt}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}

is the base change of the map
x_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
.

Note that by Sect.
6.2.9
, the image of the natural transformation

(\iota_{1}^{!}\circ{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}})^{L}\to\widetilde{\Phi}

evaluated on
k\in\operatorname{Vect}
under the first arrow in (
6.15
) is the map

(6.16)

\jmath_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\circ}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\delta_{1})\to\jmath_{!}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\circ}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}})\simeq\jmath_{!}\circ\jmath^{!}(\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}})\to\omega_{\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}}.

6.4.2.

Denote by
\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}
the projection

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}.

Note that this morphism is pseudo-proper (see
[
Ga1
, Sect. 1.5.3]
); hence, the functor

(\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})_{!}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})\to\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}),

left adjoint to
(\pi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})^{!}
, is well-defined and satisfies base change.

Hence, we obtain that the functor

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}})\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})\overset{(\iota_{x_{0}})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

appearing in (
6.15
) can be rewritten as

(6.17)

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}})\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}})\overset{(-)_{x_{0}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where:

•

\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
denotes the projection
\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
;

•

(\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{!}
denotes the partially defined left adjoint to
(\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})^{!}
.

Thus, we obtain that we need to show that the morphism (
6.16
) becomes an isomorphism after applying the functor
(
6.17
).

6.4.3.

The question is local; hence we can assume that
(X,x_{0})=({\mathbb{A}}^{1},0)
. Consider the objects of
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})
obtained
by applying the functor

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}})\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}})\xrightarrow{\imath^{!}}\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})

to the two sides of (
6.16
), where
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
is the
non-unital
marked Ran space (which is a non-categorical prestack) and

\imath:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}

is the obvious map. Note that both these objects are equivariant with respect to the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

on
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
by dilations.

6.4.4.

Now we apply the contraction principle to the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, which says the functors

x_{0}^{!}:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

and

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},-):\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})\to\operatorname{Vect}

are canonically equivalent when restricted to
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant D-modules on
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
15
15

15

Proof: the map
x_{0}:\operatorname{pt}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
is right inverse to
p:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{pt}
. Hence
x_{0}^{!}\circ p^{!}\simeq\operatorname{Id}
. We only need to show this natural isomorphism exhibits
x_{0}^{!}
and the left adjoint of
p^{!}
, when restricted to
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant objects. Note that we have

{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{I\in\operatorname{Fin}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}\times_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}X^{I})

where
\operatorname{Fin}
is the category of finite sets. Moreover, this isomorphism is compatible with the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-actions on both sides. Hence the desired claim follows from the contraction principle for schemes (
[
DG
, Appendix A]
).
. This implies the functors

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\overset{(-)_{x_{0}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}

and

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\xrightarrow{\imath^{!}}\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},-)}\operatorname{Vect},

are canonically equivalent when restricted to
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant D-modules on
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
. Note that by
[
GLC2
, Lemma C.5.12]
, the latter functor is canonically equivalent to

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}},-)}\operatorname{Vect}.

6.4.5.

Thus, it remains to show that the morphism (
6.16
) becomes an isomorphism after applying the functor

(6.18)

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}})\overset{(\widetilde{\pi}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}})\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

6.4.6.

Let us again assume that
(X,x_{0})
is arbitrary. We obtain that it suffices to show that the morphism (
6.16
)
induces an isomorphism after applying the functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}},-).

I.e., we need to show that the locally closed embedding

(6.19)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\circ}}\times\{1\}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\circ}}\times\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}

induces an isomorphism on homology.

As we will show in the next subsection, the latter assertion follows from the
homological contractibility

statement from
[
Ga1
]
.

6.5.
The contractibility statement

6.5.1.

Write

X=\overline{X}-\underline{x},

where
\overline{X}
is a complete (and smooth) curve and
\underline{x}=\{x_{1},...,x_{n}\}
is a finite collection
of points on
\overline{X}
.

Let
\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
denote the moduli stack of
G
-bundles on
\overline{X}
. Let
\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}

(resp.,
\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}
) be the moduli stack of
G
-bundles with structure of level

1
at
\{x_{1},...,x_{n}\}
(resp., additional structure of level
K
at
x_{0}
).

We have a commutative diagram

(6.20)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\circ}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:locally closed}}}>{}>\widetilde{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}@>{\operatorname{id}}>{}>\widetilde{\operatorname{Bun}}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}.\end{CD}

We will show that both vertical maps in (
6.20
) induce isomorphisms at the level of
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(-)
.
This will establish the corresponding fact for the morphism (
6.19
).

We will show that both vertical maps in (
6.20
) are in fact universal homological equivalences.

6.5.2.

The fact that the left vertical arrow in (
6.20
) is a universal homological equivalence is
the statement of the contractibility theorem from
[
Ga1
]
, applied to the complete curve
\overline{X}

and
\{x_{0},x_{1},...,x_{n}\}
as marked points.

6.5.3.

Note that the right vertical arrow in (
6.20
) is the base change of the map

(6.21)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}}\to\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}.

Hence, it suffices to show that (
6.21
) is a universal homological equivalence.

6.5.4.

Consider the forgetful map

{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
and the map
\operatorname{add}_{x_{0}}:{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{x_{0}}
.
They realize
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
as a retract of
{\operatorname{Ran}}
. Similarly, the map (
6.21
)
is a retract of the map

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\to\operatorname{Bun}^{\operatorname{level}^{1}_{\underline{x}}}_{G}.

Hence, it suffices to show that the latter map is a universal homological equivalence.
However, this is again an instance of the contractibility theorem, applied to the complete curve
\overline{X}

and
\{x_{1},...,x_{n}\}
as marked points.

∎

7.
Proof of Theorem
4.7.3
for a torus

For the next three sections we will be concerned with the proof of Theorem
4.7.3
.

In this section we take
G=T
to be a torus, and we will prove Theorem
4.7.3

using local geometric class field theory.

This special case will also be used in the proof of the general case of Theorem
4.7.3
.

7.1.
Reduction to the case of characters

7.1.1.

Write

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}\simeq T\times\operatorname{ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathfrak{t}})\to{\mathfrak{t}}).

Fourier-Laumon transforms identifies the monoidal category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}})
with

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}/\check{\Lambda})\otimes\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathfrak{t}})\to{\mathfrak{t}})^{*}),

equipped with the
pointwise
tensor product.

7.1.2.

Note that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the inclusion

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}}

is an equivalence if and only if for every
non-zero
geometric point

\chi^{+}\in({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}/\check{\Lambda})\times(\operatorname{ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathfrak{t}})\to{\mathfrak{t}})^{*}),

the fiber
{\mathbf{C}}_{\chi^{+}}
is zero, see
[
AGKRRV
, Lemma 21.4.6]
.

7.1.3.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
. Thus, we obtain that in order to prove
Theorem
4.7.3
, it suffices to show that for any such
\chi^{+}
,

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}_{\chi^{+}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}})_{x_{0}}=0.

Up to changing the ground field, we can assume that
\chi^{+}
is a rational point.

7.1.4.

Note that
k
-rational points of
({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}/\check{\Lambda})\times(\operatorname{ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}({\mathfrak{t}})\to{\mathfrak{t}})^{*})
can be thought
of character sheaves on
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}
.

For
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
and a
k
-rational point
\chi^{+}
as above, we have

{\mathbf{C}}_{\chi^{+}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{0},

where
{\mathbf{C}}_{0}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
and the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}
is via the first factor.

Thus, we need to show that for a non-trivial
\chi^{+}
and any
{\mathbf{C}}_{0}
, the category

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{0})_{x_{0}}

is zero.

7.1.5.

We will prove:

Theorem 7.1.6
.

For a non-trivial
\chi^{+}
,
the category

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}((\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})})_{x_{0}}

is zero.

The proof in the presence of
{\mathbf{C}}_{0}
is the same. The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
7.1.6
.

7.2.
Character sheaves on
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}

7.2.1.

Let
\chi
be an extension of
\chi^{+}
to a character sheaf on all of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}
. (Note that the space of such extensions
is a torsor over

\operatorname{Hom}(\Lambda,B(k^{\times}))\simeq B({\check{T}}(k)),

where
{\check{T}}
is the Langlands-dual torus.)

The choice of
\chi
equips the category
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})}
with an action of
\Lambda
, so that

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})})^{\Lambda}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

Let us denote the above copy of
\operatorname{Vect}
, viewed as an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, by
\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}
.

7.2.2.

Using the equivalence
16
16

16

Note that the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\Lambda){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, which appears in the formula below, identifies with
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\check{T}})
,
viewed as a monoidal category under
convolution
.

\Lambda{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\Lambda){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

of
[
Ga3
]
, we can recover
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi^{+})}
as

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\Lambda)}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}).

Hence, we can reformulate Theorem
7.1.6
as:

Theorem 7.2.3
.

The category

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})})_{x_{0}}

is zero.

7.2.4.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
7.2.3
. The proof will amount
to a simple computation, once we input the assertion of geometric class field theory (gCFT), reviewed
in the next subsection.

7.3.
Geometric class field theory

7.3.1.

Consider the map

X\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}},\quad x\mapsto\{x,x_{0}\}.

Let
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,X}
denote the pullback of
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
along this map.
Denote by
j
and
i
the open and closed embeddings

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,X-x_{0}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,X}\hookleftarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,x_{0}},

respectively.

Note that we have the identifications

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,X-x_{0}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}\text{ and }{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,x_{0}}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}.

7.3.2.

For
\lambda\in\Lambda
consider the connected component

({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}=({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{0}\subset({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}.

Its closure in
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{T,X}
, denoted
\overline{({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}}
, is a
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}
-torsor over
X
,
and its special fiber identifies with

({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{\lambda}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}.

7.3.3.

We will need the following statement from gCFT:

Theorem 7.3.4
.

Up to replacing
X
by an open subset containing
x_{0}
, there exists a
{\check{T}}
-local system
\sigma
on
X-x
such that
for every
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the local system

\lambda(\sigma)\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\chi^{+}

on

X\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}\simeq({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}

extends to a local system on
\overline{({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}}
.

Moreover,
\sigma
is unique, up to tensoring with
{\check{T}}
-local systems unramified near
x
.

7.3.5.

Note that a choice of
\sigma
as in the theorem determines an extension
\chi^{+}\rightsquigarrow\chi
. Indeed,
the restriction of
\chi
to
({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{\lambda}
equals the restriction of the extended local system to

\overline{({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,X-x_{0}})^{\lambda}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}}\hookleftarrow({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{\lambda}.

7.3.6.

Note that the assignment

\lambda\rightsquigarrow\lambda(\sigma)

extends to a local system on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}}
, to be denoted
\Lambda(\sigma)
, which is equipped with a natural factorization
structure.

It follows formally from Theorem
7.3.4
that the local system

\Lambda(\sigma)\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\chi

on

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}\simeq({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}\times\{x_{0}\})\underset{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}

extends (uniquely) to a local system, to be denoted
\chi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
, on

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
.

Moreover,
\chi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
has a natural factorization structure with respect to
\Lambda(\sigma)
.

7.4.
The module
\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})}

Recall the object

(7.1)

\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

In this subsection we will describe it as the factorization restriction
of the tautological (i.e., vacuum) object
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\in\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
.

7.4.1.

Denote by
\pi_{!}^{\sigma}
the factorization functor
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})\to\operatorname{Vect}
, given by the precomposition of
\pi_{!}
(see Sect.
3.2.1
) with
the operation of tensoring by the inverse of
\Lambda(\sigma)
.

We claim:

Proposition 7.4.2
.

The object (
7.1
) identifies canonically with
\mathbf{Res}_{\pi_{!}^{\sigma}}(\operatorname{Vect}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})
.

Proof.

Note that the operation of tensoring by
\Lambda(\sigma)
is a factorization automorphism of
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})
.

Moreover, tensoring by
\chi_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
defines an
isomorphism

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}}\to(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})})^{({\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}},\chi)},

i.e.,

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\chi}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})},

compatible with the above automorphism of
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{T})
.

Now, the assertion of the proposition follows from that of Proposition
3.2.3
.

∎

7.4.3.

Denote

R_{{\check{T}},\sigma}:=\pi_{!}^{\sigma}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{T}})\in\operatorname{FactAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}).

Thus, by (
1.9
), we can reformulate Theorem
7.1.6
as follows:

Theorem 7.4.4
.

The category
R_{{\check{T}},\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
is zero.

Remark 7.4.5
.

The assertion of Theorem
7.4.4
follows easily from that of
[
Bogd
, Equation (4.10) and/or Theorem 3.8]
.
Below will give an alternative (in a sense, more elementary) proof.

7.5.
Proof of Theorem
7.4.4
via chiral algebras

7.5.1.

Let
R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}
be the chiral algebra (on
X-x_{0}
) corresponding to
R_{{\check{T}},\sigma}
,
so that

R_{{\check{T}},\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\simeq R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{x_{0}},

see
[
GLC2
, Sect. D.1]
.

Thus, we need to show that
R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{x_{0}}=0
.

7.5.2.

Note that the D-module on
X-x_{0}
underlying
R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}
identifies canonically with

\underset{\lambda}{\oplus}\,R^{\operatorname{ch},\lambda}_{{\check{T}},\sigma},\quad R^{\operatorname{ch},\lambda}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}=\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1].

The chiral operation is given by

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2}})_{*}((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(\mu(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1]))\to\\
\to\Delta_{*}((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\overset{!}{\otimes}(\mu(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1]))[1]\simeq\Delta_{*}((\lambda+\mu)(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1]).

7.5.3.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be an object of
R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{x_{0}}
; let
M
denote the underlying the vector space,
so that the D-module underlying
{\mathcal{M}}
is
i_{*}(M)
.

The chiral action is given by

\operatorname{act}:(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\to i_{*}(M).

The axiom of chiral action implies that the (signed) sum of the following three morphisms

(7.2)

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\to\Delta_{*}\circ i_{*}(M)

(as D-modules on
X^{2}
) is zero:

•

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\overset{\{-,-\}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\to}\Delta_{*}\circ(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\overset{\operatorname{act}}{\to}\Delta_{*}\circ i_{*}(M)
;

•

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{act}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to(\operatorname{id}\times i)_{*}\circ(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M\overset{\operatorname{act}}{\to}(\operatorname{id}\times i)_{*}\circ i_{*}(M)=(i\times i)_{*}(M)=\Delta_{*}\circ i_{*}(M);

•

The map, obtained from the previous one by interchanging the roles of
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.

7.5.4.

Since
\sigma
is non-trivial at
x
, we can choose
\lambda\in\Lambda
, so that the 1-dimensional local system

\lambda(\sigma)
is non-trivially ramified at
x
.

Let us restrict the three maps in (
7.2
) to

(7.3)

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}(R^{\operatorname{ch},\lambda}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}R^{\operatorname{ch},-\lambda}_{{\check{T}},\sigma})\otimes M.

Note that the space of maps

(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}})\to i_{*}(M)

is zero, and similarly for
-\lambda
.

Hence, the restriction of the 2nd and 3rd maps to (
7.3
) are zero. Hence, so is the restriction of the 1st map.

7.5.5.

By definition, the first map identifies with

(7.4)

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}\left((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(-\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\right)\otimes M\to\\
\to\Delta_{*}\circ(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\otimes M\overset{\operatorname{act}}{\to}\Delta_{*}\circ i_{*}(M).

Note however, that the above copy of

\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1]=R^{\operatorname{ch},0}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}

is the chiral unit in
R^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\check{T}},\sigma}
, and since
{\mathcal{M}}
is a unital chiral module, the last arrow in (
7.4
)
comes from the canonical map

(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\to i_{*}(k).

Since (
7.4
) is zero, this means that the first arrow in (
7.4
) factors via a map

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}\left((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(-\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\right)\otimes M\to\Delta_{*}(\omega_{X}[-1])\otimes M.

If
M\neq 0
, this would mean that the canonical map

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}\left((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(-\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\right)\to\Delta_{*}\circ(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1]))

factors as

(j_{x_{1}\neq x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{0},x_{2}\neq x_{0}})_{*}\left((\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(-\lambda(\sigma)\otimes\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])\right)\dashrightarrow\\
\dashrightarrow\Delta_{*}(\omega_{X}[-1])\to\Delta_{*}\circ(j_{x\neq x_{0}})_{*}(\omega_{X-x_{0}}[-1])),

which is false.

∎[Theorem
7.4.4
]

8.
Towards the proof of Theorem
4.7.3
: the key geometric lemma

In this subsection we supply a key geometric input for the proof of Theorem
4.7.3
,
incarnated by Lemma
8.2.7
.

8.1.
Extending the loop group action

8.1.1.

Consider the (corr-unital) factorization group ind-scheme
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
and its (co-unital) factorization
group subscheme
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
, so that their fibers at
x_{0}
are
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
and
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
,
respectively and

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(G)/{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G).

We consider also the corresponding tautological (i.e., vacuum) factorization module spaces at
x_{0}
, denoted

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
and
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
over
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
and
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
, respectively.

8.1.2.

Unwinding the definitions, the
!
-pullback along the multiplication map of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
defines a
lax-unital
factorization functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)),

while the similar functor for
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
is a strictly unital factorization functor (see Sect.
C.2.6
and Sect.
C.5
). In other words, we obtain (associative) coalgebra objects

(8.1)

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\in\mathbf{coAlg}(\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}})

while

(8.2)

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G))\in\mathbf{coAlg}(\mathbf{UntlFactCat}).

Note that we have a homomorphism between these coalgebra objects

i^{!}:\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G))

given by
!
-pullback along the factorization map
i:{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
.

8.1.3.

We define a unital factorization action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
on a unital factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
to be a comodule structure of
{\mathbf{A}}
with respect to the coalgebra (
8.1
), such that the composition

{\mathbf{A}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G))\otimes{\mathbf{A}}\xrightarrow{i^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\mathbf{A}}}\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G))\otimes{\mathbf{A}}

is
strictly
unital.

If
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
acts on a unital factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
, and
{\mathbf{D}}
is a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module
category at
x_{0}
, we can similarly define the notion of unital factorization actions of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
that are compatible with the given
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
-action on
{\mathbf{A}}
.

Note that in this case, the fiber
{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}
at
x_{0}
acquires an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
as a DG category.

8.1.4.
Example

An example of a unital factorization category equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
is
{\mathbf{A}}:=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
.

For this choice of
{\mathbf{A}}
, an example of a unital factorization module category
{\mathbf{D}}
equipped with a compatible
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
-action
is
\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
.

Note that the resulting
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action on

(\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}

is given by
left
translations.

8.1.5.

For
{\mathbf{A}}
as above, denote

{\mathbf{A}}^{0}:={\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)},

which is defined as the cosimplicial limit of

{\mathbf{A}}\rightrightarrows\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G))\otimes{\mathbf{A}}\cdots

in
\mathbf{UntlFactCat}
. Note that the forgetful functor

\iota:{\mathbf{A}}^{0}\to{\mathbf{A}}

is a
strictly
unital factorization functor.

For
{\mathbf{D}}
as in Sect.
8.1.3
, consider

{\mathbf{D}}^{0}:=\mathbf{Res}_{\iota}({\mathbf{D}})\in{\mathbf{A}}^{0}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

We claim:

Proposition-Construction 8.1.6
.

Under the above circumstances, we have a natural action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\mathbf{D}}^{0}

as an object of

{\mathbf{A}}^{0}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
,
so that the action on

{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}

is the action from Sect.
8.1.3
.

Proof.

Let

(8.3)

{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}

be the group ind-scheme over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
defined in
[
GLC2
, Sect. C.10.10]
.

By construction,
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
comes equipped
with a projection to the constant group ind-scheme with fiber
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

By the construction of the operation of factorization restriction (see
[
GLC2
, Secrt. B.9.28]
), the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}

on the crystal of categories underlying
{\mathbf{D}}
restricts to an action of

{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
on
\mathbf{Res}_{\iota}({\mathbf{D}})
.
Moreover, this action factors via

{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}.

∎

Remark 8.1.7
.

Informally, Proposition
8.1.6
reads as follows: the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}

commutes with the factorization module structure with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}^{0}
.

Corollary 8.1.8
.

For a factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{0}
, the
category

\iota({\mathcal{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{D}})_{x_{0}}\overset{\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GLC2}{}{}, Lemma B.12.12]}}}{\simeq}{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{D}}^{0})_{x_{0}}

carries an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
compatible with the forgetful functor

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{D}}^{0})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}.

8.1.9.
Example

Let us consider what may be a familiar situation in which Proposition
8.1.6
is applicable.

Consider the factorization category
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
of Kac-Moody representations
(at a given level). It carries an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
(twisted by the level), defined
as in
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.22]
.

The corresponding
category
(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa))^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}
is by the definition the factorization version of the
Kazhdan-Lusztig category, denoted
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.28]
.

Consider

\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}\in\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

Then Proposition
8.1.6
says that the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{x_{0}}

commutes with fusion against objects of
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)
.

In particular, Corollary
8.1.8
says that for a factorization algebra

{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
, the category

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa))_{x_{0}},

carries a natural action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

8.1.10.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
. By the construction of the functor (
2.1
),
the example in Sect.
8.1.4
shows that the resulting object

{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}

carries a compatible action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}
.

Applying Corollary
8.1.8
to

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\in\operatorname{Alg}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})),

viewed as a
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
- (and hence
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
)-equivariant object,
we obtain that the category

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

carries an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, which commutes with the forgetful functor

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\to({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}.

8.2.
The key geometric lemma

8.2.1.

We continue to be in the context of Sect.
8.1.3
. Consider the restrictions

{\mathbf{A}}_{X}:={\mathbf{A}}|_{X}\text{ and }{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}:={\mathbf{D}}|_{X\times x_{0}},

along
X\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
and
X\times x_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}
, respctively.

Denote by

(X-x_{0})\overset{j}{\hookrightarrow}X\overset{i}{\hookleftarrow}\{x_{0}\}

the corresponding morphisms, and by

{\mathbf{A}}_{X-x_{0}}\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}\overset{j_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\overset{i_{*}}{\hookleftarrow}{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}

the corresponding functors.

8.2.2.

For a triple of objects
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{X-x_{0}}
and
{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}
, consider the space

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

For future reference, we note that if
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{0}_{X-x_{0}}
, we have a canonical identification

(8.4)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}(\iota({\mathbf{a}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})),

see Sect.
8.1.5
for the notation. This follows from the
construction of the operation of factorization restriction (see
[
GLC2
, Secrt. B.9.28]
).

8.2.3.

More generally, for an affine test-scheme
S
and an
S
-point
g
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, consider the spaces

(8.5)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)}\left(\operatorname{pr}^{!}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})),\operatorname{pr}^{!}(i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\right)

and

(8.6)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)}\left(\operatorname{pr}^{!}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})),\operatorname{act}^{!}(i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\right),

where
\operatorname{pr}^{!}
and
\operatorname{act}^{!}
are the two functors

{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}(S).

8.2.4.

In what follows, we will abuse the notation slightly and omit
S
. So we will simply write

j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})
instead of
\operatorname{pr}^{!}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}))
, and we will write

g\cdot i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}):=\operatorname{act}^{!}(i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

Note also that

g\cdot i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\simeq i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}),

where in the right-hand side
g\cdot-
refers to the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action on
{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}
.

Thus, instead of (
8.5
) we will write

(8.7)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})),

and instead of (
8.6
) we will write

(8.8)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

8.2.5.

Let us come back for a moment to the statement of Proposition
8.1.6
. It implies that for

{\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
and
g
as above, we have a canonical isomorphism

(8.9)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

Hence, by Proposition
8.1.6
, the expression in (
8.8
) is canonically
isomorphic to

(8.10)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(g^{-1}\cdot{\mathbf{d}})),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

8.2.6.

The key assertion behind the proof of Theorem
4.7.3
is the following:

Main Lemma 8.2.7
.

Suppose that the object
{\mathbf{a}}
belongs to
({\mathbf{A}}_{X-x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{X-x_{0}}}
.

(a) If
g
is a point of
17
17

17

In the formula below,
N
is the maximal unipotent subgroup of
G
.

{\mathfrak{L}}(N)_{x_{0}}
, there exists a canonical isomorphism
between the spaces (
8.7
) and (
8.8
).

(b) If
{\mathbf{d}}\in({\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}
, then for any
g\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
,
there exists a natural isomorphism, to be denoted
\alpha_{g,{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}
,
between the spaces (
8.7
) and (
8.8
),
such that for

g_{1}\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
the diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))@>{\alpha_{g_{1}\cdot g,{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}((g_{1}\cdot g)\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\\
&&@V{=}V{}V\\
@V{{\mathbf{d}}\text{ is spherical}}V{\sim}V{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g_{1}\cdot(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})))\\
&&@V{\sim}V{\text{\eqref{e:action of sph}}}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}g_{1}^{-1}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))@>{\alpha_{g,g_{1}^{-1}\cdot{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}g_{1}^{-1}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\end{CD}

commutes.

In what follows, adopting the conventions of Remark Sect.
8.2.4
, we will write the sought-for isomorphism in
Main Lemma
8.2.7
as

(8.11)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\overset{\alpha_{g,{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}{\simeq}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

Remark 8.2.8
.

Let us emphasize the difference between the assertion of Main Lemma
8.2.7
and that of Proposition
8.1.6
:

In (
8.9
), both
{\mathbf{d}}
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
are moved by
g
(while
{\mathbf{a}}
is only required to be
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
-equivariant).

By contrast, in Main Lemma
8.2.7
only
{\mathbf{d}}
(or
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
) is moved by
g
, but
{\mathbf{a}}
is required to be
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
-equivariant.

Remark 8.2.9
.

One can show that when
G
is semi-simple, one can get rid of the condition that

{\mathbf{d}}
be
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
-equivariant. However, when
G
has a non-trivial connected center, the

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
-equivariance condition on
{\mathbf{d}}
is necessary.

8.2.10.

Here is how Main Lemma
8.2.7
will be used:

Corollary 8.2.11
.

Let
{\mathbf{a}}
,
{\mathbf{d}}
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
be as in Lemma
8.2.7
.

(a) We have an isomorphism

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\Bigl(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\Bigr)\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}}_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\right).

(b) For
G
reductive, under the additional assumptions of Lemma
8.2.7
(b), we have

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\Bigl(j_{*}\left({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{x_{0}}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\Bigr)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\left(j_{*}\Bigl({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}({\mathbf{d}})\Bigr),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\right),

where
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}
is as in Sect.
4.6.11
.

Proof.

We prove point (a), as point (b) is similar. By adjunction,

(8.12)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}}_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\right)\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g_{\operatorname{univ}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\right),

where
g_{\operatorname{univ}}
is the tautological
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)
-point of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)
.

Now, Main Lemma
8.2.7
(a) implies that the left-hand side in (
8.12
) identifies with

(8.13)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\right).

Both objects
