j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})\text{ and }i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}\otimes\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))

are the same-named objects in
{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}
, tensored with
\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)}\in\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))
.
Since the latter object is compact, the expression in (
8.13
) is isomorphic to

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\right)\otimes{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))}(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\left(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\right)\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)).

Now, since
N
is unipotent,
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))
is contractible, and hence
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N))\simeq k
.

∎

8.3.
Proof of Main Lemma
8.2.7
: reduction to a particular congruence level

In this subsection we will show that we can assume that both
{\mathbf{d}}
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
are
invariant with respect to some congruence subgroup
K_{n}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

8.3.1.

First, as the category
{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}
is acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\supset{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
, every object can be written as a colimit
of objects invariant with respect to congruence subgroups.

Thus, we can assume that
{\mathbf{d}}
is invariant with respect to some
K_{n}
,
n\geq 1
.

8.3.2.

We now use the fact that
{\mathbf{a}}
is
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{X-x_{0}}
-equivariant, and we apply isomorphism
(
8.4
):

By Proposition
8.1.6
, the category
{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{X\times x_{0}}
is acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and in particular
by
K_{n}
. By assumption, with respect to this action, the object

j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})\in{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{X\times x_{0}}

is
K_{n}
-invariant.

Hence, the left-hand side in (
8.11
) remains unchanged if we replace
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}

by
\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{n}}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})
.

Similarly, the right-hand side in (
8.11
) remains unchanged if we replace

g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
by
\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{n}}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})
.

8.3.3.

Now, for a given point
g
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, let
m\gg 1
be large enough so that

\operatorname{Ad}_{g}(K_{m})\subset K_{n}.

In this case,

\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{n}}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{n}}(g\cdot\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{m}}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

Therefore, the right-hand side in (
8.11
) remains unchanged if we replace

{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
by
\operatorname{Av}_{*}^{K_{m}}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})
.

8.3.4.

Thus, we can assume that
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
is also invariant with respect to some congruence subgroup.

8.4.
Proof of Main Lemma: reduction of point (a) to point (b)

8.4.1.

Given an
S
-point
g
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(N)_{x_{0}}
, we need to establish isomorphism (
8.11
).

By the previous subsection, we can assume that both
{\mathbf{d}}
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
are invariant with respect to
the subgroup
K_{n}(N):=K_{n}\cap{\mathfrak{L}}(N)_{x_{0}}
for some
n\geq 1
.

8.4.2.

Let
\lambda
be a dominant coweight so that

\operatorname{Ad}_{t^{\lambda}}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}})\subset K_{n}(N).

I.e.,

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}\subset\operatorname{Ad}_{t^{-\lambda}}(K_{n}(N)).

Using the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action on
{\mathbf{D}}^{0}_{X\times x_{0}}
, we can identify the two sides of (
8.11
)
with ones, where we replace
{\mathbf{d}}
by
t^{-\lambda}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
by
t^{-\lambda}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
,
and
g
by
\operatorname{Ad}_{t^{-\lambda}}(g)
.

However, now
t^{-\lambda}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}
and
t^{-\lambda}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
are
\operatorname{Ad}_{t^{-\lambda}}(K_{n}(N))
-equivariant,
and hence
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}
-equivariant, by construction.

8.4.3.

This reduces the assertion of point (a) of Lemma
8.2.7
to point (b) (for
G
replaced by
N
).

8.5.
Proof of Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b): the mechanism

In this subsection we will explain the main geometric idea behind the proof of Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b).

We will describe a paradigm in which one obtains an isomorphism (
8.11
).

8.5.1.

Assume that
{\mathbf{d}}
is invariant with respect to a subgroup
K\subset{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
. Note that using the
averaging functor as in Sect.
8.3.2
, we can assume that
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
is also
K
-invariant
18
18

18

In
practice, for the proof of Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b), we will take
K
to be
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
itself.
.

Let
g
be an element of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, i.e., a map
\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}\to G
. Let
g^{\prime}
be a map

(8.14)

g^{\prime}:X\times X-\Delta\to G

with the following properties:

•

For every
x\neq x_{0}
, the restriction of map

g^{\prime}_{x}:=g^{\prime}|_{(X-x)\times x}:X-x\to G

along

\mathcal{D}_{x_{0}}\hookrightarrow X-x
lies in
K
;

•

The restriction of the map

g^{\prime}_{x_{0}}:=g^{\prime}|_{(X-x_{0})\times x_{0}}:X-x_{0}\to G

along
\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}\hookrightarrow X-x_{0}
equals

g
modulo
K
.

We claim that a choice of a map
g^{\prime}
as above gives rise to an isomorphism (
8.11
).

8.5.2.

For any map as in (
8.14
), its Laurent expansion in the first coordinate around the divisor

\Delta\cup(x_{0}\times X)

defines a section of

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{X\times x_{0}}:={\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}|_{X\times x_{0}}

over
X\simeq X\times x_{0}
, and hence acts by a self-equivalence on
{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}
.

We claim that the action of the above element
g^{\prime}
is such that

(8.15)

g^{\prime}\cdot(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}))\simeq j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}})

and

(8.16)

g^{\prime}\cdot(i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\simeq i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}).

This would give rise to an isomorphism (
8.11
).

8.5.3.

To prove (
8.15
), we need to establish the corresponding isomorphism
over
X-x_{0}
.

Note that a map (
8.14
) acts on an object

{\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{X-x_{0}}\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{x_{0}}

via:

•

The Laurent expansion of
g^{\prime}
around the diagonal divisor and the action of
the resulting element of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{X-x_{0}}
on
{\mathbf{a}}
;

•

The Laurent expansion of
g^{\prime}
around
x_{0}\times X
and the resulting action of

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\mathbf{d}}
.

Now, the condition that
{\mathbf{a}}
is
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{X-x_{0}}
-equivariant implies that

g^{\prime}\cdot{\mathbf{a}}\simeq{\mathbf{a}}.

And the condition that the
g^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}\times x}\in K
combined with
the assumption that
{\mathbf{d}}
in
K
-invariant implies that
g^{\prime}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}\simeq{\mathbf{d}}
.

8.5.4.

To prove (
8.16
), we need to show that the action of

g^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}\times x_{0}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)

on
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
produces the same result as
g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
.

However, this follows from the assumption that

g^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{D}^{\times}_{x_{0}}\times x_{0}}=g\,\operatorname{mod}\,K

and the fact that
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
is
K
-invariant.

8.5.5.

Assume now that
g^{\prime}
is in fact a map

X\times X\to G,

i.e., that it is regular around the diagonal.

Unwinding the construction, we obtain that the resulting isomorphism (
8.11
) equals

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:action of sph}}}{\simeq}\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}})),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\overset{{\mathbf{d}}\text{ is spherical}}{\simeq}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})).

8.5.6.

Note also that the isomorphism (
8.11
) is naturally compatible with the group structure on

\operatorname{Maps}(X\times X-\Delta,G)
, i.e., for a pair of points
g^{\prime}_{1},g^{\prime}_{2}\in\operatorname{Maps}(X\times X-\Delta,G)
, the diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))@>{\alpha_{g_{2},{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g_{2}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))\\
@V{\alpha_{g_{1}\cdot g_{2},{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}V{}V@V{}V{\alpha_{g_{1},g_{2}\cdot{\mathbf{d}},{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}}}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}((g_{1}\cdot g_{2})\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}))@>{=}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}(j_{*}({\mathbf{a}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{d}}),i_{*}(g_{1}\cdot(g_{2}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime})))\end{CD}

commutes.

8.6.
Proof of Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b): approximating the loop group

The assertion of the Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b) is étale-local, so in what follows we will assume that the pair
(X,x_{0})
is
({\mathbb{A}}^{1},0)
. We will implement the mechanism from Sect.
8.5

or
K={\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

8.6.1.

We will construct a group ind-scheme (ind-affine, of ind-finite type)

\Gamma
, equipped with a map

\phi:\Gamma\to\operatorname{Maps}(X\times X-\Delta,G),

such that the composite map

(8.17)

\Gamma\overset{\phi}{\to}\operatorname{Maps}(X\times X-\Delta,G)\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}

satisfies:

•

For
\Gamma^{+}:=\Gamma\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}{\times}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}

the resulting map

(8.18)

\Gamma/\Gamma^{+}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}/{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}={\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,x_{0}}

is an isomorphism.

Given such a map, the construction in Sect.
8.5
will imply the assertion of Main Lemma
8.2.7
(b).

8.6.2.

We take

\Gamma:=G(t,t^{-1}):=\mathbf{Maps}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0,G).

We let
\phi
be defined by precomposition with the projection

X\times X-\Delta={\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-\Delta\overset{t_{1},t_{2}\mapsto t_{1}-t_{2}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}-0.

8.6.3.

Note that the map (
8.17
) is the standard map corresponding to
k[t,t^{-1}]\hookrightarrow k(\!(t)\!)
.
Hence,

\Gamma^{+}=\mathbf{Maps}({\mathbb{A}}^{1},G)=G[t].

Hence, the map (
8.18
) is the map

G(t,t^{-1})/G[t]\to G(\!(t)\!)/G[\![t]\!],

which is known to be an isomorphism
19
19

19

This is a consequence of the Beauville-Laszlo theorem,
combined with the fact that every
G
-bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
can be trivialized on
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, étale-locally with
respect to the
scheme of parameters.
.

9.
Proof of Theorem
4.7.3

9.1.
What to we need to show?

9.1.1.

Recall that by Corollary
8.1.8
, the category

(9.1)

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

carries an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

Since the forgetful functor

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{C}}

is conservative and compatible with the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-actions, by Lemma
4.6.4
, it suffices to show that the functor

(9.2)

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

is an equivalence.

9.1.2.

The strategy of the proof will be as follows:

(1)

We will show that the category (
9.1
) is
spherically generated

(see Sect.
4.5.9
for what this means);

(2)

We will show that the functor
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}
on the spherical subcategory
of (
9.1
) (see Sect.
4.6.11
) is conservative.
By Proposition
4.6.12
, this will imply that (
9.2
) is an equality.

9.2.
Functoriality revisted

In this subsection we briefly revisit the set-up of Sect.
2.2
.

9.2.1.

Note that we have a canonical map of factorization algebras in
\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})

\omega_{\phi}:({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi})_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}})\to\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}.

Hence, we obtain a functor

(9.3)

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}})_{x_{0}}\overset{\operatorname{Res}_{\omega_{\phi}}}{\longrightarrow}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi})_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\simeq\\
\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathbf{Res}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\phi}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}))_{x_{0}}\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G^{\prime}})})_{x_{0}}.

Note that the source (resp., target) of the above functor carries a natural action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}
),
see Corollary
8.1.8
.

9.2.2.

Unwinding the constructions, we obtain:

Lemma 9.2.3
.

The functor (
9.3
) intertwines the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}
-action
on the target with the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G^{\prime})_{x_{0}}
-action on the source obtained from the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-action by
precomposing with
\phi
.

9.3.
Proof of spherical generation

9.3.1.

Replacing
{\mathbf{C}}
by the kernel of the right adjoint to (
4.25
), we can assume that

{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}=0
.

We will show that the category (
9.1
) is zero in this case.

9.3.2.

By lemmas
2.2.4
and
9.2.3
, and using the fact that Theorem
3.1.7

has already been proved for the group
T
, we know that the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(T)_{x_{0}}

(and in particular of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}
) has been canonically trivialized.

In particular, the inclusion

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}}}\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)\hookrightarrow\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

is an equality.

9.3.3.

We claim that the inclusion

\operatorname{alm-inv}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}}\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)\hookrightarrow\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

is also an equality.

To prove this, it suffices to show that the functor
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}}_{*}
is conservative on (
9.1
). Let

{\mathbf{c}}\in\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

be an object in the kernel of
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}}}_{*}
. Then by Corollary
8.2.11
(a), we obtain that

(9.4)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\Bigl(j_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})\Bigr)=0,

where:

•

{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}
;

•

{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}={\operatorname{oblv}}_{\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}}({\mathbf{c}})
.

9.3.4.

We claim that (
9.4
) implies that
{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}=0
. Indeed, the structure of factorization
\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
-module on
{\mathbf{c}}

gives rise to a map

j_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})\to i_{*}({\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})[1],

and we claim that if this map is zero, then
{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}
is zero.

Indeed, this follows from the fact that
{\mathbf{c}}
was a
unital
factorization module, and hence
the composition

j_{*}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})\to j_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})\to i_{*}({\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})[1]

is the canonical morphism.

9.3.5.

We now claim that the inclusion

\operatorname{inv}_{K_{1}}\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{alm-inv}_{K_{1}}\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)\hookrightarrow\\
\hookrightarrow\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

is also an equality.

Indeed, this follows from the triangular decomposition

K_{1}=(K_{1}\cap{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N)_{x_{0}})\cdot(K_{1}\cap{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(T)_{x_{0}})\cdot(K_{1}\cap{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(N^{-})_{x_{0}}),

and the fact that the equality holds for each of the factors.

9.3.6.

Thus, the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
on

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}:=\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}

factors through

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\twoheadrightarrow G,

while we have:

•

({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{N}={\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}

•

The action of
T
on
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
is trivialized;

•

({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{G}=0
.

In Sect.
9.4
below we will show that any
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
with the above properties is zero.

Remark 9.3.7
.

Note that if we weakened the second assumption to
\operatorname{alm-inv}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})={\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
, the assertion that

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}=0
would be false: a counterexample is provided by Sect.
4.2.6
.

Remark 9.3.8
.

As we shall see, the first condition (i.e., that
({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})^{N}={\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
) is actually superfluous. So, in fact we are proving the following:

Proposition 9.3.9
.

Let us be a given a categorical representation of
G
, such that its restriction to
T
can be trivialized. Then the initial
categorical representation is almost trivial.

9.4.
The toric localization argument

To simplify the notation, for the duration of this subsection we will perform the notational change,

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow{\mathbf{C}}.

9.4.1.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be a category equipped with an action of
T
. Then the category

{\mathbf{D}}^{T}
is acted on by
\operatorname{Vect}^{T}
.

In particular, the (commutative algebra)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(BT)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}({\mathfrak{t}}^{*}[-2])

maps to the Bernstein center of
{\mathbf{D}}^{T}
.

9.4.2.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}^{T}_{0}\subset{\mathbf{D}}
be the
non-cocomplete
subcategory consisting of objects, on which
some non-zero graded ideal in
\operatorname{Sym}({\mathfrak{t}}^{*}[-2])
acts trivially.

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}
the quotient

{\mathbf{D}}^{T}/{\mathbf{D}}^{T}_{0},

taken in the world of non-cocomplete categories
.

9.4.3.

We consider
{\mathbf{C}}^{N}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
itself as acted on by
T
(note that by
[
BZGO
, Remark 1.2]
, if
{\mathbf{C}}\neq 0
, then
{\mathbf{C}}^{N}\neq 0
).
Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{D}(T\backslash G/B)
. Convolution with
{\mathcal{F}}
can be thought of as a functor

{\mathbf{C}}^{B}=({\mathbf{C}}^{N})^{T}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{T}.

The following assertion is an abstract version of the toric localization principle:

Lemma 9.4.4
.

For
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{B}
, the image of
{\mathcal{F}}\star{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{T}
under

{\mathbf{C}}^{T}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}

is canonically isomorphic to the image of

\underset{w\in W}{\oplus}\,(w\cdot{\mathbf{c}})\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{w},

where:

•

W
denotes the Weyl group;

•

For
w\in W
we denote by the same symbol the corresponding
T
-fixed point in
G/B
;

•

w\cdot{\mathbf{c}}:=\delta_{w}\star{\mathbf{c}}
, where
\delta_{w}
is viewed as an object of
\operatorname{D}(G/B)^{T}=\operatorname{D}(T\backslash G/B)
.

•

{\mathcal{F}}_{w}
is the !-fiber of
{\mathcal{F}}
at
w\in G/B
.

The proof is given in Sect.
9.5
below.

Corollary 9.4.5
.

The image of
{\mathbf{c}}
under
{\mathbf{C}}^{T}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}
is a retract of the image of

\underline{k}_{G/B}\star{\mathbf{c}}.

9.4.6.

Note now that the assumption that
{\mathbf{C}}^{G}=0
implies that the functor

\underline{k}_{G/B}\star(-):{\mathbf{C}}^{B}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{T}

vanishes.

Hence, from Corollary
9.4.5
we obtain that this assumption forces that the inclusion

({\mathbf{C}}^{N})^{T}_{0}\subset({\mathbf{C}}^{N})^{T}={\mathbf{C}}^{B}

is an equality.

9.4.7.

We now use the assumption that the
T
-action on
{\mathbf{C}}
is trivialized. Hence so is the
T
-action on
{\mathbf{C}}^{N}
.
This assumption implies that

({\mathbf{C}}^{N})^{T}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{N}\otimes\operatorname{Vect}^{T},

where the action of
\operatorname{Vect}^{T}
is via the second factor.

Now, for
0\neq{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{N}
, the object

{\mathbf{c}}\otimes k\in{\mathbf{C}}^{N}\otimes\operatorname{Vect}^{T}

does
not
belong to
({\mathbf{C}}^{N})^{T}_{0}
.

∎

9.5.
Proof of Lemma
9.4.4

9.5.1.

It is enough to show that the image of
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}(T\backslash G/B)\simeq\operatorname{D}(G/B)^{T}
under

\operatorname{D}(G/B)^{T}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{D}(G/B)}

is isomorphic to the image of

\underset{w}{\oplus}\,(\delta_{w}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{w}).

We have a canonical map

\underset{w}{\oplus}\,(\delta_{w}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{w})\to{\mathcal{F}},

whose cone has the property that its !-fibers at the points
w
are zero.

Hence, it is enough to show that if
{\mathcal{F}}
has vanishing !-fibers at all the points
w
, then
its projection to
\widetilde{\operatorname{D}(G/B)}
vanishes.

9.5.2.

Using Cousin decomposition, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is the *-extension from an object on
a single Schuber cell
(G/B)_{w}\in G/B
. Furthermore, by assumption, it is the *-extension from
the open subscheme
(G/B)_{w}-w
. Hence, it is enough to show that the inclusion

\operatorname{D}((G/B)_{w}-w)^{T}_{0}\subseteq\operatorname{D}((G/B)_{w}-w)^{T}

is an equality.

We claim that this is the case for any scheme
Y
on which a torus
T
acts without fixed points.

9.5.3.

First, in order to show that the inclusion

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{T}_{0}\subseteq\operatorname{D}(Y)^{T}

is an equality, we can replace the action of the original
T
by the action of any

{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
that maps to
T
.

The fact that
T
has no fixed points on
Y
implies that we can find
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to T

that acts on
Y
with finite stabilizers. We will show that for this copy of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, the action of

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(B{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq k[\eta],\quad\deg(\eta)=2

on
\operatorname{D}(Y)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
factors though a non-zero ideal.

9.5.4.

The action
of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(B{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
on

\operatorname{D}(Y)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\simeq\operatorname{D}(Y/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})

factors via a homomorphism

(9.5)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(B{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}).

Hence, it is enough to show that this homomorphism factors though a non-zero ideal.

9.5.5.

The assumption on the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
Y
implies that
Y/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is a Deligne-Mumford stack.
Hence,
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(Y/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
is finite-dimensional.

Hence, the homomorphism (
9.5
) has a non-trivial kernel.

∎

9.6.
Proof of the conservativity of
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}

9.6.1.

Let

{\mathbf{c}}\in\left(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\right)^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}}

be an object in the kernel of
\operatorname{Av}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}_{!}
.

Then by Corollary
8.2.11
(b), we obtain that

(9.6)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{X\times x_{0}}}\Bigl(j_{*}(\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}),i_{*}({\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}})\Bigr)=0.

9.6.2.

This implies that
{\mathbf{c}}_{x_{0}}=0
by the same argument as in Sect.
9.3.4
.

∎[Theorem
4.7.3
]

10.
An application: integrable Kac-Moody representations

In this section we discuss an application of Theorem
2.1.6
(rather, of its incarnation as
Theorem
3.1.7
). Namely, we establish an equivalence between integrable Kac-Moody
representations and representations of the integrable quotient of the Kac-Moody
chiral algebra.

10.1.
Integrable Kac-Moody representations and the integrable quotient

10.1.1.

We define integrable Kac-Moody representations, following the framework of
[
Ro
, Sect. 7.4]
. Namely,
we let
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
be a factorization line bundle on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}
.

Consider the factorization functor

(10.1)

\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto\Gamma^{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G},{\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}).

We define the
integrable Kac-Moody factorization algebra
in
\operatorname{Vect}
to be

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}:=\Gamma^{\operatorname{IndCoh}}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G},\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}).

Remark 10.1.2
.

Recall that to the datum of
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
there corresponds a discrete invariant, called a level,
and denoted
\kappa
, which is a
W
-invariant
{\mathbb{Z}}
-valued quadratic form on the coweight lattice

\Lambda
of
G
.

It is shown in
[
Ro
, Theorem 7.4.3]
that if the restriction of
\kappa
to one of the simple factors of

G
is negative-definite, then
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}=0
.

If the restriction of
\kappa
to all simple factors of

G
is non-negative definite, then

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}:={\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]\in\operatorname{D}(X)

lives in single cohomological degree
0
, so can be regarded as a classical
chiral algebra.

In the latter case, if
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected,
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}
is what is
usually called
the integrable quotient
of the Kac-Moody chiral algebra at level
\kappa
, denoted

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}:={\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}|_{X}[-1]

(per our conventions, we reserve the symbol
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}
for the corresponding factorization algebra).

In the opposite case, namely, when
G=T
is a torus,
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}
is the lattice chiral algebra.

10.1.3.

Recall that the pullback of

{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}}
along the unit section

\operatorname{unit}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}:{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}

is canonically trivialized (as a factorization line bundle over
\operatorname{pt}
).

We quote the following result (see
[
Zhao
, Corollary C]
):

Theorem 10.1.4
.

The pullback
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
along the projection

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}

carries a uniquely defined multiplicative structure, compatible with the
trivialization of its restriction to
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
, given by the trivialization of
\operatorname{unit}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}^{*}({\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}})
.

10.1.5.

Consider the resulting central extension of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
; denote it by

1\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\widehat{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{\kappa,x_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to 1.

The datum of such a central extension is equivalent to that of a
weak
action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
\operatorname{Vect}
.
Denote the resulting object
20
20

20

We refer the reader to
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.19]
, where weak actions of loop groups on categories are discussed.

of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}
by
\operatorname{Vect}_{\kappa}
.

The category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
of integrable
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
-representations at level
\kappa
is by definition

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect}_{\kappa}).

Remark 10.1.6
.

The category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
is well-defined for
any

\kappa
. However, when
\kappa
is negative-definite,
the natural forgetful functor

(10.2)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\to\operatorname{Vect}

is zero
21
21

21

However, the forgetful functor
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\to\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)
is conservative. There is no contradiction here, since the
forgetful functor
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)\to\operatorname{Vect}
is not conservative.
.

Indeed, for an object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
, the
n
-th cohomology of its image under (
10.2
)
would be an
integrable representation
of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
at level
\kappa
in the classical sense. But for
\kappa
negative-definite, there
are non-zero such, because the sign of
\kappa
makes the dominance condition on the highest weight impossible
to satisfy.

Yet, the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
is non-zero. In fact, for any
\kappa
, we
have a natural identification

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa^{\prime})\simeq({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa))^{\vee},\quad\kappa^{\prime}=-\kappa-\kappa_{\operatorname{Killing}}.

10.1.7.

In this section we will prove:

Theorem 10.1.8
.

Suppose that the level
\kappa
is non-negative definite on each simple factor.
Then there is a canonical equivalence

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\simeq{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

commuting with the tautological forgetful functors of both sides to
\operatorname{Vect}
.

The theorem will be proved in Sects.
10.3
-
10.5
.

Remark 10.1.9
.

The statement of Theorem
10.1.8
is false for
\kappa
negative-definite:

Indeed, according
to Remark
10.1.2
, in this case
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}=0
, so the right-hand side
in Theorem
10.1.8
is zero. However, according to Remark
10.1.6
, the left-hand side is non-zero.

Remark 10.1.10
.

The statement of Theorem
10.1.8
is known at the level of abelian categories, in two cases:
either when
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected or when
G
is a torus.

When
G
is semi-simple and simply-connected, this is the statement that for a module
{\mathcal{M}}
over the affine Kac-Moody Lie algebra
at level
\kappa
,
the following two conditions are equivalent:

(i) The action of the affine Kac-Moody Lie algebra on
{\mathcal{M}}
integrates to an action of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{\kappa,x_{0}}
;

(ii) When we view
{\mathcal{M}}
as a chiral module over
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}
(i.e., a
factorization module over
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}
at
x_{0}
), the action factors through the integrable quotient
chiral module over

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}.

When
G=T
is a torus, the statement of Theorem
10.1.8
at the abelian level is equivalent to that
of
[
BD1
, Theorem 3.10.14]
.

10.1.11.

Under the assumption on the level
\kappa
, the category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
is known to be semi-simple
(e.g., this can be proved by the same method
as in
[
Ro
, Appendix D]
). Hence, from Theorem
10.1.8
, we obtain:

Corollary 10.1.12
.

The category
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
is semi-simple.

Remark 10.1.13
.

The statement of Corollary
10.1.12
is known at the abelian level. The innovation here
is that it continues to hold at the derived level, i.e., that there are no higher Exts between
objects in the heart.

One may (somewhat recklessly) conjecture that the same holds for any rational VOA.

10.2.
Factorization categories with an action of the loop group

Recall the setting of Sects.
8.1.1
-
8.1.3
.

Thus, let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a factorization algebra category, equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}}
,
compatible with the factorization structure.

In particular,
{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}
is a category acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and we can form

(10.3)

({\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

In this subsection we will discuss an additional feature of the above construction
in the unital setting.

10.2.1.

Let us assume that
{\mathbf{A}}
is unital and that its unit object
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
is
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)
-equivariant.
In this case, the action on the unit gives rise to a unital factorization functor

\Phi:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to{\mathbf{A}}.

In particular, we can form the object

(10.4)

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

10.2.2.

We now add the following technical condition: we assume that
{\mathbf{A}}
is
tight
, i.e., the functor of the insertion of the unit

\operatorname{ins.unit}_{\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}}:{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{2}},\quad\underline{x}_{1}\subseteq\underline{x}_{2}

admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint (see
[
GLC2
, Sect. C.16.1]
).

In particular, this implies that for any
\underline{x}
, the object
({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{x}}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}
is compact. We impose
an even stronger condition, namely, that
({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{x}}
is compact as an object of
({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}
.

Proposition-Construction 10.2.3
.

Under the above circumstances, the objects (
10.3
) and (
10.4
)
are canonically isomorphic.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of this proposition.

10.2.4.

We first construct a map in one direction, (
10.3
)
\to
(
10.4
). By the definition
of factorization restriction, the datum of such a map is equivalent to the datum of a functor

(10.5)

\Phi_{m}:({\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}_{\underline{x}}\simeq{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}},\quad\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}

compatible with factorization via
\Phi
.

10.2.5.

Recall the group ind-scheme
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}
, see
(
8.3
). It projects onto
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and let us denote by
\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}}

the kernel. In particular, we have a short exact sequence

(10.6)

1\to\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\to 1.

(Note that
\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}
identifies also with the kernel of

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)_{x_{0}}
.)

We can identify

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}}\simeq{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}/\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}},

where the
right
action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}}
comes
from the short exact sequence (
10.6
).

10.2.6.

Consider the functor

(10.7)

\operatorname{ins.unit}_{\{x_{0}\}\subseteq\underline{x}}:{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}.

Both categories are acted on by
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
, and the functor (
10.7
) is compatible with these actions.
Moreover, (
10.7
) factors via a functor

(10.8)

\operatorname{ins.unit}_{\{x_{0}\}\subseteq\underline{x}}:{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}\to({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}.

The functor (
10.8
) is also compatible with the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
-actions, where the action
on the right-hand side is via the short exact sequence (
10.6
).

10.2.7.

For future reference we note that it follows from the assumptions on
{\mathbf{A}}
in Sect.
10.2.2
that the functor (
10.8
)
admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint.

10.2.8.

Recall that

({\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}_{\underline{x}}=\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}.

Thus, from (
10.8
) we obtain a functor

(10.9)

({\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}_{\underline{x}}\to\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}^{\operatorname{level}^{\infty}_{x_{0}}}_{G,\underline{x}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}}{\otimes}({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}

10.2.9.

We rewrite the right-hand side in (
10.9
) as

(10.10)

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}/\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}{\otimes}({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}{\otimes}({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}.

Now, the action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}
on
{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}
gives rise to a functor

(10.11)

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}{\otimes}({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}.

Composing (
10.9
), (
10.10
) and (
10.11
), we obtain the sought-for functor
(
10.5
).

The compatibility with factorization against
\Phi
follows from the construction.

10.2.10.

We now show that the resulting functor

(10.12)

({\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})

is an equivalence.

In order to do se, we apply Proposition
C.10.20
. We need to show:

•

The functor (
10.12
) induces an equivalence between the fibers at
x_{0}
;

•

The functor
\Phi
admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint;

•

The functor
\Phi_{m}
admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint.

The fact that the first condition is satisfied is automatic. Indeed, at the level of fibers at
x_{0}
, the functor
(
10.12
) is the identity functor
{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{x_{0}}
.

The fact that
\Phi
admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint follows from the fact that
({\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{x}}

is compact as an object of
{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}_{\underline{x}}
, combined with the ind-properness of
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\underline{x}}
.

Finally, let us show that
\Phi
admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint. For that it is sufficient show that
both functors (
10.9
) and (
10.11
) admit a colimit-preserving right adjoints.

10.2.11.

The fact that (
10.9
) admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint follows from the corresponding fact for
(
10.8
), see Sect.
10.2.7
.

For the functor (
10.11
), we write it as a composition

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\otimes({\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}})^{\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{+}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\to\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\otimes{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}},

and it suffices to show that the second arrow admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint.

Using the
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}
-action on
{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}
, we rewrite this arrow as

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\otimes{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}},

induced by the functor

\operatorname{D}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\operatorname{mer}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{reg}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}\simeq\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\underline{x}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}})\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\underline{x}}/\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

This implies the desired assertion since
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,\underline{x}}
is ind-proper and
\operatorname{Hecke}_{x_{0}}
is pseudo-proper.

∎[Proposition
10.2.3
]

10.3.
Proof of Theorem
10.1.8

10.3.1.

We consider the factoriztion category
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
of Kac-Moody representations at level
\kappa
,
see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.22]
. It is naturally equipped with a (strong) action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)

at level

\kappa
.

Now, the datum of the central extension
\widehat{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)}_{\kappa}
allows us to modify the weak action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)

on
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
, so that the resulting strong action occurs at level
0
. The resulting object

\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

has the universal property that

(10.13)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}}({\mathbf{C}},\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{\underline{x}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}\operatorname{-weak}}({\mathbf{C}},\operatorname{Vect}_{\kappa}),\quad{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{\underline{x}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},\quad\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}},

see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.12]
.

10.3.2.

Taking
{\mathbf{C}}
in (
10.13
) to be
\operatorname{Vect}
, we obtain

(10.14)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa):=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}\operatorname{-weak}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect}_{\kappa})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}}(\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{x_{0}}).

10.3.3.

Consider the object

(10.15)

\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}\in\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

We now perform the crucial step in the proof of Theorem
10.1.8
. Namely, we combine (
10.14
) with Theorem
3.1.7
, and obtain an equivalence

(10.16)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\simeq\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}.

The equivalence (
10.16
) commutes with the forgetful functors to
\operatorname{Vect}
, where in the
right-hand side, the corresponding functor is

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{x_{0}}\overset{{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect},

where the second arrow is the tautological forgetful functor.

10.3.4.

Note now that
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
, viewed as a factorization category equipped with an action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)
, fits into the paradigm of
Sect.
10.2
.

The corresponding factorization functor

\Phi:\operatorname{D}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})\to\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)

is the functor of
{\mathcal{L}}_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
-twisted IndCoh sections, to be denoted
\Gamma^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\kappa}
.
I.e., its composition with the forgetful functor

(10.17)

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}:\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)\to\operatorname{Vect}

is the functor (
10.1
), to be denoted
\Gamma_{\kappa}
.

Thus, applying Proposition
10.2.3
, we obtain that the object (
10.15
) identifies with

\mathbf{Res}_{\Gamma^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\kappa}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}}).

10.3.5.

Let
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}
be the factorization algebra in
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
equal to the image of

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}}
under the functor
\Gamma^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\kappa}
.

Applying (
1.9
), we rewrite

\omega_{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G,{\operatorname{Ran}}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\left(\mathbf{Res}_{\Gamma^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\kappa}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})\right)_{x_{0}}\simeq\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}}})_{x_{0}}.

10.3.6.

Thus, combining, we obtain an equivalence

(10.18)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\simeq\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}.

(see Sect.
1.5.5
for the notation), which commutes with the natural forgetful functors of both
sides to
\operatorname{Vect}
.

Remark 10.3.7
.

Note that the equivalence (
10.18
) did not use the assumption that
\kappa
is non-negative definite.

10.3.8.

By construction

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}\simeq{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}(\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}).

Hence, in order to prove Theorem
10.1.8
, it remains to show the following:

Proposition 10.3.9
.

The functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{\operatorname{KM}}:\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

induced by the factorization functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
, is an equivalence.

∎[Theorem
10.1.8
]

Remark 10.3.10
.

As we will see, Proposition
10.3.9
would be almost tautological, if not not for some
homological algebra “issues” (the idea is that the category
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
is “almost” the same
as
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
).

Yet, this issues become fatal when
\kappa
is negative-definite.
(Indeed, as was remarked above, the equivalence (
10.18
) holds for any
\kappa
.)

10.4.
Proof of Proposition
10.3.9
: reduction to the bounded below category

10.4.1.

Note that the chiral algebra
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}
is concentrated in non-positive cohomological
degrees
22
22

22

In fact, according to Remark
10.1.2
, it is actually concentrated in degree
0
,
a fact that will be used later.
. Hence, the category

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
acquires a t-structure,
characterized by the property that the forgetful functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}}:{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

(which is by definition conservative) is t-exact. Moreover,
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
is left-complete in its t-structure,
see
[
GLC2
, Proposition B.9.18]
.

10.4.2.

Note that the factorization category
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
carries a t-structure in the sense of
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.11]

(see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.14.22]
).

Since the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
is t-exact and conservative
on the bounded below category
, we obtain
that the object

\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]\in\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{X}

is connective.

Hence, by
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.16]
, the category
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
also acquires
a t-structure, characterized by the property that the forgetful functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}}:\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{x_{0}}

(which is by definition conservative) is t-exact.

Remark 10.4.3
.

For future reference, we note that the object
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]

lies in the heart of the t-structure of
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{X}
.

Let us consider
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]
as an object of
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)_{X}
, where

\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)=\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}(G)}.

Recall that the level was assumed
non-negative definite

(on every simple factor of
{\mathfrak{g}}
). We claim that in this case, the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
, restricted to
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)_{x_{0}}
,
is actually conservative
23
23

23

This observation is due to G. Dhillon.
.

Indeed, in this case, the compact generators of
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)_{x_{0}}
have a finite cohomological dimension
(this can be seen, e.g., from the Kashiwara-Tanisaki localization); hence
\operatorname{KL}(G,\kappa)_{x_{0}}
has no non-zero
infinitely connective objects.

Hence, the fact that
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]={\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,ch}}_{G,\kappa}
lies in the heart of
the t-structure implies that the same is true for
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]
.

10.4.4.

In particular, we obtain that the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
, appearing in Proposition
10.3.9
, is t-exact
(since its composition with
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}}
, which is t-exact and conservative, is t-exact).

Recall that the source category in Proposition
10.3.9
, being equivalent to
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
,
is semi-simple (see Sect.
10.1.11
). Moreover, the same argument shows that its irreducible objects are bounded below
(in fact, that they lie in the heart of the t-structure).

Since the target category is left-complete in its t-structure, we obtain that in order to prove that
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
is an equivalence,
it suffices to show that it induces an equivalence on the corresponding
bounded below categories.

10.5.
Proof of Proposition
10.3.9
: the bounded below part

10.5.1.

Let
\operatorname{Vac}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}
denote the factorization unit in
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
. Denote

{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}:={\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}(\operatorname{Vac}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}).

This is the usual factorization algebra attached to
{\mathfrak{g}}
at level
\kappa
.

10.5.2.

The unit for
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}
, which is a map

\operatorname{Vac}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}\to\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa},

induces a homomorphism

(10.19)

{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}\to{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}

as unital factorization algebras in
\operatorname{Vect}
.

Let us regard
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
as a unital
lax factorization category
, see
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.12]
.
The homomorphism (
10.19
) allows us to upgrade
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}
to a unital factorization algebra in

{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
(see
[
GLC2
, Setct. C.11.18]
); when viewed as such we will denote it by
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}
.

The forgetful factorization functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}}:{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

induces a functor

(10.20)

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

to be denoted
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}}
.

According to
[
GLC2
, Lemma C.11.19]
, the functor (
10.20
) is an equivalence.

10.5.3.

The functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
induces a factorization functor

(10.21)

\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)\simeq\operatorname{Vac}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa))\to{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}},

to be denoted
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
.

We have

{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}({\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{int}}_{G,\kappa})\simeq{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}

as factorization algebras in
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
.

In particular,
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
induces a functor

(10.22)

\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},

to be denoted
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
.

Unwinding, we obtain that the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
identifies with the composition

{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}}\circ{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}.

10.5.4.

By
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.15]
, the lax factorization category
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}

carries a t-structure in the sense of
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.11]
. It is characterized by the property
that the (conservative) forgetful functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}}
is t-exact.

Since
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}
is connective, by
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.16]
, we obtain that the category

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
acquires a t-structure, characterized by the property
that the (conservative) forgetful functor

{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{Vect}

is t-exact.

In particular, we obtain that the equivalence
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}}
of (
10.20
)
t-exact.

10.5.5.

Hence, in order to prove Proposition
10.3.9
, it remains to show that
the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
of (
10.22
) induces an equivalence on the bounded below subcategories
of the two sides.

Recall (see Remark
10.4.3
) that the object
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]
lies in the heart of the t-structure
24
24

24

It is here
that we crucially use the assumption that
\kappa
is non-negative definite.
. Hence, its values over all powers of
X
are bounded below. Hence, for an eventually coconnective object in
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)_{x_{0}}
, the morphisms
that define on it a structure of object of
\operatorname{Vac}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}
take place in the bounded
below subcategories of values
\operatorname{KM}({\mathfrak{g}},\kappa)
on powers of
X
.

The same is true for
{\mathbb{V}}^{\operatorname{Int}}_{G,\kappa}|_{X}[-1]
and
{\mathbb{V}}_{{\mathfrak{g}},\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
.

Therefore, in order to prove that
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{Int,enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
induces an equivalence on the bounded below subcategories,
it suffices to show that the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{\operatorname{KM}}
of (
10.21
) induces an equivalence
between the bounded below subcategories of the two sides (evaluated on powers of
X
).

However, the latter is the
assertion of
[
GLC2
, Lemma 4.2.3(a)]
.

∎[Proposition
10.3.9
]

10.6.
An addendum: failure of the coherent version of Theorem
2.1.6

10.6.1.

Parallel to the setting of Sect.
2.1
, one can consider:

•

The category
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}

of DG categories, equipped with a weak action of
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
;

•

The factorization category

\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
;

•

The functor

(10.23)

{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}\to\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}},\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})}.

10.6.2.

However, we claim that, unlike Theorem
2.1.6
, the functor (
10.23
) fails to be fully faithful.
Namely, as we shall presently explain, the functor

(10.24)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\to\\
\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}){\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{x_{0}}}(({\mathbf{C}}_{1})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})},({\mathbf{C}}_{2})^{\operatorname{fact}_{x_{0}},\operatorname{IndCoh}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})})

fails to be an equivalence.

Namely, the right-hand side admits a natural conservative functor to
\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})
, whose composition with the functor
in (
10.24
) is the natural forgetful functor

(10.25)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}^{\operatorname{weak}}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\to\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}).

However, we claim that we can find
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
so that the functor (
10.25
) fails to be conservative.

10.6.3.

Namely, we take
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}=\operatorname{Vect}
and
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}=\operatorname{Vect}_{\kappa}
, where
\kappa
is negative-definite. The left-hand side
in (
10.25
) is
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)
, and (
10.25
) is the natural forgetful functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}},\kappa)\to\operatorname{Vect}.

However, the above functor is zero for negative-definite (see Remark
10.1.6
).

Appendix A
D-modules in infinite type

In this section we (re)collect some facts pertaining to the extension of the theory of D-modules
to algebro-geometric objects of infinite type. The goal is to make sense of the category of D-modules
on the loop group
{\mathfrak{L}}(G)_{x_{0}}
.

A.1.
The case of affine schemes

In this subsection we develop the theory of D-modules on affine schemes (not necessarily of finite type).
We will mostly follow
[
GLC2
, Sect. A.4-A.5]
.

A.1.1.

Let
S
be a scheme (not necessarily of finite type). Set

(A.1)

\operatorname{D}^{!}(S):=\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0}),

where:

•

The colimit is taken over the (opposite of the) category of affine schemes of finite type
that receive a map from
S
;

•

The transition functors are given by !-pullback.

Equivalently, for a fixed presentation

(A.2)

S\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,S_{0,\alpha},

where
S_{0,\alpha}
are affine schemes of finite type, we have

(A.3)

\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0,\alpha}).

For an arbitrary
S
, there is no reason for
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)
defined in the above way to be
compactly generated or dualizable.

A.1.2.

We shall say that an object
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)
is
ind-holonomic
it lies in the essential image
of

\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(S_{0,\alpha})\to\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0,\alpha})=\operatorname{D}^{!}(S).

A.1.3.

We set

\operatorname{D}_{*}(S):=\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}^{!}(S),\operatorname{Vect}).

Using Verdier duality, we obtain:

\operatorname{D}_{*}(S)\simeq\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0}),

where the limit is taken with respect to the *-pushforward functors.

In terms of the presentation (
A.2
), we have

\operatorname{D}_{*}(S)\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0,\alpha}).

A.1.4.

Since the transition functors in (
A.1
) are symmetric monoidal, the category
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)

carries a natural symmetric monoidal structure. Its unit object, denoted
\omega_{S}
, is the image
of
\omega_{S_{0}}
for any
S\to S_{0}
.

Note that we have a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(S):=\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(S_{0})\simeq\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{D}(S_{0})}(\omega_{S_{0}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)}(\omega_{S}).

In addition, we have a natural action of
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)
on
\operatorname{D}_{*}(S)
.

The category
\operatorname{D}_{*}(S)
has a distinguished object, denoted
\underline{k}_{S}
; it corresponds to the compatible
family of functors

\operatorname{D}(S_{0})\to\operatorname{Vect},\quad S\to S_{0}

equal to

\underset{S\to S^{\prime}_{0}\overset{f}{\to}S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}(\omega_{S^{\prime}_{0}},f^{!}({\mathcal{F}})).

Remark A.1.5
.

One can describe the object
\underline{k}_{S}
more explicitly as follows: let

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}_{*}(S)\subset\operatorname{D}_{*}(S)

be the full subcategory equal to

\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(S_{0})\subset\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}(S_{0}),

where the limits are taken with respect to the *-pushforward functors.

Since on the holonomic category, the *-pushforward admit left adjoints, we can rewrite
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}_{*}(S)
also as

\underset{S\to S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(S_{0}),

where the colimit is taken with respect to the *-pullbacks.

In terms of the latter presentation,
\underline{k}_{S}
equals the image of
\underline{k}_{S_{0}}

for some/any
S_{0}
.

A.1.6.

For a map
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
between affine schemes, we have a tautologically defined functor

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{1}).

Dually, we have a functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{1})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{2}).

A.1.7.

Assume now that
f
is of finite presentation. In this case we can define a functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{1})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{2}),

which satisfies base change against !-pullbacks.

Dually, we can define

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{2})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{1}),

which satisfies base change against *-pushforwards. When
f
is étale, this functor sends
\underline{k}_{S_{2}}\mapsto\underline{k}_{S_{1}}
.

A.1.8.

Assume now that
f
is a closed embedding of finite presentation. In this case the functors

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{1})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}^{!}(S_{2}):f^{!}

and

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{1})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}_{*}(S_{2}):f^{!}

form adjoint pairs.

A.2.
Extension to prestacks–the !-version

A.2.1.

The functoriality of
\operatorname{D}^{!}(-)
on affine schemes with respect to !-pullbacks allows us to extend the functor

\operatorname{D}(-)^{!}:(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{DGCat}

to arbitrary prestacks by the
procedure of right Kan extension
.

Explicitly, for
{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{PreStk}
, we have

(A.4)

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})=\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{!}(S),

where:

•

The limit is taken over the (opposite of the) category of affine schemes mapping to
{\mathcal{Y}}
;

•

The transition functors are given by !-pullback.

A.2.2.

We shall say that an object of
\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
is
ind-holonomic
if its value on each
S\to{\mathcal{Y}}

belongs to the ind-holonomic subcategory (see Sect.
A.1.2
).

A.2.3.

Since the transition functors in (
A.4
) are symmetric monoidal, the category

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
acquires a symmetric monoidal structure.

Its unit is the object
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
, whose value on every
S\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is

\omega_{S}\in\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)
.

A.2.4.

By Sect.
A.1.7
, if
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is map between prestacks that is affine and of finite presentation,
we have a well-defined functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

that satisfies base change against !-pullbacks.

Assume now that
f
is a closed embedding of finite presentation, In this the functors

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}):f^{!}

are an adjoint pair.

A.2.5.

Let
f
again be arbitrary. The functor
f^{!}
has a partially defined left adjoint, to be denoted
f_{!}
.

In particular, we can consider the partially defined functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Vect},

left adjoint to

k\mapsto\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}.

Lemma A.2.6
.

The functor
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},-)
is defined on ind-holonomic objects.

Proof.

We first consider the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}=S
is an affine scheme. Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)
be obtained
as
g^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{0})
for
g:S\to S_{0}
, where
S_{0}
is an affine scheme of finite type and

{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{hol}}(S_{0})
.

Then the sought-for value of
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(S,{\mathcal{F}})
is given by

\underset{S\to S^{\prime}_{0}\overset{f}{\to}S_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(S^{\prime}_{0},f^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{0})).

Let now
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a general prestack and
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
be ind-holonomic. Then it is easy to see that

\underset{f:S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(S,f^{!}({\mathcal{F}}))

provides the value of the sought-for left adjoint.

∎

A.2.7.

We will denote

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}({\mathcal{Y}},\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}).

Note that the dual of
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})
identifies with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{\operatorname{D}^{!}(S)}(\omega_{S})\simeq\underset{S\to{\mathcal{Y}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(S).

A.3.
The case of (non-affine) schemes

In this subsection we study/define the categories
\operatorname{D}^{!}(-)
and
\operatorname{D}_{*}(-)
on
schemes
.

A.3.1.

Let now
Y
be a (not necessarily affine) scheme, assumed quasi-compact and separated. Let
S\to Y
be a Zariski cover, where
S
is affine,
and let
S^{\bullet}
be its Cech nerve. Consider
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S^{\bullet})
as a cosimplicial category under !-pullbacks.

A standard argument shows that the restriction functor

\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y)\to\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{D}^{!}(S^{\bullet}))

is an equivalence.

In addition, since the morphism in
S^{\bullet}
are of finite presentation, we can view
\operatorname{D}^{!}(S^{\bullet})
as a
simplicial

category under *-pushforwards. The functor of *-pushforward gives rise to a functor

(A.5)

|\operatorname{D}^{!}(S^{\bullet})|\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y),

and another standard argument shows that (
A.5
) is also an equivalence.

A.3.2.

We continue to assume that
Y
is a scheme. Note that we can consider
\operatorname{D}_{*}(S^{\bullet})
as simplicial
(resp., cosimplicial) category via *-pushforwards (resp., !-pullbacks).

Set

\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y):=|(\operatorname{D}_{*}(S^{\bullet})|.

A standard argument shows that the restriction functor

(A.6)

\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)\to\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{D}_{*}(S^{\bullet})),

given by !-pullback, is an equivalence.

The category
\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)
contains a canonically defined object, denoted
\underline{k}_{Y}
, whose value on the terms of
\operatorname{D}_{*}(S^{\bullet})

is
\underline{k}_{S^{\bullet}}
(see the last sentence in Sect.
A.1.7
).

From the equivalences (
A.5
) and (
A.6
), we obtain that we have a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y),\operatorname{Vect})\simeq\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y),

One shows that the above constructions is canonically independent of the choice of the cover
S\to Y
.

A.3.3.

For a map
f:Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
we have a naturally defined functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{1})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{2}).

This functor can be also thought of as obtained from

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{2})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{1})

by duality.

A.3.4.

We have a natural action of
\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y)
on
\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)
.

We shall call an object

\omega_{Y}^{\operatorname{fake},*}\in\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)

a
fake
dualizing sheaf, if the functor

\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y)\to\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}\omega_{Y}^{\operatorname{fake},*}

is an equivalence.

A.3.5.

Let
Y
be written

(A.7)

Y\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,Y_{\alpha},

where the transition maps

Y_{\alpha}\overset{f_{\alpha,\beta}}{\to}Y_{\beta}

are affine.

In this case, it is easy to see that the functor

(A.8)

\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y),

defined by !-pullback, is an equivalence.

Similarly, the functor

(A.9)

\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)\to\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{\alpha}),

defined by *-pushforward, is an equivalence.

A.3.6.

Let
f:Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
be a map of finite presentation between schemes.

Using the equivalence (
A.8
), we construct
a functor

(A.10)

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{1})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{2}),

which satisfies base change against
!
-pullbacks.

This construction allows us to extend the definition of *-pushforward
on
\operatorname{D}^{!}(-)
for schematic maps between prestacks.

Similarly, we construct a functor

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{*}(Y_{2})\to\operatorname{D}^{*}(Y_{1}),

which can also be thought of as obtained from (
A.10
)
by duality.

When
f
is a closed embedding, the functors

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{1})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{2}):f^{!}

form an adjoint pair.

A.3.7.

We shall say that
Y
is
placid
if there exists a presentation (
A.7
), where:

•

The schemes
Y_{\alpha}
are of finite type;

•

The maps
f_{\alpha,\beta}
are smooth,

In this case, the transition functors in (
A.8
) preserve compactness, so
\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y)

is compactly generated, and in particular, dualizable. Hence, in this case
\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)
is also compactly
generated and

\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)\simeq(\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y))^{\vee}.

For a presentation (
A.7
) as above, we can rewrite
\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)
as

(A.11)

\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}(Y_{\alpha}),

where the limit is taken with respect to the *-pullback functors (they are defined thanks to the smoothness
assumption).

In terms of this presentation, the object
\underline{k}_{Y}
equals to the image of
\underline{k}_{Y_{\alpha}}
for any
\alpha
.

A.3.8.

Suppose that
Y
is placid, and assume that in the presentation (
A.7
), the transition
maps
f_{\alpha,\beta}
are equidimensional.

A dimension theory for such a presentation is an assignment

\alpha\mapsto d_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{Z}}

such that for a pair of indices and an arrow
\alpha\to\beta
in the category of indices,
we have

d_{\alpha}-d_{\beta}=\operatorname{dim.rel.}(f_{\alpha,\beta}).

Note that a choice of a dimension theory gives rise to a fake dualizing object
\omega_{Y}^{\operatorname{fake},*}\in\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y)
. Namely,
in terms of (
A.11
),
\omega_{Y}^{\operatorname{fake},*}
is the image of
\omega_{Y_{\alpha}}[-2d_{\alpha}]
for
some/any
\alpha
.

A.3.9.

Note that if the schemes
Y_{\alpha}
are smooth and connected,
we have a distinguished dimension theory, given by
d_{\alpha}=\dim(Y_{\alpha})
. In this case
\omega_{Y}^{\operatorname{fake},*}=\underline{k}_{Y}
.

A.4.
The case of ind-schemes

A.4.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an ind-scheme. Write

(A.12)

{\mathcal{Y}}=\underset{i}{``\operatorname{colim}"}\,Y_{i},

where
Y_{i}
are schemes, and the transition maps
Y_{i}\overset{f_{i,j}}{\to}Y_{j}
are closed embeddings, and
the index category is filtered.

Recall that the category
\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
is a priori defined, see Sect.
A.2.1
. Note, however, that the functor

(A.13)

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{i}),

given by !-pullback is an equivalence, where the limit is formed also using the !-pullback functors.

A.4.2.

We define

(A.14)

\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}_{*}(Y_{i}),

where the colimit is taken with respect to *-pushforwards.

It is clear that the definition does not depend on the presentation of
{\mathcal{Y}}
as in Sect.
A.12
.

A.4.3.

For a map between ind-schemes

f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},

we obtain a well-defined functor

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

In particular, we have a well-defined functor

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}({\mathcal{Y}},-):\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{pt})=\operatorname{Vect}.

Suppose now that
f
is schematic of finite presentation. In this case we also obtain a functor

f^{!}:\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}).

When
f
is a closed embedding, the functors

f_{*}:\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{D}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}):f^{!}

form an adjoint pair.

A.4.4.

We have a naturally defined action of
\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})
on
\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

We shall call an object

\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{fake},*}\in\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}})

a
fake
dualizing sheaf, if the functor

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{D}_{*}({\mathcal{Y}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{fake},*}

is an equivalence.

A.4.5.

Following
[
BD2
, Sect. 7]
, we shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
reasonable
if it admits a presentation (
A.12
), where the transition maps

f_{i,j}
are of finite presentation.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is reasonable, and
Z
is a closed subscheme of
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we shall say that
Z
is
reasonable

if for some/any
i
such that
Z\subset Y_{i}
, this closed embedding is of finite presentation.

Reasonable subschemes of
{\mathcal{Y}}
form a filtered category. A presentation of
{\mathcal{Y}}
as in (
A.12
)
with
Y_{i}
reasonable will be called a
reasonable presentation
.

A.4.6.

Let (
A.12
) be a reasonable presentation. In this case the functors

f_{i,j}^{!}:\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{j})\to\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{i})

admit left adjoints.

Hence, using the equivalence (
A.13
), we can rewrite

(A.15)

\operatorname{D}^{!}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{!}(Y_{i}),

where the colimit is taken with respect to *-pushforwards.

Similarly, in the formation of the colimit in (
A.14
), the transition functors
(f_{i,j})_{*}

admit right adjoints. Hence, we can rewrite

(A.16)

\operatorname{D}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{D}^{*}(Y_{i}),

where the limit is formed using the !-pullback functors.
