{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
is the functor corresponding to the coCartesian fibration

\big(\widetilde{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}\big)_{\underline{x}_{0}/}\to(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}})^{\operatorname{op}},

where the source is the slice (a.k.a. comma) category of arrows out of
\underline{x}_{0}
.

C.6.3.

Recall
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
is an abelian monoid object in
\operatorname{CatPreStk}
, with addition morphism given by
(\underline{x},\underline{y})\mapsto\underline{x}\cup\underline{y}
. It is clear that
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
is a
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-module object in
\operatorname{CatPreStk}
, with the action morphism given by the same formula

\operatorname{union}:\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;(\underline{x},\underline{y})\mapsto\underline{x}\cup\underline{y}.

This morphism is well-defined because if
\underline{y}
contains (the image of)
\underline{x}_{0}
, so does
\underline{x}\cup\underline{y}
.

As in the non-marked case, for any
marked
finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{*\}
, we define the subfunctor

(C.10)

\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\subseteq\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

that contains those
(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{y})
such that any pair of graphs of
\underline{x}_{i}
and
\underline{y}
is disjoint.

C.6.4.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category. Recall its underlying crystal of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
has a multiplicative structure over the disjoint loci.

A
unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category
{\mathbf{C}}
at
\underline{x}_{0}
is a crystal of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
over
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
equipped with a
multiplicative
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
-module structure over the disjoint loci, with respect to the
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-module structure on
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
.

In other words, for any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
, we have an isomorphism:

(C.11)

\operatorname{act}_{I}:\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}},

and a homotopy-coherent datum of compatibility with (
C.2
). Here

\operatorname{union}_{I}:\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{Ran}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

is the map
(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{y})\mapsto(\cup\underline{x}_{i})\cup\underline{y}
, and
(-)|_{\operatorname{disj}}
means restriction along (
C.10
).

C.6.5.
Remark

Roughly speaking, a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category consists of the following data
31
31

31

To simplify the notations, in below we pretend
\underline{x}_{0}
is a
k
-point. Otherwise certain base-changes are necessary.
:

•

For any
\underline{y}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, assign a DG category
{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}
;

•

For any
\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, assign a functor
\operatorname{ins}_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}:{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}
;

•

For any finite collection of
disjoint
points
(\underline{x}_{i})_{i\in I^{\circ}}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
and
\underline{y}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, assign an equivalence

\operatorname{act}_{(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{y})}:\big(\otimes{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\big)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbf{C}}_{(\sqcup\underline{x}_{i})\sqcup\underline{y}}

compatible with the equivalences (
C.3
).

•

Commutative squares similar to (
C.4
).

•

Datum of higher compatibilities.

C.6.6.
Example

Note that the above definitions make sense even for
\underline{x}_{0}=\emptyset
. In this case, it is easy to see the following data are equivalent:

(i)

A unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category
{\mathbf{M}}
;

(ii)

A (plain) DG category
{\mathbf{M}}_{0}
.

Indeed, given
{\mathbf{M}}
, we can consider its fiber at
\emptyset\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\emptyset}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, which is a DG category; conversely given
{\mathbf{M}}_{0}
, the tensor product

\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{0}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\simeq\mathbf{CrysCat}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\emptyset})

has an obvious unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure. One can check these two constructions are inverse to each other.

C.6.7.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category. The pullback of
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
along the morphism
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}
is naturally a factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module at
\underline{x}_{0}
. We denote the resulting object by
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
.

C.6.8.
Variant

As in Sect.
C.4.5
, for a unital lax-factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
, we can define the notion of unital lax-factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories by allowing
\operatorname{act}_{I}
(see (
C.11
)) to be non-invertible.

C.7.
Morphisms between unital factorization module categories

C.7.1.

Let
F:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
be a lax-unital factorization functor between unital factorization categories. For unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
-module cateogry
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
at
\underline{x}_{0}
, a
lax-unital
F
-linear factorization functor
G:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
is a morphism
\underline{G}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})
equipped with commutative diagrams (for any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
)

\textstyle{\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{F})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{G})|_{\operatorname{disj}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}(\underline{G})|_{\operatorname{disj}}}
\textstyle{\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\prime})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime})|_{\operatorname{disj}}}

and a homotopy-coherent datum of compatibility with (
C.6
).

When
F
is (strictly) unital, we say
G
is (strictly) unital if
\underline{G}
is contained in
\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{strict}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})
. In fact,
G
is
automatically
unital.

Lemma C.7.2
.

Let
F:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
be a unital factorization functor, and
G:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
be a lax-unital
F
-linear factorization functor between unital factorization module categories at
\underline{x}_{0}
. Then
G
is strictly unital.

Sketch.

For any affine points
\underline{y},\underline{y}^{\prime}:S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
such that
\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}
, we need to show the following natural transformation is invertible:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ins}_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{G_{\underline{y}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{\underline{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ins}_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{G_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{\underline{y}^{\prime}}.}

Note that if
S=\cup S_{\alpha}
is a finite covering by locally closed subschemes such that
G_{\underline{y}|_{S_{\alpha}}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}|_{S_{\alpha}}}
is invertible for each
\alpha
, then
G_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}
is also invertible. Hence without lose of generality, we can assume
\underline{y^{\prime}}=\underline{y}\sqcup\underline{z}
such that
\underline{y}\cap\underline{z}=\emptyset
. Using the factorization structure, the natural transformation
G_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}
can be identified with

\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ins}_{\emptyset\subseteq\underline{z}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{F_{\emptyset}\otimes G_{\underline{y}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{z}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{\underline{z}}\otimes G_{\underline{y}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{\emptyset}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{\underline{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ins}_{\emptyset\subseteq\underline{z}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{F_{\emptyset\subseteq\underline{z}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{\underline{z}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{\underline{y}}.}

This implies
G_{\underline{y}\subseteq\underline{y}^{\prime}}
is invertible because
F_{\emptyset\subseteq\underline{z}}
is invertible by assumption.

∎

C.7.3.

Let

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

be the 2-category such that:

•

An object is a pair
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})
, where
{\mathbf{A}}
is a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}
is a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category at
\underline{x}_{0}
;

•

A morphism
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})
is a pair
(F,G)
, where
F
is a lax-unital factorization functor, and
G
is a lax-unital
F
-linear factorization functor.

Let

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

be the 1-full subcategory such that morphisms are strictly unital factorization functors.

We have a forgetful functor

(C.12)

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}},\;({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto{\mathbf{A}}.

The fiber of this functor at
{\mathbf{A}}
is

{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},

the 2-category of unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories at
\underline{x}_{0}
. Note that this category is also the fiber of the forgetful functor

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlFactCat},\;({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto{\mathbf{A}}.

See Lemma
C.7.2
.

C.7.4.

Note that
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, together with its
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-module structure, is defined over
S_{0}
. It follows that the symmetric monoidal 2-category

\mathbf{CrysCat}(S_{0})\simeq\operatorname{D}(S_{0})\mathbf{-mod}

acts on the fibers of (
C.12
). In particular, it acts on
{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
.

C.7.5.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}
be a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category at
\underline{x}_{0}
. Recall a factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
in
{\mathbf{A}}
is a lax-unital factorization functor
{\mathcal{A}}:\operatorname{Vect}\to{\mathbf{A}}
(see Sect.
C.5.3
).

A
factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
is
defined
to be a lax-unital factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear functor

{\mathcal{C}}:\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}\to{\mathbf{C}}.

Here
\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
is the unital factorization
\operatorname{Vect}
-module category in Sect.
C.6.7
.

Pairs
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{C}})
of factorization algebras and modules in
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})
form a category, which is denoted by
\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. There is a forgetful functor

(C.13)

\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}}),\;({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{C}})\mapsto{\mathcal{A}}

whose fiber at
{\mathcal{A}}
is the category

{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}

of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

C.7.6.

The action in Sect.
C.7.4
induces an action of
\operatorname{D}(S_{0})
on the fibers of the functor (
C.13
). In particular
\operatorname{D}(S_{0})
acts on
{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}
.

C.7.7.

For
{\mathbf{C}}={\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
, we write

{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}:={\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

See Sect.
C.6.7
.

C.7.8.

Below is a concrete description of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules in a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category
{\mathbf{C}}
.

As in Sect.
C.5.4
, for each marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
, we have a functor

\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\big)\times\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}\big(\big((\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}},\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)

that sends

({\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{F}})\to\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{M}}_{i})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathcal{F}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}},

and a functor

\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}\big(\big((\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}},\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}({\mathbf{C}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)

that sends

{\mathcal{N}}\mapsto\operatorname{union}_{I}^{!}({\mathcal{N}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}.

The factorization structure on
{\mathbf{C}}
provides an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{act}_{I}:\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}\big(\big((\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}},\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)\to

\displaystyle\to\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}\big(\big((\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}},\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}({\mathbf{C}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big).

Then a factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathbf{C}}
is an object

\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\in\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})=:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}^{\operatorname{lax}}

equipped with isomorphisms (for any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
)

\operatorname{act}_{I}\big(\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{union}_{I}^{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{C}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}

and a homotopy-coherent datum of compatibility with (
C.7
).

C.7.9.

Unwinding the definitions, we have the following result. A rigorious proof will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

Lemma C.7.10
.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}
be a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category at
\underline{x}_{0}
. For a factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
in
{\mathbf{A}}
, the category
{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}
is naturally a DG
\operatorname{D}(S_{0})
-module category
32
32

32

In other words,
{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}
is presentable and stable, and the acting functors are compatible with colimits.
and the forgetful functors

{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto\underline{{\mathcal{C}}}\mapsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

are
\operatorname{D}(S_{0})
-linear and colimit-preserving.

C.7.11.
Example

Let
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}})
be as in Sect.
C.6.7
. For a factorization algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
in
{\mathbf{A}}
, its restriction along
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
defines a factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module in
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
. We denote this object by

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

C.7.12.

The following result appears in
[
GLC2
, Lemma C.14.10]
. We will provide a more homotopy-coherent proof in
[
CFZ
]
.

Lemma C.7.13
.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}
be a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category at
\underline{x}_{0}
. Then the forgetful functor

\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

is an equivalence.

C.7.14.

By definition, for any lax unital factorization functors
(F,G):({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})
between unital factorization categories and their modules, we have a functor

\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{C}})\mapsto(F\circ{\mathcal{A}},G\circ{\mathcal{C}})

compatible with (
C.9
). In particular, we obtain a functor

{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to F({\mathcal{A}})\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto G\circ{\mathcal{C}}.

We also write
G({\mathcal{C}}):=G\circ{\mathcal{C}}
.

C.7.15.
Variant

As in Sect.
C.5.10
, we can define
lax-factorization functors
between unital factorization module categories, and use such a notion to define
lax-factorization module objects
.

C.8.
Change of base

In Sect.
C.6
and Sect.
C.7
, we introduced factorization
module
structures at a fixed affine point
\underline{x}_{0}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0})
. In this subsection, we explian how such structures depend on
\underline{x}_{0}
.

C.8.1.

Throughout this subsection, we fix the following notations.

Let
\underline{x}_{0}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0})
and
\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0}^{\prime})
be two affine points and

(C.14)

\textstyle{S_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{x}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{S_{0}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

be a 2-morphism in
\operatorname{CatPreStk}
. In other words,
\alpha
is a morphism in
\widetilde{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
(see Sect.
C.2.2
).

C.8.2.

By construction, there is a canonical morphism

{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\alpha}:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}

defined over the morphism
f_{\alpha}:S_{0}^{\prime}\to S_{0}
and compatible with the forgetful morphisms to
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
. Moreover, this morphism is compatible with the
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-module structures on
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
and
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
(see Sect.
C.6.3
).

It follows that pullback along
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
defines a functor

(C.15)

\alpha_{\dagger}:\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}\to\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}

compatible with the forgetful functors to
\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}
33
33

33

Note that the definition of factorization (algebra) categories is independent of the points
\underline{x}_{0}
.
.

In particular, for
{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}
, we obtain a functor

\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}.

We write

{\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}:=\alpha_{\dagger}({\mathbf{C}}).

In the case when
f_{\alpha}=\operatorname{id}_{S_{0}}
is the identity morphism, we write

\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}:=\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}

and call
\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{C}})
the
propogation of
{\mathbf{C}}
along
\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}
.

C.8.3.
Remark

The construction
\alpha\mapsto\alpha_{\dagger}
is compatible with compositions. In fact, one can construct a coCartesian fibration of 2-categories

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}\to\widetilde{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

such that its fiber at
\underline{x}_{0}
is
\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}
, and the covariant transport functor along
\alpha
is
\alpha_{\dagger}
. A rigorious construction of this coCartesian fibration will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.8.4.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category. The functor

\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}

sends
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
to
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}}
.

C.8.5.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. Write

{\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}:=\alpha_{\dagger}({\mathbf{C}})\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}.

The functor (
C.15
) induces a functor

\displaystyle\alpha_{\dagger}:\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}}((\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}),({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}}))\to

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}}((\operatorname{Vect},\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}}),({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})).

By definition, this is a functor

(C.16)

\alpha_{\dagger}:\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}

compatible with forgetful functors to
\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}})
.

In particular, for
{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}})
, we obtain a functor

(C.17)

\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}})_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}.

When
{\mathbf{C}}={\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
, this gives

(C.18)

\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}

In the case when
f_{\alpha}=\operatorname{id}_{S_{0}}
is the identity morphism, recall

\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{C}}):={\mathbf{C}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}.

Hence we also denote the functor (
C.17
) by

\operatorname{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}:=\alpha_{\dagger}:{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{C}}))_{\underline{x}^{\prime}_{0}}

and call
\operatorname{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}})
the
propogation of
{\mathcal{C}}
along
\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}
.

C.8.6.
Example

The functor (
C.18
) sends
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
to
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}}
. See Sect.
C.7.11
.

C.8.7.

By construction, the functor (
C.17
) fits into a canonical commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!\operatorname{-pull}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}}),}

where the vertical arrows are the forgetful functors.

On the other hand, the 2-morphim (

) induces a 2-morphism

\textstyle{S_{0}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\alpha}}

\textstyle{S_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},}

which induces a natural transformation

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!\operatorname{-pull}}

(by the definition of lax sections). Combining these squares, we obtain a canonical natural transformation

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

C.8.8.
Remark

The natural transformation (
C.19
) can be concretely described as follows. For simplicity, we assume
S_{0}=S_{0}^{\prime}=\operatorname{pt}
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}

{\mathcal{A}}

{\mathbf{C}}

The top horizontal arrow sends

C.8.9.

Since the morphism

{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\alpha}:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}

C.9.
External fusion

The main goal of this subsection is to explain
{\mathcal{A}}\underline{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
(see Lemma
C.8.10
) is naturally a unital
lax-factorization
category via
external fusion
.

The
construction
of external fusion for factorization modules was sketched in
[
Ra
, Sect. 6.22]
and
[
GLC2
, Sect. B.11.14]
. However, to work with external fusion, especially in a homotopy-coherent way, it is better to characterize them via universal properties. Recall the tensor product of usual modules can be
defined
as the object that corepresents multilinear morphisms. Following this idea, we will
define
the fusion product of factorization modules as the object that corepresents
factorization multi-functors
. In fact, this approach to external fusion was alluded to in
[
Ra
, Sect. 6.26]
.

C.9.1.

Let
S_{0}
be an affine scheme and
\underline{x}_{i}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0})
(
i\in I
) be a finite collection of
disjoint

S_{0}
-points. Consider the
S_{0}
-morphism

(C.22)

\operatorname{union}_{(\underline{x}_{i})}:\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\big)_{/S_{0}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}},\;(\underline{y}_{i})\mapsto\cup\underline{y}_{i},

where the source is the fiber product of
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}
relative to
S_{0}
. By definition, (
C.22
) is a
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-multilinear morphism, i.e., it is
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
-linear in each factor of the source.

Let

(C.23)

\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\big)_{/S_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\subseteq\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\big)_{/S_{0}}

be the subfunctor containing
disjoint
points
(\underline{y}_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Let

and its restriction to the disjoint locus (

Using the language in Sect.

C.9.3.

A
lax-unital factorization multifunctor

(F,G):({\mathbf{A}},({\mathbf{C}}_{i})_{i\in I})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})

consists of the following data:

•

A lax-unital factorization functor
F:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
;

•

A morphism

\underline{G}:\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i})_{/S_{0}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\operatorname{union}_{(\underline{x}_{i})}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime})|_{\operatorname{disj}}

in

\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{lax}}\big(\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\big)_{/S_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\big),

such that for each
i\in I
,
\underline{G}
is a factorization
F
-linear functor
35
35

35

This is a structure rather than property.
with respect to the
i
-th factorization module structures on the source and the target.

For a fixed
F
, we call

G:({\mathbf{C}}_{i})_{i\in I}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}

as above a
lax-unital factorization
F
-linear multi-functor
.

We say
(F,G)
is (strictly) unital if
\underline{F}
and
\underline{G}
are strict morphisms.

C.9.4.

One can mimic the definition of compositions of usual multilinear maps to define compositions of
factorization
multilinear functors. Namely, for a given map
\phi:I\to I^{\prime}
between finite sets and (lax-)unital factorization multifunctors

(F,G_{i^{\prime}}):({\mathbf{A}},({\mathbf{C}}_{i})_{i\in\phi^{-1}(i^{\prime})})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}})\;\;i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}

and

(F^{\prime},G^{\prime}):({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}),

there is a canonical (lax-)unital factorization multifunctor

\big(F^{\prime}\circ F,G^{\prime}\circ_{\phi}(G_{i^{\prime}})\big):({\mathbf{A}},({\mathbf{C}}_{i})_{i\in I})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}).

A homotopy-coherent construction of these compositions will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.9.5.
Remark

The notion of
{\mathbf{A}}
-multilinear functors and their compositions is closely related to the framework of
pseudo-tensor categories
in
[
BD1
]
. See Sect.
C.13
for more details.

C.9.7.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. For
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0})
such that
\underline{x}\cap\underline{x}_{0}=\emptyset
, consider

\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}\sqcup\underline{x}}({\mathbf{C}})\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}\sqcup\underline{x}}

(see Sect.
C.8.2
). There is a canonical unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear bifunctor

({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}}},{\mathbf{C}})\to\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}\sqcup\underline{x}}({\mathbf{C}})

constructed as follows.

By definition, we need to define a strict morphism

(C.24)

(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{S_{0}}}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times_{S_{0}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}})}

between crystal of categories over
({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times_{S_{0}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}})
, such that it is compatible with the factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structures coming from both factors. Here the RHS means pullback of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
along the map

({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times_{S_{0}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},\;(\underline{y},\underline{y}_{0})\to\underline{y}\sqcup\underline{y}_{0}.

Note that this map factors as

({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times_{S_{0}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\to({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{union}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}.

Hence to construct (
C.24
), we only need a strict morphism

(C.25)

(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}|_{({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}}}

compatible with the factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structures coming from both factors. However, the factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure of
{\mathbf{C}}
implies there is a canonical
isomorphism
(
C.25
).

C.9.8.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and

{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\;\;(i\in I),\;\;{\mathbf{D}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}}

with

being finite. We say a
unital
factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear multi-functor

exhibits

as the fusion product of

if pre-composing with
(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{A}},G)
induces an equivalence between

•

the category of lax-unital factorization functors

({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{D}})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})

•

the category of lax-unital factorization multi-functors

({\mathbf{A}},({\mathbf{C}}_{i})_{i\in I})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})

for any unital factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
and

.

Note that
{\mathbf{D}}
, equipped with the multi-functor
G
, is essentially unique if exists. We write

\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{{\operatorname{fact}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}

for this object.

Proposition C.9.10
.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}

is an equivalence.

Sketch.

It is enough to treat the case

. To simplify the notations, we asssume
S_{0}=\operatorname{pt}
(otherwise one just replace absolute products, tensor products below by relative ones).

Consider the Bar simplicial diagram

\cdots{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}}\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}}

associated to the

-modules

. We can restrict to the disjoint loci and obtain a simplicial diagram

(C.26)

\cdots({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\rightrightarrows({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}})_{\operatorname{disj}}.

For

, let

u_{n}:({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})^{\times n}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}})_{\operatorname{disj}}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}\sqcup\underline{x}_{2}}

be the union map. Note that these maps provide an augmentation of the diagram (
C.26
). One can show this gives an etale
37
37

37

A categorical
{\mathcal{Z}}
is etale over
{\mathcal{Y}}

(see Sect.

{\mathcal{Y}}
.
hypercover for
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}\sqcup\underline{x}_{2}}
.

The factorization structures on
{\mathbf{A}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}_{i}
implies the objects

(C.27)

(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\boxtimes n}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{strict}}(({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})^{\times n}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}})_{\operatorname{disj}}).

are compatible with (
C.26
) and pullback functors. One can prove crystals of categories on categorical prestacks satisfy etale hyperdescent. It follows that there is a unique object

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{strict}}(({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{1}}\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{2}})_{\operatorname{disj}})

such that
u_{\bullet}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\boxtimes\bullet}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}
. It is easy to see
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
has a natural factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure and the equivalence

(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}u_{0}^{*}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

exhibits
{\mathbf{C}}
as the fusion product of
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
.

∎

C.9.11.
Example

It is easy to see (for example from the proof of the above proposition) that the
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear bifunctor

({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}}},{\mathbf{C}})\to\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}\sqcup\underline{x}}({\mathbf{C}})

constructed in Sect.
C.9.7
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{{\operatorname{fact}}}}{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\mathbf{prop}_{\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq\underline{x}_{0}\sqcup\underline{x}}({\mathbf{C}}).

In particular,

{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{{\operatorname{fact}}}}{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}^{\prime}}}\simeq{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}\sqcup\underline{x}^{\prime}}}.

C.9.12.

The details for the remaining part of this subsection will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.9.13.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathcal{A}}
be a factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}
. For a finite collection of disjoint points
\underline{x}_{i}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S_{0})
,
i\in I
, there is a canonical functor

(C.28)

\prod{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}},\;({\mathcal{C}}_{i})_{i\in I}\mapsto\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}^{{\operatorname{fact}}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}

that sends
({\mathcal{C}}_{i})_{i\in I}
to the lax-unital factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear functor

\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}}}

corresponding to the lax-unital factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear
multi-functor

(\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{i}}})_{i\in I}\xrightarrow{({\mathcal{C}}_{i})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{i}}})_{i\in I}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}}}.

One can show the functor (
C.28
) is
\operatorname{D}(S_{0})
-multilinear. Hence we obtain a functor

(C.29)

\bigotimes_{\operatorname{D}(S_{0})}{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}},\;({\mathcal{C}}_{i})_{i\in I}\mapsto\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}^{{\operatorname{fact}}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{i},

which is called the
external fusion functor
for factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules. By construction, we have a canonical commutative diagram:

(C.30)

\textstyle{\bigotimes_{\operatorname{D}(S_{0})}{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigotimes_{\operatorname{D}(S_{0})}{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{i}},}

where the vertical arrows are the forgetful functors, and the bottom equivalence is due to the factorization structure on
{\mathbf{A}}.

C.9.14.

Moreover, using the universal propery of external fusion, one can show the functors (
C.29
) are compatible with the change-of-base functors (
C.17
). In other words, for any finite set
I
, we obtain a strict morphism

\operatorname{mult}_{I}:(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}{\mathcal{A}}\underline{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}({\mathcal{A}}\underline{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}

in

\mathbf{CrysCat}^{\operatorname{strict}}((\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\operatorname{disj}}).

Finally, using the universal propery of external fusion, one can supply a datum of associativity and commutativity for the functors
\operatorname{mult}_{I}
. In other words, we obtain a structure of unital
lax-factorization
category (see Sect.
C.4.5
) on
{\mathcal{A}}\underline{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
. We denote it just by

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\in\mathbf{UntlFactCat}.

Moreover, the (
C.21
) provides a
lax-unital
factorization functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\to{\mathbf{A}}.

By construction, it sends the unit

\operatorname{unit}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}

of
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
to
{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}})
. Therefore we write

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{enh}}:=\operatorname{unit}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}).

C.9.15.

The construction
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathcal{A}})\mapsto{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
is functorial in
{\mathbf{A}}
. In other words, for any lax-unital factorization functor
F:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
, we have a canonical
unital
38
38

38

The functor below is strictly unital by Lemma
C.5.9
, which is also true for unital lax-factorization categories (including
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}
).
factorization functor

F^{\operatorname{enh}}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}\to F({\mathcal{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}

compatible with
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}
,
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{F({\mathcal{A}})}
and

.

Combining with Lemma

, we obtain a canonical unital factorization functor

F^{\operatorname{enh}}:{\mathbf{A}}\to F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}

such that
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}})}\circ F^{\operatorname{enh}}\simeq F
.

C.9.16.
Variant

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathcal{A}}
be a factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}
. For any
{\mathbf{C}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, one can similarly construct a unital
lax-factorization

({\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}})
-module category at
\underline{x}_{0}
, denoted by

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}),

such that its fiber at
\underline{x}_{0}
is the DG category of factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules in
{\mathbf{C}}
(see Lemma
C.8.10
). It is equipped with a lax-unital
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}
-linear factorization functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathbf{C}}}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}}.

C.9.17.

Moreover, for a lax-unital factorization functor
(F,G):({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{C}})\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})
, we have a
unital
factorization
F^{\operatorname{enh}}
-linear functor

G^{\operatorname{enh}}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}})\to F({\mathcal{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}).

In particular, we have a unital factorization
F^{\operatorname{enh}}
-linear functor

G^{\operatorname{enh}}:{\mathbf{C}}\to F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}).

Conversely, given any
G^{\operatorname{enh}}
as above, one can recover
G
as
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}}),{\mathbf{C}}}\circ G^{\operatorname{enh}}
. One can check these two constructions are inverse to each other. In other words, for fixed
F:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
and modules
{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
, the following two data are equivalent:

•

A unital factorization
F^{\operatorname{enh}}
-linear functor

G^{\operatorname{enh}}:{\mathbf{C}}\to F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}}){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}).

•

A lax unital factorization
F
-linear functor

G:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}.

C.10.
Restrictions of factorization modules

C.10.1.

We say a functor
{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
between 2-categories is a (1,2)-Cartesian fibration if

•

There are enough Cartesian 1-morphisms. In other words, for any morphism
f:u\to v
in
{\mathbf{B}}
and a lifting
V\in{\mathbf{E}}
of
v
, there exists a lifting
F:U\to V
of
f
such that for any
W\in{\mathbf{E}}
over
w\in{\mathbf{B}}
, the following square of categories is Cartesian:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{E}}(W,U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F\circ-}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{E}}(W,V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{B}}(w,u)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\circ-}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{B}}(w,v)}

•

There are enough Caresian 2-morphisms. In other words, for objects
U,V\in{\mathbf{E}}
over
u,v\in{\mathbf{B}}
, the functor

\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{E}}(U,V)\to\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{B}}(u,v)

is a Cartesian fibration.

•

The collection of Cartesian 2-morphisms are closed under horizontal compositions.

C.10.2.
Remark

The theory of (1,2)-Cartesian fibrations is developed in
[
GHL
]
,
[
AS1
]
and
[
AS2
]
under the name of
inner 2-Cartesian fibrations
. In particular, there is a Grothendieck cosntruction in
[
AS2
]
which says knowing a (1,2)-Cartesian fibration
{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
is equivalent to knowing a functor
{\mathbf{B}}^{1\operatorname{-op},2\operatorname{-op}}\to 2-\mathbf{Cat}
.

C.10.3.

The following result will be one of the main theorems for
[
CFZ
]
.

Theorem C.10.4
.

The forgetful functor

(C.31)

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}

is a (1,2)-Cartesian fibration.

C.10.5.

We will explain the main ideas of the proof in the next subsection. For now, we deduce some useful results from it.

C.10.6.

The Grothendieck construction in
[
AS2
]
provides a functor

(C.32)

(\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}})^{2\operatorname{-op},1\operatorname{-op}}\to 2-\mathbf{Cat}.

In other words, for a
lax-unital
factorization functor
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
, we have a contravariant transport functor between the fibers of (
C.31
):

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}:{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

The construction
\Phi\mapsto\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}
is contravariant. In other words, for a 2-morphism
\Phi\to\Phi^{\prime}
, we have a natural transformation

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}.

When
\Phi
is unital, we also write
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}:=\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}
.

C.10.7.

Recall that inside any 2-category, there is a notion of
adjoint pair of 1-morphisms
. Moreover, a functor between 2-categories always sends adjoint pairs to adjoint pairs. Applying to the functor to (
C.32
), we obtain:

Proposition C.10.8
.

Suppose
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{B}}:\Psi
is an adjoint pair in
\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}
, then we have an adjoint pair

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}:{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}:\mathbf{Res}_{\Psi}^{\operatorname{untl}}

in
2-\mathbf{Cat}
. In particular, for
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
and
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, we have a canonical equivalence between the following categories:

•

The category of unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear functors
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})\to{\mathbf{M}}
;

•

The category of unital factorization
{\mathbf{B}}
-linear functors
{\mathbf{N}}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\Psi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{M}})
.

C.10.9.

For
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, the object
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})
is called the
restriction of
{\mathbf{D}}
along
\Phi
. By definition, it is equipped with a lax-unital
\Phi
-linear factorization functor

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})\to{\mathbf{N}},

which is a Cartesian lifting of
\Phi
in
\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
.

In other words, for any test lax-unital factorization functor
F:{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{A}}
and test object
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, pre-composing with
\mathbf{Res}_{F}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})\to{\mathbf{N}}
induces an equivalence between the following categories:

•

The category of lax-unital
\Phi\circ F
-linear factorization functors
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{N}}
;

•

The category of lax-unital
F
-linear factorization functors
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{Res}_{F}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})
.

Taking
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Vect}
and
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Vect}^{\operatorname{fact}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
, we obtain the following result.

Proposition C.10.10
.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
be a lax-unital factorization functor and
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. Then for any
{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}})
, pre-composing with
\mathbf{Res}_{F}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})\to{\mathbf{N}}
induces an equivalence

{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}(\mathbf{Res}_{F}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))_{\underline{x}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}F({\mathcal{A}})\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

C.10.11.

Combining with Lemma
C.7.13
, we obtain the following result.

Corollary C.10.12
.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
be a lax-unital factorization functor and
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. There is a canonical dotted equivalence making the following diagram commute

\textstyle{(\mathbf{Res}_{F}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{F(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathbf{A}})\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{oblv}_{\underline{x}_{0}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{N}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{N}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.}

C.10.13.

In particular, for
{\mathbf{A}}=\operatorname{Vect}
, we obtain:

Corollary C.10.14
.

Let
{\mathbf{B}}
be a unital factorization category and
{\mathcal{B}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{B}})
. For any
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, there is a canonical equivalence

(\mathbf{Res}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))_{\underline{x}_{0}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

C.10.15.
Remark

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
be a
unital
factorization functor and
{\mathbf{M}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
. Then the underlying crystal of categories for
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{M}})
can be explicitly calculated as a limit (see Sect.
C.11.7
and Sect.
C.11.11
). For example, the restriction of
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{M}})
along the map

X\times S_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{{\operatorname{untl}}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;y\mapsto y\cup\underline{x}_{0}

fits into the following Cartesian square (Sect.
C.11.18
):

\textstyle{\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{M}})|_{X\times S_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{M}}|_{X\times S_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{A}}|_{X}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{M}}|_{\underline{x}_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{M}}|_{X\times S_{0}}),}

where

•

j:(X\times S_{0})\setminus\operatorname{graph}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to X\times S_{0}
is the complement of the union of the graphs for elements in
\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq X(S_{0})
;

•

the bottom horizontal functor is provided by the factorization
{\mathbf{B}}
-module structure on
\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}
and the functor
\Phi
.

C.10.16.

Let
g:{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{B}})
. The natural transformation
\mathbf{Res}_{g}^{\operatorname{untl}}:\mathbf{Res}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
provides a functor

(\mathbf{Res}_{{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to(\mathbf{Res}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

By the above corollary, we obtain a canonical functor

\operatorname{Res}_{g}:{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

For
{\mathcal{N}}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, its image
\operatorname{Res}_{g}({\mathcal{N}})
is called the
restriction of
{\mathcal{N}}
along
g
.

C.10.17.
Remark

By definition, a (1,2)-Cartesian fibration
\pi:{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{F}}
induces a Cartesian fibrations

\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{E}}(u,v)\to\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathbf{F}}(\pi(u),\pi(v))

of 1-categories. Hence Theorem
C.31
implies the forgetful functor

\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{B}})

is a Cartesian fibration between 1-categories. It follows from construction that the functor
\operatorname{Res}_{g}
is the contravariant transport functor for this Cartesian fibration.

C.10.18.

We now provide a useful criterion to check whether a factorization module category is obtained via restriction. We need the following lemma.

Lemma C.10.19
.

The forgetful functor

{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{CrysCat}(S_{0}),\;{\mathbf{M}}\mapsto{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

is conservative on 2-morphisms.

Sketch.

Let
G_{1},G_{2}:{\mathbf{M}}\to{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}
be factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear functors and
\alpha:G_{1}\to G_{2}
be a 2-morphism between them such that
\alpha_{\underline{x}_{0}}
is invetible. For any affine test scheme
\underline{x}:S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, we need to show
\alpha_{\underline{x}}
is also invertible.

Suppose
\underline{x}=\underline{x}_{0}|_{S}\sqcup\underline{y}
can be written as a disjoint union of subsets. Then the factorization structure implies

(G_{i})_{\underline{x}}:{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}_{\underline{x}}

can be identified with

\operatorname{Id}\otimes(G_{i})_{\underline{x}_{0}}:{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{y}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}_{0}|_{S}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{y}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}_{0}|_{S}}^{\prime},

and the 2-morphism
\alpha_{\underline{x}}
can be identified with
\operatorname{Id}\otimes\alpha_{\underline{x}_{0}|_{S}}
. This implies
\alpha_{\underline{x}}
is invertible because
\alpha_{\underline{x}_{0}}
is so.

For the general case, we can replace
S
with a covering of locally closed subschemes such that the above property holds on each subscheme. This reduces the general case to the above case.

∎

Proposition C.10.20
.

Let

(\Phi,\Phi^{m}):({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})\rightleftarrows({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{N}}):(\Psi,\Psi^{m})

be an adjoint pair in
\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
such that:

(i)

The left adjoint
\Phi
is unital
39
39

39

In fact, a standard argument shows that this is automatic.
;

(ii)

It induces an equivalence

\Phi^{m}_{\underline{x}_{0}}:{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{N}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}:\Psi^{m}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

in
\mathbf{CrysCat}(S_{0})
.

Then the canonical factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-linear functor

{\mathbf{M}}\to\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})

is an equivalence.

Sketch.

Using the universal property of
\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, it is easy to show the given adjoint pair can be written as the composition of
40
40

40

Similar claim is true for any (1,2)-Cartesian fibration.

({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})\rightleftarrows({\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))

and

({\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}}))\rightleftarrows({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{N}}).

Moreover, by Corollary
C.10.12
and assumption (i), the second pair induces an equivalence

\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{N}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

Hence by assumption (ii), the first pair also induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Res}_{\Phi}^{\operatorname{untl}}({\mathbf{N}})_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

Now the claim follows formally from Lemma
C.10.19
.

∎

C.11.
Sketch of Theorem
C.10.4

In this subsection, we explain the main ideas in the proof of Theorem
C.10.4
. A detailed proof will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.11.1.

Let
{\mathbf{E}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
be a functor between 2-categories. To show it is a (1,2)-Cartesian fibration, one only needs to show:

(a)

There are enough
locally Cartesian 1-morphisms
. By definition, this means for any arrow
\Delta^{1}\to{\mathbf{B}}
, the base-change
{\mathbf{E}}\times_{\mathbf{B}}\Delta^{1}\to\Delta^{1}
has enough Cartesian 1-morphisms.

(b)

There are enough
locally Cartesian 2-morphisms
.

(c)

The collection of locally Cartesian 1-morphisms are closed under compositions.

(d)

The collection of locally Cartesian 2-morphisms are closed under both horizontal and vertical compositions.

In
[
CFZ
]
, we will provide a
constructive
proof for (a) and (b), and use these explicit constructions to verify (c) and (d). In this subsection, we only explain the construction of locally Cartsian 1-morphisms in

(C.33)

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}

The construction for locally Cartesian 2-morphisms is similar but much simpler.

C.11.2.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
be a lax-unital factorization functor, i.e., a moprhism in the base of (
C.33
). Let
{\mathbf{N}}
be a unital factorization
{\mathbf{B}}
-module at
\underline{x}_{0}

lying over

We can enlarge (

(module)-cateogires:

\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}^{\operatorname{lax-untl}}.

By Sect.

and Sect.
C.9.16

in the base of (
C.34

By the universal property in Sect.

•

The category of unital factorization

C.11.3.

Roughly speaking, the above reduction allows us to get rid of lax-unital functors, with the caveat that
{\mathbf{B}}
(and
{\mathbf{N}}
) is allowed to be
lax-factorization
.

C.11.4.

Now the desired claim (about existence of (
C.35
)) follows formally from the following two claims:

(i)

The functor

(C.36)

\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}

has enough locally Cartesian 1-morphisms.

(ii)

Consider the embedding

\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

and its fiber at an object
{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbf{UntlFactCat}
:

{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{laxfact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

The latter functor admits a right adjoint.

C.11.5.

Claim (i) is obvious modulo homotopy-coherent issues.

Namely, for any morphism
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
in
\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}
and an object
{\mathbf{N}}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{laxfact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
, the underlying crystal of categories
\underline{{\mathbf{N}}}
has a unital
lax-
factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure given by

\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{N}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{B}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{N}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\operatorname{union}_{I}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{N}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}},

where the first functor is given by
\Phi
, and the second functor is the lax-factorization
{\mathbf{B}}
-module structure on
\underline{{\mathbf{N}}}
(see Sect.
C.6.8
). In other words, we obtain a canonical object

\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{lax-fact}}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{N}})\in{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{laxfact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

equipped with a unital factorization
\Phi
-linear functor

\mathbf{Res}^{\operatorname{lax-fact}}_{\Phi}({\mathbf{N}})\to{\mathbf{N}}.

One can check this is a locally Cartesian 1-morphism in (
C.36
). A homotopy-coherent proof using the language of (generalized) operads will be provided in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.11.6.

Claim (ii) is proved via an explicit construction of the desired right adjoint
strictening
functor

\mathbf{Str}_{\mathbf{A}}:{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{laxfact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

In fact, for future reference, we will show the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlFactCat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}}

is right adjiontable along the horizontal directions. In other words, the horizontal functors admit right adjoints, and the Beck–Chevalley transformation is invertible:

\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Str}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlFactCat}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Str}}

Note that the desired functor
\mathbf{Str}_{\mathbf{A}}
can be given by the restriction of the top horizontal functor on the fiber over
{\mathbf{A}}
.

C.11.7.

We will construct an endo-functor

\sharp:\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}

equipped with a natural transformation
\mu:\sharp\to\operatorname{Id}
and define

\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}}):=\lim\big(\cdots\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp\sharp})\xrightarrow{\mu({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp})}({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp})\to({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})\big)

to be the sequential limit of the
\sharp
-construction. We will show

(1)

The objects
\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})
is contained in
\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
;

(2)

The functor

\operatorname{Fun}(-,{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp})\to\operatorname{Fun}(-,{\mathbf{A}})

is invertible when restricted to
\mathbf{UntlFactCat}
;

(3)

The functor

\operatorname{Fun}(-,({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp}))\to\operatorname{Fun}(-,({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}}))

is invertible when restricted to
\mathbf{UntlFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
.

It is clear that these properties imply the claim in Sect.
C.11.6
.

C.11.8.
Remark

The definition of
\sharp
below might look mysterious, but in fact, it comes from a general construction about operads once we reformulate factorization structures using the language in Sect.
C.13
.

C.11.9.

Let
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})\in\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}
be a pair. To define its image under
\sharp
, we need some notations.

For any finite set
I\in\operatorname{Fin}
, we write

{\mathcal{R}}_{I}:=\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}

and

\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{I}:=(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{I}).

For any
marked
finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}\in\operatorname{Fin}_{*}
, we write

{\mathcal{R}}_{I}:=\big((\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})\times{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}

and

\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}_{I}:=\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\in\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{I}).

The readers should be able to distinguish the marked and non-marked notations based on the context.

For a morphism
\phi:I\to J
in either
\operatorname{Fin}
or
\operatorname{Fin}_{*}
, we have an etale map

\operatorname{union}_{I\to J}:{\mathcal{R}}_{I}\to{\mathcal{R}}_{J},\;(\underline{y}_{i})_{i\in I}\to(\underline{z}_{j})_{j\in J}

given by
\underline{z}_{j}:=\bigsqcup_{i\in\phi^{-1}(j)}\underline{y}_{i}
(see Footnote
37
for the definition of etale maps). One can show the functor

T_{I\to J}:=\operatorname{union}_{I\to J}^{*}:\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{J})\to\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{I})

admits a right adjoint

T_{J\leftarrow I}:=\operatorname{union}_{I\to J,*}:\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{I})\to\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{R}}_{J}),

and there are base-change isomorphisms between them. In particular, one can prove

(C.37)

T_{J\to K}\circ T_{K\leftarrow I}\simeq T_{J\leftarrow I\times_{K}J}\circ T_{I\times_{K}J\to I}=:T_{J\leftarrow I\times_{K}J\to I}

C.11.10.

Note that the lax-factorization structure on
(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}})
provides canonical morphisms

(C.38)

\displaystyle\theta_{I\to J}:\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{I}\to T_{I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})

for

\displaystyle I,J\in\operatorname{Fin}

\displaystyle\theta_{I\to J}:\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}_{I}\to T_{I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}_{J})

for

\displaystyle I,J\in\operatorname{Fin}_{*}.

We have a functor

(C.39)

\displaystyle\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{CryCat}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}})

\displaystyle(I\xrightarrow{\phi}J)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}),

where

•

\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})
is the category of twisted arrows in
\operatorname{Fin}
. In other words, an object in

is a morphism
\phi:I\to J
in
\operatorname{Fin}
, while a morphism in

is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{J^{\prime};}

•

The functor (
C.39
) sends the above commutative diagram to the composition

T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})\xrightarrow{T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\theta_{J\to J^{\prime}})}T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J^{\prime}}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J^{\prime}})\to T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I^{\prime}\to J^{\prime}}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J^{\prime}}),

where the last morphism is induced by the adjunction
(T_{I^{\prime}\to I},T_{I\leftarrow I^{\prime}})
.

Similarly we have a functor

(C.40)

\displaystyle\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin}_{*})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{CryCat}({\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}_{\underline{x}_{0}})

\displaystyle(I\xrightarrow{\phi}J)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle T_{\{0\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}_{J}),

C.11.11.

We now define

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}}:=\lim_{I\to J}T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})

indexed by

C.11.12.

We now explain that
(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp}})
has a canonical unital lax-factorization structure. We will only do this for
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}}
. The module part can be constructed by replacing non-marked sets with marked ones.

We will only construct the structure morphisms (for any finite set
K\in\operatorname{Fin}
)

(C.42)

(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{k\in K}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\sharp})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\operatorname{union}_{K}^{*}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}})|_{\operatorname{disj}}=T_{K\to\{1\}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}),

and leave the higher compatibilities to
[
CFZ
]
.

We have a canonical morphism

(C.43)

(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{k\in K}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\sharp})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\lim_{(I_{k}\to J_{k})_{k\in K}}\big(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{k\in K}}T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I_{k}\to J_{k}}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J_{k}})\big)|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}

by exchanging limits with external products and restrictions
41
41

41

In fact, the restriction functor
f^{*}:\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Z}})\to\mathbf{CrysCat}({\mathcal{Y}})
commutes with limits for any map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
between categorical prestacks. This follows from the fact that
-\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S_{1})}\operatorname{D}(S_{2})
commutes with limits for affine schemes
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
.
. Unwinding the definitions, we have

\big(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{k\in K}}T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I_{k}\to J_{k}}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J_{k}})\big)|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}\simeq T_{K\leftarrow\sqcup I_{k}\to\sqcup J_{k}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{\sqcup J_{k}}

Hence we obtain a morphism

(C.44)

(\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{k\in K}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}^{\sharp})|_{\operatorname{disj}}\to\lim_{(I_{k}\to J_{k})_{k\in K}}T_{K\leftarrow\sqcup I_{k}\to\sqcup J_{k}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{\sqcup J_{k}}\simeq\lim_{I\to J\to K}T_{K\leftarrow I\to J}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J},

where the last limit is indexed by
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin}_{/K})
, which is equivalence to the category of twisted arrows
I\to J
equipped with a map
J\to K
.

On the other hand, one can show
T_{K\to\{1\}}
commutes with limits (see Footnote
41
). Hence we have

(C.45)

T_{K\to\{1\}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp})\simeq\lim_{I\to J}T_{K\to\{1\}}\circ T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})\simeq\lim_{I\to J}T_{K\leftarrow K\times I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}),

where the last equivalence is due to the base-change isomorphism (
C.37
). Let
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})_{K}
be the category of twisted arrows
I\to J
in
\operatorname{Fin}
equipped with a map
I\to K
. Note that

\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})\to\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})_{K},\;(I\to J)\mapsto(K\leftarrow K\times I\to J)

is left adjoint to the forgetful functor. It follows that we have a canonical equivalence

\lim_{K\leftarrow I\to J}T_{K\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\lim_{I\to J}T_{K\leftarrow K\times I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}),

where the first limit is indexed by
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})_{K}
. Combining with (
C.45
), we obtain an equivalence

(C.46)

T_{K\to\{1\}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp})\simeq\lim_{K\leftarrow I\to J}T_{K\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}).

Finally, the forgetful functor
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin}_{/K})\to\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})_{K}
admits a right adjoint

(K\leftarrow I\to J)\mapsto(I\to K\times J\to K).

This implies we have a canonical equivalence

(C.47)

\lim_{K\leftarrow I\to J}T_{K\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})\simeq\lim_{I\to J\to K}T_{K\leftarrow I\to J}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}.

Now the desired morphism (
C.42
) is defined to be the composition

\eqref{eqn-lax-on-fix-5}^{-1}\circ\eqref{eqn-lax-on-fix-4}^{-1}\circ\eqref{eqn-lax-on-fix-2}.

In
[
CFZ
]
, we will show these morphisms (when
