varies) indeed define an object

{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}\in\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat}.

Similarly, we have

({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp})\in\mathbf{UntlLaxFactModCat}_{\underline{x}_{0}}.

Moreover, (
C.41
) can be upgraded to a morphism

({\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp},{\mathbf{M}}^{\sharp})\to({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}}).

C.11.13.

We now explain claim (1) in Sect.
C.11.7
. We will only do this for

\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}}):=\lim\big(\cdots\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp\sharp}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}\to{\mathbf{A}}\big)

The module part can be constructed by replacing non-marked sets with marked ones.

For any integer
m
, we say a unital lax-factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
is
m
-strict
if for any collection of disjoint affine points
\underline{x}_{k}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
,
k\in K
satisfying
|\sqcup\underline{x}_{k}|\leq m
, the structural functor

\operatorname{mult}_{(\underline{x}_{k})}:\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{k}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{k}}

is invertible. Note that
{\mathbf{A}}
is always
(-1)
-strict. Also note that
{\mathbf{A}}
being
0
-strict is equivalent to
{\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}
.

To prove claim (1), we only need to show
{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}
is
m
-strict whenever
{\mathbf{A}}
is
(m-1)
-strict.

We first consider the case
m=0
. Unwinding the definitions, we have

{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}_{\emptyset}\simeq\lim_{I\to J}\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})\big)_{\emptyset}\simeq\lim_{I\to J}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J})_{(\emptyset)_{j\in J}}\simeq\lim_{I\to J}({\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset})^{\otimes J}

where recall the limit is indexed by
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})
. Note that the forgetful functor

\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})\to\operatorname{Fin},\;(I\to J)\mapsto J

is a weak homotopy equivalence. It follows that

\lim_{I\to J}({\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset})^{\otimes J}\simeq\lim_{J\in\operatorname{\operatorname{Fin}}}({\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset})^{\otimes J}\simeq({\mathbf{A}}_{\emptyset})^{\otimes\emptyset}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

Hence
{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}_{\emptyset}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}
as desired.

We now prove the general case when
m>0
. We need to show the structural functor

\operatorname{mult}_{(\underline{x}_{k})}:\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}_{k}}^{\sharp}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\sqcup\underline{x}_{k}}^{\sharp}

is inverible when
|\sqcup\underline{x}_{k}|\leq m
. Since the
m=0
case is known, we can assume
|K|\geq 2
and each
\underline{x}_{k}
is non-empty. This implies
|\underline{x}_{k}|<m
.

By construction in Sect.
C.11.12
, we only need to show the fiber of (
C.43
) at

(\underline{x}_{k})_{k\in K}\in\big(\prod_{k\in K}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}(S)={\mathcal{R}}_{K}(S)

is invertible. In other words, we need to show we can exchange limits with tensor products in the following expression:

\bigotimes_{k\in K}\big(\lim_{I_{k}\to J_{k}}\big(T_{\{k\}\leftarrow I_{k}\to J_{k}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J_{k}}\big)_{\underline{x}_{k}}\big)_{/\operatorname{D}(S)}

For this purpose, we prove the following stronger claim: for fixed
k\in K
and
any

\operatorname{D}(S)
-module category
{\mathcal{C}}
, the functor

{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\lim_{I_{k}\to J_{k}}\big(T_{\{k\}\leftarrow I_{k}\to J_{k}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J_{k}}\big)_{\underline{x}_{k}}\to\lim_{I_{k}\to J_{k}}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\big(T_{\{k\}\leftarrow I_{k}\to J_{k}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J_{k}}\big)_{\underline{x}_{k}}

is invertible. For this purpose, we prove the following stronger claim: for
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
such that
|\underline{x}|<m
, the functor

(C.48)

\lim_{I\to J}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}\big)_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}

is an equivalence.

Using the assumption that
{\mathbf{A}}
is
(m-1)
-strict, we have

\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}\big)_{\underline{x}}\simeq\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{I}\big)_{\underline{x}}.

Since the forgetful functor
\operatorname{TwArr}(\operatorname{Fin})\to\operatorname{Fin}^{\operatorname{op}},\;(I\to J)\mapsto I
is a weak equivalence, we obtain

\lim_{I\to J}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I\to J}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{J}\big)_{\underline{x}}\simeq\lim_{I\in\operatorname{Fin}^{\operatorname{op}}}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow I}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{I}\big)_{\underline{x}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}\big(T_{\{1\}\leftarrow\{1\}}\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{\{1\}}\big)_{\underline{x}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{\operatorname{D}(S)}{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}

as desired.

C.11.14.
Remark

The above argument is closely related to the notion of
pro-nilpotent operads
in
[
FG
]
. We will explain this in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.11.15.

Note that the equivalence (
C.48
) says:

Lemma C.11.16
.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital
(m-1)
-strict factorization category. Then the functor
{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}\to{\mathbf{A}}
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}_{\underline{x}}\to{\mathbf{A}}_{\underline{x}}

for any affine point
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
with
|\underline{x}|<m
.

C.11.17.

In particular, we have shown the restriction of the sequence

\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\to\mathbf{UntlLaxFactCat},\;-n\mapsto{\mathbf{A}}^{n\sharp}

at a point
\underline{x}\in{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
becomes stable for
-n<-|\underline{x}|
. In particular,

\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{x}}\simeq({\mathbf{A}}^{n\sharp})_{\underline{x}}\textrm{ for }n>|\underline{x}|.

C.11.18.
Remark

In fact, a more elaborate analysis gives

\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}})_{\underline{x}}\simeq({\mathbf{A}}^{n\sharp})_{\underline{x}}\textrm{ for }n\geq|\underline{x}|-1\geq 0.

For example, a direct calculation shows the restriction
\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}})
along
X^{2}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
fits into the following Cartesian square

\textstyle{\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{A}})|_{X^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}|_{X^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{A}}|_{X}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{A}}|_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{A}}|_{X^{2}}),}

where

•

j:X^{2}\setminus X\to X^{2}
is the complement of the diagonal embedding;

•

the bottom horizontal functor is provided by the lax-factorization structure on
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
.

Similarly, one can show

\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{M}})_{\underline{x}}\simeq({\mathbf{M}}^{n\sharp})_{\underline{x}}\textrm{ for }n\geq|\underline{x}|-|\underline{x}_{0}|.

For example, the restriction of
\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{M}})
along the map

X\times S_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{{\operatorname{untl}}}_{\underline{x}_{0}},\;y\mapsto y\cup\underline{x}_{0}

fits into the following Cartesian square

\textstyle{\mathbf{Str}({\mathbf{M}})|_{X\times S_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{M}}|_{X\times S_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{A}}|_{X}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{M}}|_{\underline{x}_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}({\mathbf{M}}|_{X\times S_{0}}),}

where

•

j:(X\times S_{0})\setminus\operatorname{graph}_{\underline{x}_{0}}\to X\times S_{0}
is the complement of the union of the graphs for elements in
\underline{x}_{0}\subseteq X(S_{0})
;

•

the bottom horizontal functor is provided by the lax-factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure on
\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}
.

C.11.19.

Finally, we explain claim (2) in Sect.
C.11.7
. Claim (3) can be proved similarly by replacing non-marked sets with marked ones.

Let
{\mathbf{B}}
be any test unital factorization category. We want to show

\operatorname{Fun}({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp})\to\operatorname{Fun}({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{A}})

is an equivalence. We only explain how to construct the desired inverse functor

(C.49)

\operatorname{Fun}({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{A}})\to\operatorname{Fun}({\mathbf{B}},{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}),

and leave the verification to
[
CFZ
]
.

Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{B}}\to{\mathbf{A}}
be a unital factorization functor. The
\sharp
-construction is functorial, hence we have a functor

\Phi^{\sharp}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\sharp}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\sharp}.

Since
{\mathbf{B}}
is
\infty
-strict by assumption, Lemma implies
\mu_{\mathbf{B}}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\sharp}\to{\mathbf{B}}
is an equivalence. We now define the functor (
C.49
) to be
\Phi\mapsto\mu_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\circ\Phi^{\sharp}
.

∎[Sketch of Theorem
C.10.4
]

C.12.
Induced modules

In this subsection, we fix a unital factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
and a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category
{\mathbf{M}}
at
\underline{x}_{0}
. For simplicity, we assume
\underline{x}_{0}=x_{0}
is a single
k
-point on
X
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}})
be a factorization algebra in
{\mathbf{A}}
and

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}:{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}

be the forgetful functor. We will study the
partially defined
left adjoint
{\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}
of this functor.

C.12.1.
Warning

For general
{\mathcal{A}}
, the functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}
does not preserve limits, because general external tensor products and
!
-pullback functors do not preserve limits. In fact, we do not know how to calculate limits in
{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}
(although we know they exist by presentability). As a consequence,
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}
does not admit a left adjoint.

C.12.2.

We are going to provide a sufficient condition on
{\mathcal{A}}
and
V
such that there exists an
object

{\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}(V)
such that

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}}({\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}(V),{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}}(V,{\mathcal{M}}_{x_{0}}).

Such an object
{\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}(V)
is called the
induced
(a.k.a.
free
) factorization
{\mathcal{A}}
-module in
{\mathbf{M}}
.

C.12.3.
Remark

More generally, one can ask what are the coCartesian arrows in the Cartesian fibration (see Sect.
C.10.17
)

\operatorname{FactMod}({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}\to\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}}).

We will treat this problem in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.12.4.

To construct
{\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}(V)
, we need some notations.

Let
X_{\circ}:=X\setminus x_{0}
be the punctured curve and
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}
be the unital Ran space for
X_{\circ}
. Consider the map

\mathfrak{j}:{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},(\underline{y},x_{0})\mapsto\underline{y}\sqcup\{x_{0}\}.

Note that
\mathfrak{j}
induces a bijection between
k
-points, but is not an isomorphism. In fact, one can check

•

{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}
is a Cartesian space over
{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
(see Sect.
C.1.5
);

•

For any affine points
S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
, the base-change of
\mathfrak{j}
is a finite disjoint union of locally closed immersions.

Similarly, for any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
, we have a map

(C.50)

\mathfrak{j}_{I}:\big(\prod_{i\in I}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\times x_{0}\to\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}\simeq\big(\big(\prod_{i\in I^{\circ}}{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)\times{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}\big)_{\operatorname{disj}}

given by
((\text{id})_{i\in I^{\circ}},\mathfrak{j})
. To simplify the notations, we write it as

\mathfrak{j}_{I}:{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0}\to{\mathcal{R}}_{I}.

Note that we have a functor

(C.51)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{j}_{I}^{!}:\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I}})\to\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0}})\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}})\simeq\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}})\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}},

where the first equivalence is given by the factorization
{\mathbf{A}}
-module structure on
{\mathbf{M}}
.

C.12.5.

Note that
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}}
is a unital factorization category on the punctured curve
X_{\circ}
. We denote this object by

{\mathbf{A}}_{\circ}\in\mathbf{UntlFactCat}(X_{\circ}),

to distinguish it from the object
{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbf{UntlFactCat}(X)
. Note that the latter category is denoted just by
\mathbf{UntlFactCat}
in the rest of this appendix.

By restriction, we also obtain an object

{\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\in\operatorname{FactAlg}({\mathbf{A}}_{\circ}).

C.12.6.

Similarly,
\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}}
is a unital factorization
{\mathbf{A}}_{\circ}
-module category
at the point
\emptyset\in{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}
. Here we use the identification

({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\emptyset}\simeq{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0},\;\underline{y}\mapsto(\underline{y},x_{0}).

We denote this object by

{\mathbf{M}}_{\circ}\in{\mathbf{A}}_{\circ}\mathbf{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}_{\emptyset}.

Via the correspondence in Sect.
C.6.6
,
{\mathbf{M}}_{\circ}
is given by the DG category
{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
. It follows that

(C.52)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}}_{\circ})_{\emptyset}:=\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{{\operatorname{fact}}_{\emptyset}},{\mathbf{M}}_{\circ})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Vect},{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}})\simeq{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}.

By construction, this is just the forgetful functor
{\operatorname{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}}

C.12.7.

The above equivalence can be proved in a more explicit way.

Given
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}}_{\circ})_{\emptyset}
, its fiber
{\mathcal{M}}_{\emptyset}
is an object in
({\mathbf{M}}_{\circ})_{\emptyset}\simeq{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
; conversely, given an object
V\in{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
, the tensor product

\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V\in\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{\circ})\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}\simeq\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}(({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}})_{\emptyset},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}_{\circ})

has a canonical factorization
{\mathbf{A}}_{\circ}
-module structure. One can check these two constructions are inverse to each other.

C.12.8.

Given an object
V\in{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
, we say it is
adapted to
{\mathcal{A}}
-induction
if it satisfies the following conditions:

•

For any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
, the partially defined left
\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}
to the functor (
C.51
) is well-defined at the object
\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V
, i.e., the following object exists:

\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V\big)\in\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I}}).

In particular, we have an object

\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\in\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}).

•

For any marked finite set
I=I^{\circ}\sqcup\{0\}
, the canonical (Beck–Chevalley) morphism

\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V\big)\to\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\big)|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}

is invertible.

C.12.9.

Let
V\in{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
be an object adapted to
{\mathcal{A}}
-induction. We claim
\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)
can be canonically upgraded to an object

\mathfrak{j}_{!}({\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\in{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}.

We will only construct the structural isomorphisms (see Sect.
C.7.8
)

(C.53)

\operatorname{act}_{I}\big(\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{union}_{I}^{!}(\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V))|_{\operatorname{disj}},

and leave the higher compatibilities to
[
CFZ
]
.

By assumption, we have

(C.54)

\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\big)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\simeq\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I^{\circ}}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)\big)|_{{\operatorname{disj}}}\simeq\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V\big).

On the other hand, we have a Cartesian square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{j}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I,\circ}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{R}}_{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{j}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}}}

such that the vertical arrows are etale (see
37
). This implies the Beck–Chevalley natural transformation

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I,\circ}\times x_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I,\circ,*}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\mathcal{R}}_{I},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\mathcal{R}}_{I}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{union}_{I,*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{j}_{I}^{!}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}})}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\operatorname{lax}}({\operatorname{Ran}}_{x_{0}}^{\operatorname{untl}},\underline{{\mathbf{M}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{j}^{!}}

is invertible, where the vertical functors are right adjoint to the
!
-pullback functors. Passing to partially defined left adjoints, we obtain

(C.55)

\operatorname{union}_{I}^{!}(\mathfrak{j}_{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V))|_{\operatorname{disj}}\simeq\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}\big(\operatorname{union}_{I,\circ}^{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)|_{\operatorname{disj}}\big)

Via the isomorphisms (
C.54
) and (
C.55
), the desired isomorphism (
C.53
) is given by applying
\mathfrak{j}_{I,!}
to

\operatorname{act}_{I,\circ}\big((\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits_{i\in I}}\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ})_{\operatorname{disj}}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V\big)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{union}_{I,\circ}^{!}(\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V)|_{\operatorname{disj}},

which is given by the factorizaiton
\underline{A}_{\circ}
-module structure on
\underline{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V
(see Sect.
C.12.7
).

C.12.10.

Modulo issues about homotopy coherence, it is clear

\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}}(\mathfrak{j}_{!}({\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V),{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}}_{\circ})_{\emptyset}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V,{\mathcal{M}}|_{{\operatorname{Ran}}_{\circ}^{\operatorname{untl}}\times x_{0}}).

By (
C.52
), the RHS can be identified with

\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}}(V,{\mathcal{M}}_{x_{0}}).

Hence we obtain the following result.

Proposition C.12.11
.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
,
{\mathbf{M}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}
be as in Sect.
C.12
. Suppose
V\in{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}
is adapted to
{\mathcal{A}}
-induction (see Sect.
C.12.8
), then the partially defined left adjoint
\operatorname{ind}_{\mathcal{A}}
to the forgetful functor

{\operatorname{oblv}}_{\mathcal{A}}:{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-mod}^{\operatorname{fact}}({\mathbf{M}})_{x_{0}}\to{\mathbf{M}}_{x_{0}}

is defined on
V
, and we have

{\operatorname{ind}}_{\mathcal{A}}(V)\simeq\mathfrak{j}_{!}({\mathcal{A}}_{\circ}\mathbin{\mathop{\boxtimes}\displaylimits}V),

where the RHS is defined in Sect.
C.12.9
.

C.13.
The Ran operad

This subsection serves as an advertisement for
[
CFZ
]
, where all the homotopy-coherent difficulties in this appendix (as well as those ignored in
[
GLC2
, Appendix B, C]
) will be treated by the methods developed in Lurie’s
Higher Algebra

[
Lu
]
.

C.13.1.

In
[
CFZ
]
, we will rewrite the foundations of factorization structures
42
42

42

It is fair to say
[
Ra
]
is the only homotopy-coherent foundation of factorization categories that exists in the literature. However, there are several disadvantages in Raskin’s approach which makes it hard to prove results claimed in Sect.
C.9
.
using the language of
generalized operads
developed in
[
Lu
]
.

C.13.2.

In
[
Lu
, Chapter 2]
, Lurie defined an
\infty
-operad as an
\infty
-cateogry
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{*}
over the category of marked finite sets that satisfies certain conditions. Roughly speaking, an
\infty
-operad is a colored operad (introduced by May in
[
Ma
]
) enriched over the
\infty
-category of spaces, except that the collection of colors is allowed to form a category
43
43

43

It is the category
{\mathcal{O}}:={\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\times_{\operatorname{Fin}_{*}}\{0,1\}
.
rather than a space/set. Equivalently, an
\infty
-operad is a pseudo-tensor category (introduced by Beilinson–Drinfeld in
[
BD1
]
), but the underlying category is allowed to be an
\infty
-category.

C.13.3.

A generalized
\infty
-operad should be viewed as a
family of
\infty
-operads
parameterized by some base category
{\mathcal{C}}
(see
[
Lu
, Sect. 2.3]
). Just like the usual theory of various types of algebras and monoidal categories can be developed using the corresponding operads, for any generalized
\infty
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{*}\times{\mathcal{C}}
, one can develop the notion of
{\mathcal{O}}
-monoidal categories and
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras in them.

The main idea behind
[
CFZ
]
is: there should exist a (classical) generalized operad
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
parameterized by the category
\operatorname{Aff}
of affine schemes, such that

\textrm{lax-factorization objects in }\mathcal{D}={\operatorname{Ran}}\textrm{-algebras in }\mathcal{D},

where
\mathcal{D}
is any symmetric monoidal
(\infty,2)
-category. For instance,

\textrm{lax-factorization DG categories}={\operatorname{Ran}}\textrm{-algebras in }\mathbf{DGCat}.

C.13.4.

The construction of the generalized operad
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
is easy. For any affine scheme
S
, we have a symmetric monoidal category
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
with tensor products given by unions of finite sets. In particular, it corresponds to a (classical)
\infty
-operad
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)^{\cup}
. We now define

{\operatorname{Ran}}(S)^{\otimes}\subseteq{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)^{\cup}

to be the 1-full subcategory containing of those morphisms that corrspond to
disjoint
unions. Alternatively, we equip
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}(S)
with a structure of pseudo-tensor categories, where a multi-map
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}\to y
exists iff the points
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}
are disjoint and
\sqcup x_{i}\subseteq y
.

The above construction is contravariantly functorial in
S
. Hence we have a functor from
\operatorname{Aff}^{\operatorname{op}}
to the category of (classical)
\infty
-operads. Now the generalized operad
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
is defined to be the corresponding coCartesian fibration

{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Aff}^{\operatorname{op}}.

C.13.5.

The main advantage of
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
is that it provides a natural way to deal with factorization
module
structures.

In
[
Lu
, Chapter 3]
, for any
coherent

\infty
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}
and a color
\mathfrak{m}\in{\mathcal{O}}
, Lurie defined the notion of
\mathfrak{m}
-type
{\mathcal{O}}
-modules for
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras, and provided a framework to deal with restrictions and relative tensor products of such modules, where the higher compatibilities for these constructions are encoded as certain fibrations of
\infty
-categories.

In
[
CFZ
]
, we will generalize the notion of coherence even to generalized
(\infty,2)
-operads, and prove:

Theorem C.13.6
.

{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
is coherent as a generalized
(\infty,2)
-operads.

C.13.7.

As a result, we can deal with restrictions and relative tensor products of
\underline{x}
-type
{\operatorname{Ran}}
-modules internal to any symmetric monoidal
(\infty,2)
-category, such as
\mathbf{DGCat}
, in a way similar to
[
Lu
, Chapter 3]
.

Note that a color
\underline{x}
in
{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\otimes}
is exactly an affine point
\underline{x}:S\to{\operatorname{Ran}}^{\operatorname{untl}}
for some affine scheme
S
. We will show, for example, the following two notions are the same:

•

A pair
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})
of a (unital) lax-factorization category
{\mathbf{A}}
and its module
{\mathbf{M}}
at
\underline{x}
, as defined in this appendix;

•

A pair
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{M}})
of a
{\operatorname{Ran}}
-algebra in
\mathbf{DGCat}
and its
\underline{x}
-type module
{\mathbf{M}}
, as defined in
[
CFZ
]
.

C.13.8.

Note that in above,
{\operatorname{Ran}}
-algebras/modules correspond to
lax-
factorization algebras/modules. In
[
CFZ
]
, we will explain there is an analog between

•

A (unital)
strict
factorization algebra object in a symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{D}}
;

•

A (properly defined verison of)
monoidal
functor
{\operatorname{Ran}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
.

As a consequence, the construction of the strictening functor (
C.11.6
) will be interpreted via
monoidal envelopes
and
operadic Kan extensions
(see
[
Lu
, Sect. 2.2.4 and Sect. 3.1.2]
).
